# 7. Spec: configuration

> Normative. Copied from the implementation plan (2026-10-13) in M0; this file is now the source of truth.
> MUST, SHOULD and MAY follow RFC 2119. Rules tagged `[Mn]` belong to milestone n.

### 7.1 `hushrun.yaml`

The file lives at the repository root. `--config` or `HUSHRUN_CONFIG` override the path (an empty `HUSHRUN_CONFIG` is unset), and `--config-ref <git ref>` reads it from another revision (for pull requests, T-5), whatever `HUSHRUN_CONFIG` says. This is an example:

```yaml
version: 1
repository: github.com/acme/shop
repository_url: https://hushrun.example/l/acme/shop   # where its events go (C-3, T-4)
transparency_log:                                           # the server's log key, which readers pin (T-9)
  origin: hushrun.example/l/acme/shop
  key: "ed25519:…"

# No `scopes:` block, so there is one scope, "default", with include ["**"].

checks:
  toolchain:
    run: .github/scripts/check-toolchain.sh
    env: [GO_VERSION]
  unit:
    run: go test ./...
    timeout: 20m

artifacts:
  image:
    type: oci
    location: us-central1-docker.pkg.dev/acme-prod/api/go

deploy:
  branch: main
  require: [toolchain, unit]   # CI only? set in the dashboard, not here (T-16)
```

**C-1** `version: 1` is required. Unknown keys at any level are errors, so typos can't pass silently.

[ADR 0062] Three keys of older files are *retired*: `stages`, `checks.<name>.kind` and `artifacts.<name>.locations`. They are parsed and never used. Each command names them once on stderr, unless `--quiet`, as `warning: <file>:<line>:<column>: `<key>` is ignored since hushrun 0.9.0, which has no stages: <what to do instead>`; `policy validate` and `doctor` name them too. `run`, `ready` and the pre-push hook go on as if they weren't there, and keep `backend` and `repository_url`. `plan`, and `artifact resolve` when it plans, refuse such a file (exit 2): a deploy that ignored `stages` would skip the gates its author wrote. `hushrun init` removes each check's `kind`, a line of its own or a flow map's entry, and names `stages` and `locations` without touching them: only a person can say which of a stage's gates `deploy` keeps. An artifact with only `locations` is left out. What a stage held is never read: `after`, `upstream`, `upstream_grace` and `ci` are unknown keys under `deploy`.

**C-2** `repository` is required. It is a canonical `host/path` id: lowercase host, no scheme, no `.git`. It is required because guessing from the CI or the `origin` remote can differ in case or form (SSH vs HTTPS), and a mismatch would silently invalidate evidence (E-8 step 4).

**C-3** `repository_url` is an `https://` URL (§6, L-1), where the repository's events go. In CI, the URL comes from the environment, else from this file (T-4). [ADR 0026 amendment 2] There is no `ledger_root`: a repository's server has no root to pin, and the key is unknown, like any other (C-8). [ADR 0031] There is no `trust:` section either, which is an unknown key: whose events count is the server's answer (T-15). A `hushrun.yaml` without `deploy` is valid: it names the repository, its URL and the log key (T-9). [ADR 0062] Its pipeline then deploys from any branch and requires every check with a `run` (C-7).

[ADR 0037] `backend` is the URL of an org on a server that speaks `hushrun-results/v0`, under the same rules as `repository_url`: where the repository's `hushrun run`s share results (C-11).

**C-4** `scopes.<name>` is `{include: [glob], exclude: [glob]}`, and the name follows the L-2 pattern. `digest.exclude` applies to every scope.

[ADR 0037] `scopes.<name>.external` lists what the scope's checks build against outside the repository, each `{path, include: [glob], exclude: [glob]}`: `path` is the top of a git checkout, relative to the repository's root and `/`-separated, such as `../cli`, and `include` is `["**"]` by default. Each path is listed once. It is part of a run's key (C-9), and of no event: `digest` and evidence are about the repository's own files.

```yaml
scopes:
  back:
    include: ["back/**", ".github/workflows/**"]
    external:
      - path: ../cli
        include: ["crates/**", "Cargo.lock", "Cargo.toml"]
```

**C-5** `checks.<name>`:

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `kind` | [ADR 0062] Retired (C-1): every check is a source check, valid for any deploy of the same files. Its definition object (E-6) keeps `"kind": "source"`, so evidence recorded before 0.9 keeps its digest. |
| `run` | Command that `hushrun run` executes. Optional: checks without it are attested by other tools or run with `hushrun run <check> -- <command>`. |
| `shell` | `sh`, `bash`, `pwsh` or `cmd`. If absent, `hushrun run` uses `sh` on Unix and `pwsh` on Windows. The default is not part of the definition (E-6); set `shell` to pin it. |
| `image`, `platform` | Environment pins, for evidence (`hushrun run CHECK…`, `attest`). `image` MUST be pinned by digest. Evidence counts only if its environment has the same image digest and platform (E-7). Non-isolated runs of a pinned check need `--environment-image` (§10.4). [M8] An isolated run needs `image` (E-12). `hushrun run NAME -- COMMAND` doesn't read them. |
| `env` | Names of variables the check depends on; part of the definition, values never recorded. A non-isolated `run` inherits the whole environment. An isolated run [M8] passes only these variables into the container, and never a secret: a check that names one doesn't run isolated (E-12). |
| `network` | [M8] Network for isolated runs (E-12): `none` (default), `restricted` (the network the operator names in `HUSHRUN_RESTRICTED_NETWORK`, whose egress they restrict) or `open` (the container runtime's default). Part of the definition. |
| `timeout`, `retries` | Used by `hushrun run`. `retries` defaults to 0. |
| `max_age` | Duration such as `24h` or `7d`. Older evidence is expired. |
| `version` | Free string. Bump it to invalidate existing evidence. |
| `counts_from` | [ADR 0045] What this check needs of the machine a run happens on (C-25). Not part of the definition (E-6). |
| `reports` | [ADR 0047] What a later step of a CI job may read of this check's (C-26): `files`, at most 32 globs of the report files it writes, relative to the directory it runs in, besides those hushrun finds; and `ci`, `run` (the default) or `skip`, which says the repository's readers handle a skip, so CI skips it on a pass. An unknown key or value is an error. Not part of the definition (E-6). |
| `tests` | [ADR 0068] Whether `hushrun run NAME -- COMMAND` looks at the check's test files (C-30 to C-33): `off`, the default, as if absent; `observe`, every test file runs, and hushrun records what each read and says what reuse would have done; or `reuse`, [ADR 0069] where test files that already passed on the files they read aren't run again, with audit runs (C-34). Or `{mode, unit, reads, ignore, always}`: `mode` one of those, required; [ADR 0077] `unit`, `file` (the default) or `test`, what the check reuses one by one (C-39); `reads`, `file` (the default) or `test`, what a test is taken to have read, `test` only with `unit: test`, else the error says `check `<NAME>`'s `tests.reads: test` needs `tests.unit: test`: write `unit: test` beside it, or `reads: file``; `ignore`, globs from the repository root of files in the ignored set (C-30); `always`, globs of test files that always run. The short form is `{mode}` alone, test files. An unknown key or value, or a glob that doesn't compile (C-4), is an error. Not part of the definition (E-6). |

**C-6** `artifacts.<name>` is `{type: oci | file, scope?, location}`. `scope` defaults to `default`. An OCI location is a repository with no tag or digest. [ADR 0062] One `location`: where the pipeline pushes the artifact and deploys it from, and where P-13 looks for one to reuse. A deploy of the scope deploys every artifact of the scope. `locations`, one per stage, is retired (C-1).

**C-7** [ADR 0062] `deploy`: how the repository's one pipeline deploys. Optional; without it, the pipeline takes the defaults. It was `stages.<name>`, one block per stage.

| Key | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `branch` | The only branch the pipeline deploys from (P-8). Optional. Also the default branch for `guard`. |
| `require` | The checks a deploy needs, by name. Default: every check with a `run`. |
| `lease` | Default `3h`; also caps every `leaseUntil` (P-5). |
| `dedupe` | Triggers that may skip an already deployed digest (P-10). Default `[push, upstream]`, where `upstream` is a run another workflow or pipeline started. |
| `allow_force` | Default `true`. |
| `on_repository_error` | `fail` (default) or `full`. |

`Policy::required()` is the one definition of the checks a commit needs: `deploy.require` when the file lists it, else every check with a `run`, in name order. `run --all`, `ready`, `plan`, `status` and the server's index all call it.

Gone with stages, with no replacement: `after`, `upstream`, `upstream_grace`, `ci.github.workflow`, [M9] `ci.gitlab.ref`, `<check>@<stage>` requirements, and checks of kind `deployment` or `approval`. A check that ran against an earlier stage's deployment now runs in the deploy step, or is attested before `mark passed`.

[ADR 0031 amendment 1] There is no `accept`: it is an unknown key. Whether the repository takes evidence from its CI only is set on the server (T-16), where a pull request can't remove it.

**C-8 Validation.** Every error reports `file:line:column`. These are errors:

- unknown keys, or references to unknown checks or scopes;
- [ADR 0062] a `deploy.require` entry `<check>@<stage>`, which says to require the check and run what checked that stage's deployment in the deploy step; a check required twice; a `deploy.branch` that is empty or a `refs/` path;
- an artifact without `location`, unless it has the retired `locations` (C-1);
- invalid globs (§4, pattern grammar), durations or names, or names that collide after the env mapping (L-2);
- a check `image` that isn't pinned by digest;
- [ADR 0046] a `HUSHRUN_AGENT` string (C-17) anywhere in the file, comments included, which the error doesn't show;
- [ADR 0037] an `external` path that isn't relative to the repository's root, `/`-separated, and outside it; one listed twice in a scope; an empty `include`; or `external` in a file with `deploy` [ADR 0062]: a deploy's evidence covers the repository's own files;
- [ADR 0045] a `counts_from` (C-25) at any level in a file with `deploy` [ADR 0062], `anywhere` included; one that is neither `anywhere` nor a rule; an empty rule, list or `env`; a key outside its closed list; an `os` or `arch` outside theirs, `darwin` saying to write `macos`; a version or variable value that isn't a string, saying to quote it, since YAML makes `1.20` into `1.2`; a version that doesn't start with numbers, such as `v1.25` or `1.25.x`; a value listed twice; or a variable's name that isn't one.

YAML is parsed with `serde-saphyr` with its budget limits on, and duplicate keys are errors. Files larger than 1 MiB are rejected.

### 7.2 Signers [ADR 0031]

Membership is trust. hushrun.yaml says nothing about who may sign: the events that count are those signed by the people and agents of the repository's org, from machines they registered (C-18), and by the repository's own CI. The repository's server knows both, and answers them as the repository's signers (L-13). ADR 0031 removed T-1 (principals), T-2 (acceptance), T-3 (minimum custody), T-6 (locations), T-7 (constraints), T-10 (identities), T-11 (windows and retired keys) and T-14 (trust from the default branch), with the organization policy (§7.5).

**T-15 Who counts.** An event counts when its signature verifies (E-8) and its signer is one of the repository's signers, as the server answers them (L-13):

- **A machine** of a current member of the repository's org (a person, `user:<email>`), or of one of the org's agent tokens whose scope is the org or this repository (`agent:<name>`), registered at sign-in, or with the agent token when it was made (C-18), or by the dashboard when a person approves there. Its signer is the owner, `user:<email>` or `agent:<name>`, and the highest custody it may claim is `isolated`: a laptop's or a sandbox's run is never CI's.
- **The repository's CI**: a keyless signature (E-11) whose certificate names an issuer the server lists for the repository and its Source Repository Identifier, the repository's numeric id the server pinned (GitHub Actions' `https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com` with the id GitHub gives the repository, C-12). A certificate whose Build Trigger is `pull_request_target`, which runs the base branch's workflow for a pull request that hasn't merged, isn't the CI's. Its signer is `ci`, and it may claim custody up to `ci`. [M9] A GitLab pipeline's certificate names its issuer and the project's id the same way.
- **Revocation.** Logging out, removing a member, revoking an agent token or a machine stops what it signs from counting from then on; the server keeps the machine in its answer with the time it was revoked. A timestamped signature (E-10) by a revoked machine counts when its trusted time is before the revocation; one without a timestamp counts only while the machine isn't revoked. A signer's own `time` never decides. So events signed while a machine was trusted keep counting, and nothing signed later does.
- **In CI**, when the provider reports the repository's numeric id (`GITHUB_REPOSITORY_ID`, `CI_PROJECT_ID`), a server whose answer names CI of another repository id exits 2: an answer for another repository fails loudly.
- **Where it is decided.** A reader asks the server (`GET {repository_url}/signers`, L-13) once per command, and checks the answer's statement against the log key hushrun.yaml pins (T-9): a server can't change who counts without signing it with that key, and the statement goes with the event in `--explain-json`. Without a pinned key, the answer is used unchecked, with a warning. `hushrun repository signers --out FILE` saves an answer, for `hushrun verify --signers FILE` offline. This places trust in the server, as ADR 0023 item 2 places a repository's results: whoever runs the org's server decides who its members are.

**T-16 CI only.** [ADR 0031, amendments 1 and 2; ADR 0062] While a repository is *CI-only*, its checks (P-1), its artifacts (P-13) and its lifecycle events (`started`, `deployed`, `passed`, `failed`, `superseded`) count only from events whose signer is `ci`.

- **Where it is set.** On the server, by the org's admins and owners, never in `hushrun.yaml`, so that a pull request can't remove the rule it is reviewed under. [ADR 0062] It is one switch per repository, "CI reuses only CI's results" (results-v0 §3), which covers its `hushrun run` results and its events alike. Each change is audited. There is no list of stages, for the org or for a repository: the server merged each repository's and its org's lists into the repository's periods, their union, so nothing became less strict.
- **How readers learn it.** The signers answer lists every period the repository was CI-only, oldest first (L-13). The answer's statement signs them with the rest, and `hushrun repository signers --out` saves them for `verify --signers`. An answer that still lists periods per stage is read as the union of all of them. `plan`, `run`, `mark`, `artifact resolve`, `status`, `ready` and `repository gc` apply them; `verify` says, per accepted event, whether they keep it from counting.
- **`policy explain`** asks the same answer, checked the same way (the pinned log key, or a warning without one), or reads a saved one with `--signers FILE`, and shows whether the repository is CI-only now and since when, its earlier periods, and whose events count for each required check accordingly: the CI only, or members' machines, agents and the CI. A `--signers` file that doesn't parse or verify exits 2, as in `verify`. Without a URL (T-4), or when the server doesn't answer within 15 s or its answer doesn't verify, it shows the rest from `hushrun.yaml` with one line saying whether the repository is CI-only wasn't checked, and exits 0; its JSON (`hushrun.policy.explain/v2`) says in `ciOnly` whether it was checked and what the answer said.
- **Events made before the change.** A rule change applies as a revocation does (T-15), period by period: an event someone other than the CI signed doesn't count when its trusted time (E-10) falls in a period the repository was CI-only (from its start, included, to its end, excluded); without a trusted timestamp, it could have been made now, so it doesn't count while the repository is CI-only now. A signer's own `time` never decides. So evidence made while the repository took anyone's keeps counting, before the first period or between two, and nothing made during one ever does: [ADR 0031 amendment 2] lifting the rule and setting it again doesn't count events timestamped during an earlier period. Lifting it counts untimestamped events again, and everything timestamped outside the periods.
- **The server's refusal.** While the repository is CI-only, a hosted server also refuses, with 403, a write signed by a member's or an agent's machine of a lifecycle event, an artifact or a deployed-index entry. That is a convenience that fails early: readers decide by the answer, and an event that reached a ledger anyway still doesn't count.

### 7.3 Where configuration comes from

**T-4 Signers and the repository's URL.** Who signs is never read from the content being evaluated (T-15), and in CI the repository's URL comes from outside it when the CI's settings name one, so a change can't add its own signer.

- **In CI** (any provider detected, §11.2), commands that read or write the ledger take it from `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL`, or `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_BASE` below; [ADR 0031] with neither, from the `repository_url` of the evaluated `hushrun.yaml` (T-5). That URL gets the job's ID token (L-10), whose audience names it, or, when it is on hushrun's host, an agent token in `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`, which the server there takes for its org only; never `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_TOKEN` or `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_TOKEN_COMMAND`, which go only to a ledger `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL` or `--repository-url` names, nor any other token. Set it from CI settings that live outside the repository, such as GitHub repository variables or GitLab project variables. A changed URL alone gains nothing: the server it names must answer the repository's signers with the log key hushrun.yaml pins (T-9, T-15), and the ID token it gets names it. [M9] GitLab's pipeline variables, which whoever runs a pipeline may set, outrank its project variables, so a project restricts who may set them (no one, or Maintainers with a protected deploy branch); `doctor` reports the setting when it can read it.
  - If `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL` is missing, and so are the base and `hushrun.yaml`'s `repository_url`, those commands exit 2.
  - If it differs from `hushrun.yaml`'s `repository_url`, after normalizing both (lowercase scheme and host, no trailing `/`), they exit 2.
  - Commands that never touch the ledger (`digest`, `keys`, `policy validate`) are unaffected. [ADR 0031] `policy explain` asks the ledger only for its signers answer, and without a URL shows the rest without it (T-16).
  - [M13] **`HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_BASE`**, one variable for an organization, gives each repository the ledger `<base>/<repository id>`, such as `https://hushrun.example/l/acme/github.com/acme/back`, when `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL` isn't set. The repository id comes from the CI provider (`<host>/<GITHUB_REPOSITORY>` on GitHub), not from the tree, and the commands exit 2 when the provider doesn't report one, when `hushrun.yaml`'s `repository` isn't exactly that id (C-2: a difference only in case counts, since ledger paths keep case), or when `hushrun.yaml` doesn't name that URL as its `repository_url`. So a changed base can't redirect a repository silently. `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL`, when set, wins, with a warning when the base gives another URL. A ledger under the base counts as named outside the repository for its tokens (L-10).
- **Locally,** `hushrun.yaml`'s `repository_url` is used. [M13] `--repository-url`, `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL`, and `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_BASE` with `hushrun.yaml`'s `repository`, in that order, come first. `hushrun.yaml` is the evaluated revision's, as committed (T-5): without any of these, the command exits 2, and when the working tree's `hushrun.yaml` names a `repository_url` that the committed one doesn't (right after `hushrun init`, before its commit), the message says to commit it or pass `--config hushrun.yaml`.
- [ADR 0029, ADR 0030] **Names.** The settings that name the repository's URL on hushrun say `repository`: `repository_url:`, `--repository-url`, `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL`, `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_BASE` and `--repository-base`, `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_TOKEN` and `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_TOKEN_COMMAND`. Their names before ADR 0029 (`ledger:`, `--ledger`, `HUSHRUN_LEDGER*`, `--ledger-base`) and `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY` were never released and have no aliases: the keys and flags are unknown, and the variables are ignored. A token command gets the URL as `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_URL`. Likewise `transparency_log:` and `on_repository_error` (ADR 0030; a stage's then, [ADR 0062] `deploy`'s now) were `ledger_log` and `on_ledger_error`, and those are unknown keys. The `hushrun repository` subcommand (`ls`, `show`, `signers`, `gc`, `monitor`; JSON `hushrun.repository.*`) was `hushrun ledger`, which is now an unknown subcommand, and `doctor`'s checks `server`, `server protocol`, `repository url` and `transparency log` were `ledger`, `ledger protocol`, `ledger url` and `ledger log`.
- [ADR 0031] `--trust`, `--trust-from-tree`, `HUSHRUN_TRUST` and `HUSHRUN_TRUST_REF` are gone: unknown arguments, and ignored variables.

**T-5 Policy.** `hushrun.yaml` is read from the revision under evaluation, because it is reviewed with the code it governs. [M8] Pull-request pipelines MUST instead use `--config-ref <base branch>`: a pull request's own `hushrun.yaml` hasn't been reviewed yet, and could weaken the checks it is evaluated against.

- `--config-ref <ref>` reads `hushrun.yaml` from that git ref, such as `origin/main`, as the VCS stores it there (D-9), for the scopes, the checks and `deploy` alike; `policyDigest` (E-4) is its digest. It excludes `--config`, and a ref that doesn't have the file is a configuration error (exit 2).
- In a pull request's pipeline (GitHub's `pull_request` and `pull_request_target` events; GitLab's `CI_PIPELINE_SOURCE=merge_request_event`), commands that read or write the ledger exit 2 without `--config-ref`, and with a ref that isn't the branch the pull request merges into (`GITHUB_BASE_REF`, `CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TARGET_BRANCH_NAME`), written as that branch or `<remote>/<branch>`. Commands that touch no ledger, such as `policy validate`, read the pull request's own file.

**T-12 Signers and the Sigstore trust root [M10].** What signs, what timestamps, and what verifies certificates and timestamps come from the environment, never from the content under evaluation (T-4).

- **The signer.** `HUSHRUN_SIGNING` is `key`, `sigstore` or [ADR 0028] `machine`; without it, a set `HUSHRUN_SIGNING_KEY` or `HUSHRUN_SIGNING_KEY_FILE` means `key`; [ADR 0026] else, in CI, a GitHub Actions job with an ID token (its endpoint) means `sigstore`, as the repository's keyless CI (T-15); [ADR 0028] else a machine key (C-18: [ADR 0046] the one in `HUSHRUN_AGENT`, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY`, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE`, or the one beside the credentials file) means `machine`: a person's machine, or in CI an agent's, such as a GitLab pipeline's, which signs as its agent. In CI only a key the environment names (`HUSHRUN_AGENT`, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY` or `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE`) counts here, never the one a person's sign-in left beside the credentials file on a runner, whose events would be that person's; else, in CI, `SIGSTORE_ID_TOKEN` means `sigstore`; else a command that signs exits 2 and says how to get a signer. A GitLab pipeline with a machine key signs keylessly only with `HUSHRUN_SIGNING=sigstore`, for a server that verifies GitLab's ID tokens. [ADR 0022] KMS signers (`kms`, `HUSHRUN_SIGNING_KMS`) are gone.
  - `key`: a key file (E-2), its PEM in `HUSHRUN_SIGNING_KEY` or its path in `HUSHRUN_SIGNING_KEY_FILE`.
  - `machine`: the key this machine's sign-in registered, or an agent's, registered with its token (C-18). Its events count while it is one of the repository's signers (T-15).
  - `sigstore`: keyless signing (E-11) with Fulcio at `HUSHRUN_FULCIO_URL` (default `https://fulcio.sigstore.dev`), and GitHub Actions' ID token (the job needs `permissions: id-token: write`) or `SIGSTORE_ID_TOKEN`. [M9] On GitLab CI, `SIGSTORE_ID_TOKEN` is the job's `id_tokens` entry `SIGSTORE_ID_TOKEN: {aud: sigstore}`; a token whose `aud` doesn't include `sigstore` is a configuration error (exit 2), and an expired one makes the signer unavailable (exit 9), before Fulcio sees either.
- **Timestamp authorities.** `HUSHRUN_TSA` lists URLs, separated by commas, tried in order (E-10); by default Sigstore's, `https://timestamp.sigstore.dev/api/v1/timestamp`. `none` turns timestamps off, which keyless signing refuses.
- **The Sigstore trust root** (`trusted_root.json`): the file `HUSHRUN_SIGSTORE_TRUSTED_ROOT` names; else the copy `hushrun sigstore refresh` cached under `$HUSHRUN_CACHE_DIR/sigstore/<host>/` ([ADR 0061] written whole: a refresh puts its files in a directory of their own there, and `current` names it once they are all in, so a refresh cut short leaves the copy before it), when it verifies offline with TUF from the initial root and, for public-good Sigstore, is no older than the snapshot in the binary; else that snapshot, public-good Sigstore's. `HUSHRUN_SIGSTORE_TUF_URL` and `HUSHRUN_SIGSTORE_TUF_ROOT` name another TUF repository and the root to start from, together; with them a cached trust root is required.
- **Refresh.** `hushrun sigstore refresh` updates with TUF: root versions signed by the threshold of the previous root's keys and of their own, the timestamp, snapshot and targets roles, their versions and expiry, and the trust root's length and hashes. It refuses a `targets.json` older than the cached one or than the snapshot in the binary.
- **Availability.** A signer that can't be reached or refuses (the ID token endpoint, Fulcio, every timestamp authority for keyless signing, or the TUF repository during a refresh) exits 9. Reading never needs the network: verification is offline (E-10, E-11).
- URLs are `https://`, or `http://` on the loopback for tests. Redirects are not followed, and no secret is logged.

### 7.4 The log key

**T-8 Signed trust [M13].** [ADR 0026, amendment 2] Removed, and [ADR 0031] so is the trust that replaced it (T-15). Ledgers keep no root (`root/<n>.json`) and no signed trust (`trust.dsse.json`), `hushrun.yaml` pins no root, and `trust root init`, `trust sign`, `hushrun repository put-root` and `put-trust`, and the organization's `org sign` are unknown commands. Nothing was released with them, so nothing reads them either.

**T-9 Ledger log key [M18].** An HTTP ledger that keeps a transparency log (L-11) signs its checkpoints, its listings and its signers statements (L-13) with a log key. [ADR 0031] `hushrun.yaml` pins it, at the top level, so that the ledger can't vouch for itself; it is read with the rest of the file (T-5):

```yaml
transparency_log:
  origin: hushrun.example/l/acme/back      # the ledger's URL without its scheme
  key: "ed25519:…"                          # the public half of its log key
```

- `origin` is printable ASCII without spaces, `+` or a scheme; it is what the log's checkpoints name, and the name of its key in their signatures. [M13] It stays the ledger's own URL, without its scheme, when readers reach the ledger by another one, such as a base-derived URL (T-4), and `hushrun doctor` compares it with the origin the ledger says it has. `key` is an Ed25519 public key (E-2), never a P-256 one, since signed notes are Ed25519; it must not be a machine's: the log key signs checkpoints, listings and signers statements, never events (CP-3).
- With it, readers of an `https://` ledger check every receipt, listing and checkpoint against it (L-11). A ledger whose answers carry no log is then `Unavailable` (exit 6).
- Without it, readers of a ledger that keeps a log check nothing and warn, until `hushrun.yaml` names the key: the migration of ledgers created before the log. `hushrun doctor` says which lines to add, from what the ledger says about itself (`GET {base}`), and `hushrun repository monitor` refuses to run.
- Other ledgers keep no log, and ignore `transparency_log`. `npx hushrun init` writes it from what the server tells the signed-in person (C-15).

**T-13 Witnesses [M19].** [ADR 0033] Removed. `transparency_log` names the log key only (T-9): `witnesses`, `quorum`, `map_quorum` and `max_age` there are unknown keys (C-1), and nothing reads checkpoints' cosignatures. The server holds no witnesses and `GET {base}` states none; `hushrun witness` is an unknown subcommand, and `doctor` has no `witnesses` or `server witnesses` check. Nothing was released with them. Readers check what L-11 says (L-12).

### 7.5 Organization policy

[ADR 0031] Removed: an organization's members and agents, and its repositories' CI, are the server's to say (T-15), so there is nothing to compile into each repository. `org.yaml`, `hushrun org plan` and `apply`, `hushrun doctor --org` and `hushrun adopt --org` are gone (unknown file, subcommand and arguments), and so are rules O-1 to O-8.

### 7.6 `hushrun run` with a command

`hushrun run [NAME] -- COMMAND` runs COMMAND unless this exact code already passed it, and records the result where every machine that shares the backend finds it. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`, keys or ledger. [ADR 0062] Without a command after `--`, `hushrun run CHECK…` runs hushrun.yaml's checks and records their evidence in the ledger instead (§10.4); `--all`, `--isolated`, `--timeout`, `--environment-image` and `--plan` belong to that form, and exit 2 with a command.

**C-9 Run key.** A run's key is the SHA-256 of the JCS form (RFC 8785, as in E-6) of:

```json
{"schema": "hushrun.run-key/v0", "check": "<NAME>", "command": ["<COMMAND>", "<argument>", …], "cwd": "<dir>", "scope": "<scope>", "digest": "sha256:<hex>"}
```

- `check` is NAME, a name as L-2 says. Without NAME, it is the script that runs hushrun, as npm, pnpm, yarn and bun name it in `npm_lifecycle_event`, other than `npx` (which `npx` and `npm exec` set), made a name; when it leaves none, or there is no script, COMMAND's program made a name, from its last path segment (after `/` or `\`) without its extension. Text is made a name in ASCII lowercase, with what comes before its first letter `a`–`z` dropped, each run of characters other than `a-z`, `0-9` and `_` turned into one `-`, at most 63 characters and no trailing `-`. So `"test": "hushrun run -- vitest run"` in `package.json` names its check `test`, `"test:unit"` names it `test-unit` and `"format:check"`, `format-check`; and `hushrun run -- vitest run` at a prompt, `vitest`. A program that leaves no name, such as `./123`, is a usage error.
- `command` is COMMAND, one string per argument, exactly as given: `vitest run`, `vitest run --coverage` and `npx vitest run` are three keys, so a partial run never stands in for a full one.
- `cwd` is the directory hushrun runs in, relative to the repository root with symlinks resolved, `/`-separated: `""` at the root.
- `scope` is `--scope`'s, or the only scope. Without `hushrun.yaml` it is `default`, every file. A `hushrun.yaml` with several scopes needs `--scope`, or hushrun can't tell (C-14).
- `digest` is that scope's source digest (§4) of the worktree, as `git add -A && git commit` would record it (D-10): tracked and untracked files with their modes, ignored ones left out. `--rev` hashes a commit instead, and `--digest` gives the digest. [ADR 0060] Either is the caller's word for the code COMMAND tests: hushrun can't tell what COMMAND reads, so it reads no file here for them, before COMMAND or after it, and a COMMAND that tests the files here is run without them. One that tests a commit's own export, as a release gate does on a checkout that has moved on, passes `--rev`. (`hushrun run CHECK…`, which runs a check's `run` on the files here, checks them, §10.4.)
- [ADR 0037] `external` is what the scope declares outside the repository (C-4), by path, each `{"path": "<as declared>", "digest": "sha256:<hex>"}`, the source digest (§4) of its `include` and `exclude` in that checkout's worktree (D-10), `digest.exclude` applying too: dirty files included, whatever `--rev` or `--digest` say, since it is what a build here reads. It is in the key only when the scope declares one, so every other key stays as it is.
- [ADR 0045] `counts_from` is the rule C-25 resolves for this check in this scope, normalized: an object with only the keys the rule writes, each a sorted list of strings, however the file writes them, and `env` an object of such lists by variable. `{os: linux, go: "1.25"}` is `{"go": ["1.25"], "os": ["linux"]}`. It holds the rule, never what a machine measured, so every machine that meets it computes this key, and `hushrun ready` needs no server for it. It is in the key only when there is a rule: `anywhere` and no rule add nothing, so every other key stays as it is. The rule is read from the `hushrun.yaml` the run reads (T-5, `--config`, `--config-ref`, `HUSHRUN_CONFIG`, `--rev`), the same read that says what the run has to meet.

Nothing else is in the key: not the machine, the OS, the CI provider, the environment, the branch, the commit or the time. So a pass on a laptop stands in for a run in CI on the same files, and the other way round; committing changes nothing, and going back to files that passed skips. [ADR 0044] Because the key holds no environment, two rules narrow what a pass stands in for in CI, and the key stays as it is: a CI pass counts in its own job (C-10), and a job with several variants doesn't skip (C-14). In paths and URLs, a key is its 64 lowercase hex. For example, the JCS form `{"check":"unit","command":["vitest","run"],"cwd":"","digest":"sha256:1111…1111","schema":"hushrun.run-key/v0","scope":"default"}`, with 64 `1`s, has the key `1abcbeed3a0264f77f5da8fad0aa13880079d906e0708bc931cbca454b345425`; with `"external":[{"digest":"sha256:2222…2222","path":"../cli"}]`, with 64 `2`s, the key `853ae97a48a239d2f64a5079d77204544acd3aa5350167385dac1dbd557539ee`; and with `"counts_from":{"go":["1.25"],"os":["linux"]}` and no `external`, the key `7a6794efe55fb3b588d8677119e52de0a035ed3f6e772da188f7136839d8d40b`.

**C-10 Run results.** Each run is recorded as one `hushrun.run-result/v0` object (`docs/schemas/run-result.v0.schema.json`), and [ADR 0049] the newest one for a key that counts in this job decides the next run:

```json
{"schema": "hushrun.run-result/v0", "check": "unit", "result": "pass", "exitCode": 0,
 "command": ["vitest", "run"], "cwd": "", "scope": "default", "digest": "sha256:…",
 "revision": "<commit>", "repository": "github.com/acme/shop",
 "startedAt": "2026-10-16T14:05:10.123Z", "durationMs": 41000, "provider": "github",
 "job": "unit@linux/amd64",
 "environment": {"platform": "linux/amd64", "tools": {"go": "1.25.14"}},
 "runUrl": "https://github.com/acme/shop/actions/runs/1", "tool": "hushrun 0.1.0"}
```

- `result` is `pass` when COMMAND exits 0, and `fail` otherwise. A run that a signal stops isn't recorded.
- A newest `pass` skips, unless it doesn't count here (below) or no pass skips this job (C-14): COMMAND doesn't run, hushrun exits 0, and it records a `skip` with `exitCode: null`, its own time as `durationMs`, and the pass's `durationMs` as `savedMs`, the time the skip saved. A newest `fail` runs COMMAND again. A pass and a fail on one key show a flaky check. [G13] When a run's verdict isn't that of the result that decided it here, the newest that counts in this job and variant, the line that ends it adds `; it passed on this exact code before, so it may be flaky` or `; it failed on this exact code before, so it may be flaky`, and on GitHub Actions the step summary says the same. A pass that doesn't count here, or one a run in a job whose variants or calls it can't tell apart found, decided nothing, and says nothing of flakiness.
- [ADR 0044] `job` is the CI job that ran it and the system it ran on, `<job>@<platform>`, such as `unit@linux/amd64`. `<job>` is `GITHUB_JOB` on GitHub Actions, without the workflow's file, so a CI workflow and a deploy workflow with one job id share a pass; on GitLab CI it is `CI_JOB_NAME`, which differs per `parallel` instance and `parallel:matrix` variant. `<platform>` is measured from the running binary and written as E-7 writes it, such as `darwin/arm64`; it is the only measured thing in `job`, and needs nothing from a provider. `job` is left out outside CI, and where CI names none.
- [ADR 0044] **A CI pass belongs to its job.** For a run with a `job`, a `pass` counts when its `provider` is `local`, as C-9 says; when its `job` is this run's, system included; or when it has no `job` and its `provider` is `generic`. Any other doesn't: another job's, and a pass from a named CI provider with no `job`, recorded before this rule, which costs one run, once. For a run with no `job` of its own, any `pass` counts, as before.
- [ADR 0049] **A variant's pass is its own.** A run in a job that says which of its variants it is (C-14) has a `variant`, and a `pass` counts there only when it has this `job` and this `variant`: a pass from outside CI, `local` or a generic CI's with no `job`, doesn't, since it ran no variant. A run with no `variant` takes no pass that has one.
- [ADR 0049] **The lookup asks for the newest result that counts in this job.** In CI, the run asks with its `job` and its `variant.key` (results-v0 §3), and the server answers the newest pass or fail of those that count, by the rules above, so that two jobs that run one check each find their own pass. The run checks the answer again by the same rules. A server from before this answers the newest result for the key, whatever job recorded it; the run then decides on that one, as before, and runs when it doesn't count. When nothing counts, the run asks once more for the newest result, only to say why it runs. COMMAND runs with one line, `hushrun: <NAME> running: <why>`: `this exact code passed in another CI job, and a CI pass counts in its own job`; `this exact code passed in another variant of this job, and each variant counts only its own passes`; or `this exact code passed outside CI, and a job with several variants counts only its own variant's passes`.
- [ADR 0049] `variant` is which variant of its job the run was, when the job has several and says which (C-14): `{"key": "sha256:<64 lowercase hex>", "label": "<at most 100 characters>"}`. `key` is the `sha256:` digest of the value's JCS form, so that key order and spaces don't change it: the matrix's values, or, in a called workflow, `{"inputs": …, "matrix": …}` with each `null` when there are none. `label` is the matrix's values in key order, scalars as written and anything else as compact JSON, joined with `, `, cut to 100 characters with `…`, for people to read; inputs never show in it. It informs the lookup only: the key, which holds no environment (C-9), doesn't change.
- Skips never answer a lookup: a directory keeps them apart (C-11), and a server leaves them out (C-12).
- `revision` (the commit checked out), `repository` (`host/owner/name`: the CI provider's, else `hushrun.yaml`'s, else the `origin` remote's), `provider` (`local`, or the CI provider, §11.2) and `runUrl` inform. Only the key decides.
- [ADR 0050] `command` is COMMAND with every secret in it `[redacted]`, since the record goes to the backend and shows in its dashboard. That is a word that is a secret, and what follows an `=` in a word when that is one, as in `NAME=value` or `--token=value`, with what comes before the first such `=` kept. So with `DEPLOY_TOKEN` set, `env DEPLOY_TOKEN=$DEPLOY_TOKEN make` shows as `["env", "DEPLOY_TOKEN=[redacted]", "make"]`. A secret is the value, of 8 bytes or more, of a variable of the environment hushrun runs in whose name holds `TOKEN`, `SECRET`, `PASSWORD`, `PASSWD`, `PASS`, `KEY`, `CREDENTIAL`, `AUTH`, `PRIVATE` or `CERT`, in any case; or what is shaped like a token, whatever the variables: it starts with `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghu_`, `ghs_`, `ghr_`, `github_pat_`, `glpat-` or `-----BEGIN`; with `xox`, one of `abprs` and `-`; with `AKIA` and 16 uppercase letters or digits; or with `sk-` and 20 or more letters, digits, `-` or `_`. Only the record is redacted: the key holds COMMAND as given (C-9), so a command finds the same results whatever the variables say, and what skips never changes. It is a second line of defense: a secret that no variable names and no shape shows stays as it is.
- [ADR 0037] `external` is the key's, left out when the scope declares none.
- [ADR 0045] `environment` is on every result: `platform`, as E-7 writes it, and `tools`, the version of each tool a rule made hushrun measure, by the name C-25 gives it, left out when there are none, as for a skip, which measures nothing. It holds no variable. It informs, and is the machine's own word. Only the key decides.
- `invokedBy` is `HUSHRUN_INVOKED_BY`, `agent:<id>` or `human:<id>` as events take it (E-4), with the id trimmed: a label that tells apart the runs of a fleet sharing one agent token (ADR 0027). It informs. A value of another form is left out, and hushrun says so in a line.
- [ADR 0048] `reports` is the summary of the test and coverage reports the run wrote (C-27), left out when the search went past its bounds or read none of what it found, and `{"filesComplete": true}` when it found none; a skip's is its pass's, as `savedMs` is that pass's `durationMs`. It informs, and a reader that can't read it takes the result without it.
- [ADR 0068] `units` is `{"total", "ran", "reused"}` when the check's `tests` aren't off and an adapter reported its test files (C-32): how many it collected, ran (C-32's `ran and passed`, `ran and failed` and `not observed`) and reused. [ADR 0069] With `ranAnyway`, a word, when something kept every test file from being reused (C-34): `force`, `reports`, `suspended`, or one of this field's own words for a job's variants. [ADR 0074] With `savedMs`, in a run that reused test files, how long they took when they last ran, by their recorded results (C-27's saving); never in `observe`. [ADR 0077] With `tests`, `{"total", "ran", "reused"}`, in a run with `tests.unit: test` whose adapter told its tests (C-39); a test file is then reused when none of its tests ran. It informs, and goes wherever the result does. A run that passed with reused test files is a pass, and skips the same files next time (C-34).
- [G11] `ranAnyway` says why it ran although a pass of its key would have skipped it, the one rule that stopped the skip: `variants`, its job has several variants and the run doesn't say which (C-14); `reusable`, its job may be a called workflow's that the run can't tell; `reports`, a later step reads its reports (C-26); `rule`, the pass doesn't count here, another job's, another variant's or from outside CI in a job with variants; `force`, `--force`; `inside-run`, it runs inside another run in a job with several variants; `cant-tell`, hushrun can't read its job. A run that decides without a lookup, with `--force` or by C-14's rules, asks for the newest result that counts in this job first, one lookup, with no variant for C-14's; with no pass there, it says nothing. It is left out of a skip, and informs: a dashboard counts the runs that could have skipped. A newer hushrun may say another word.
- Readers ignore fields they don't know. A result that doesn't parse, or whose `schema` is another, counts as none.

**C-11 Backends.** Results go to the first of:

1. `HUSHRUN_BACKEND`: an org's URL on a server that speaks `hushrun-results/v0` (C-12), such as `https://hushrun.example/o/acme`; `http://` only on a loopback host, as L-1 says. Empty turns results off: COMMAND always runs, and nothing is recorded. Another scheme, `file://` included (ADR 0022), turns them off too, with a message. [ADR 0046] The backend in `HUSHRUN_AGENT` (C-17) counts as named here, and wins over a `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` that isn't empty. `whoami` says `HUSHRUN_AGENT` named it. A URL that holds a string (C-17), from any source, is refused, and its error doesn't show it.
2. The repository's backend: `"hushrun": {"backend": "<URL>"}` in the `package.json` at the repository's root (the git top level), else [ADR 0037] `backend` in the `hushrun.yaml` there (C-3), committed with the code so that CI, teammates and agents need no variable (ADR 0023). A URL that isn't `https://` (L-1) turns results off, as it does in `HUSHRUN_BACKEND`, with a message naming the file. A file that doesn't parse names no backend. [ADR 0061] When both files name a backend, and not the same one, package.json's still wins, and hushrun says so once a command, on stderr unless `-q`: `package.json's hushrun.backend is <URL> and hushrun.yaml's backend is <URL>, so results go to package.json's: hushrun init --org <hushrun.yaml's org> finishes a move there, or make the two the same`. An interrupted `hushrun init --org` leaves them so, since it writes hushrun.yaml first. [ADR 0067] Outside CI, when nothing would go with its requests (no token goes there, `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` isn't withheld from it, no login for its origin, no ID token, no lookup as the repository's CI, C-13) and no agent is set (C-17), results go to this machine's directory instead (item 4), and the backend is asked nothing, until someone signs in: `hushrun: sign in to share results with <org>: npx hushrun login` at a terminal, else `hushrun: results aren't shared with <org>: nobody is signed in to it here, so they stay on this machine`, is the run's last line. A backend `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` or `HUSHRUN_AGENT` names, a token the server refuses and CI never do so.
3. The backend `hushrun login` signed in to (C-13).
4. `<cache dir>/results`, where the cache directory is `HUSHRUN_CACHE_DIR`, by default `<OS cache dir>/hushrun` (§12.6). An empty `HUSHRUN_CACHE_DIR` turns results off. [ADR 0067] In CI (§11.2) it turns them off too: a runner's directory is shared with no one, and a cache of it between jobs could be forged by whoever can write that cache. COMMAND always runs, nothing is recorded, and `run` says so once, before COMMAND, unless `-q` or inside another `hushrun run`: `hushrun: <NAME> running without skipping: results are off: in CI, nothing names a backend, and a runner's own directory isn't shared: run hushrun init to share results with CI, or set HUSHRUN_BACKEND`.

[ADR 0067] `Backend::select` alone makes this choice, shared on a server, kept on this machine or off, and every command that says where results go reads it from there: `run`, `ready` and the hook (C-22, C-23), `whoami` and `doctor` (C-17), `repository usage` (C-19) and `repository stats` (L-14).

What named the backend decides which tokens go with its requests (C-13): `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` goes only to the backend `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` names, so a change to a file in the repository can't send a CI secret elsewhere, except that an agent token (`hra_…`) also goes to the repository's or the login's backend on hushrun's host. [ADR 0046] The token in `HUSHRUN_AGENT` goes to the origin of the backend in the same string, and to no other. When `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` is set and another backend applies, hushrun says that the token isn't sent: `hushrun: HUSHRUN_TOKEN isn't sent to <base>: it goes only to the backend HUSHRUN_BACKEND names, and an agent token (hra_…) to hushrun's host, <host>`.

When a backend refuses a request that carried no token (401), hushrun runs COMMAND as C-14 says, doesn't try to record, and says nothing about it until COMMAND ends. Then it prints one line, once a run, where `<org>` is the org in the backend's URL (`…/o/<org>`), else the URL: when its stderr is a terminal and the backend is the repository's or the login's, `hushrun: sign in to share results with <org>: npx hushrun login`, the run's only line of hushrun's; otherwise, such as in a script or a CI job, which have no one to sign in, `hushrun: results aren't shared with <org>: <the server's message>`. In a GitHub Actions run of a pull request from a fork, whose head repository in the event's payload isn't `GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, the line says why instead of how, since such a job can't get a token: `hushrun: results aren't shared with <org>: GitHub gives a pull request from a fork no ID token, so its checks run, and record nothing`. [ADR 0052] A run that asked without a token as its repository's CI (C-13) and was answered ends with `…: GitHub gives a pull request from a fork no ID token, so it records nothing` in a fork's pull request, and `` …: no token: give the job `permissions: id-token: write` `` in any other job.

The cache's directory is a file ledger (L-3), created when missing, that only this machine reads: a backend that machines share is a server. A pass or a fail is kept at `results/v0/<key>/<ULID>.json` and a skip at `skips/v0/<key>/<ULID>.json`, where the ULID's time is when the run finished. A lookup reads the key's 10 newest results at most. [ADR 0067] After each run, `run` sweeps the directory, at most once a day, by the command's clock: a file of those whose ULID's time is more than 30 days before it goes, and so does a key directory left empty; anything else stays. `<cache dir>/results/swept` holds when the last sweep began, as RFC 3339, written whole before anything goes; within a day after it, by the same clock, no sweep runs, and a clock behind it sweeps by its own time. When the stamp can't be written, there is no sweep. A sweep never fails or holds up a run, and runs at once never delete a result newer than their cutoff.

**C-12 Results protocol.** A server speaks `hushrun-results/v0` at `{base}`, the org's URL normalized as L-1 says. hushrun sends requests to that origin only, with C-13's token, retried as L-4 says within 10 s for all of a run's requests, and follows no redirect.

| Request | Answer |
|---|---|
| `GET {base}/v0` | 200 `{"protocol": "hushrun-results/v0", "org": "<org>", "as": "<who the token is>", "dashboard": "<URL>", "token": {…}}`; `hushrun login` checks access with it, and `hushrun whoami` shows it (C-17). `dashboard`, the org's page in the server's dashboard, is optional; so is `token`, which describes an agent token: `{"kind": "agent", "name", "prefix", "repository": "<host/owner/name>" \| null, "expires_at", "results_per_minute"}` |
| `GET {base}/v0/results/{key}` | 200 `{"result": <the newest pass or fail recorded for the key> \| null}`, with `"ciOnly": true` when the server answered from the CI's results only (below). [ADR 0037] With `?as=ci&repository=<host/owner/name>`, what that repository's CI would find (below). [ADR 0049] With `?job=<job>[&variant=<variant key>]`, the newest of those that count in that job and variant, as C-10 says, with `"forJob": true`. [ADR 0052] Without a token, in GitHub Actions, with `?as=ci&repository=` of the run's repository (C-13): a server may answer it, and hushrun records nothing |
| `POST {base}/v0/results/{key}`, a run result as the body, and [ADR 0028] `Hushrun-Signature` with a personal or an agent token (C-18) | 2xx: recorded. [ADR 0032] 402 `quota_exceeded`: not recorded, for the org's plan (below). [ADR 0028 amendment 1] 401 `machine_required` (a personal or an agent token's result without a signature) or `machine_unknown` (signed by a key that isn't a registered machine of the token's owner): not recorded |
| `POST {base}/v0/repositories` `{"repository": "<host/owner/name>"}`, optional | 201 `{"repository": "<id>", "connected": true}` when it is new, 200 the same when it already was: the repository's CI may now record results at `{base}` with its own ID token |
| `POST {base}/v0/ledgers` `{"repository": "<host/owner/name>", "github_repository_id": <GitHub's id>, "default_branch": "<branch>", "dry_run": true}`, optional | 201 `{"name": "<ledger>", "url": "<its URL>", "dashboard": "<its page>", "github_repository_id": <id> \| null, "default_branch": "<branch>" \| null}` when the server made the org's ledger for the repository, 200 the same when the org had one; `dashboard` and `default_branch` are optional. [ADR 0026] With `dry_run`, 200 the same with `"dry_run": true` and `"exists"`, and nothing made |
| `GET {base}/v0/agent-tokens`, optional | 200 `{"items": [<agent token>, …]}`: the org's agent tokens that aren't revoked, expired ones included, newest first |
| `POST {base}/v0/agent-tokens` `{"name": "<agent>", "repository": "<host/owner/name>" \| null, "expires_in_hours": <1 to 8760>, "results_per_minute": <1 to 10000>, "machine": {"key": "<public key>", "proof": "<base64>"}}`, optional | 201: the agent token, with `"token": "hra_…"`, shown once, `"backend"`, the org's URL, and [ADR 0028 amendment 1] `"machine": {"id", "keyid", "name"}`, the agent's machine the server registered with it. `repository` null is the whole org; `expires_in_hours` and `results_per_minute` are optional, the server's defaults then; `machine`, the agent's key, is required (below) |
| `DELETE {base}/v0/agent-tokens/{id}`, optional | 204: revoked |
| `POST {base}/v0/runs/{key}` `{"schema": "hushrun.run-units/v0", "result": <run result>, "units": [{"key", "result", "durationMs"}]}`, optional | [ADR 0069] As `POST {base}/v0/results/{key}`, signed the same way over this body, with its test files' results kept with the run (C-33): each `key` 64 lowercase hex, once, `result` `pass` or `fail`, 5,000 at most; [ADR 0078] 50,000 at most and 8 MiB of body, the run's results sent failures first, then test files', then tests' passes, and never more than 50,000 nor a body past 8 MiB: hushrun cuts from the last, as it does past 50,000 (400 `invalid` ``a run records at most 50000 results of its test files and tests, and this one has <n>``, 413 past 8 MiB). Since hushrun's body fits 8 MiB, a 413 whose body is `Failed to buffer the request body: length limit exceeded` comes from a server with a smaller limit, an older one; a 413 in other words (such as `{"error": {"code": "too_large"}}`) is the server's refusal, and the run isn't sent again. An older server's 413 (`Failed to buffer the request body: length limit exceeded`) or 400 ``a run records at most 5000 test files' results, and this one has <n>`` gets the run once more with the first 5,000, and the run says once `<check>: <n> of its <m> results of test files and tests weren't kept: <org> keeps 5,000 a run`. An older server's 400 `invalid` whose message starts ``a result's `units` is `` and ends `0 when none was reused` (a `savedMs` with no test file reused, as when only tests were) gets the run once more without `savedMs`, here and on `POST {base}/v0/results/{key}`. A run's `units.tests` is `{total, ran, reused}`. 404 or 405: a server from before it, and hushrun records the result alone (C-34) |
| `POST {base}/v0/units/lookup` `{"check", "repository", "keys": [<64 hex>…], "job"?, "variant"?}`, optional | [ADR 0069] 200 `{"units": {"<key>": {"result", "durationMs", "run": <index>}}, "runs": [<run result>…], "reuse": {"state": "on" \| "suspended", "generation", "suspendedAt"?, "suspendedBy"?, "resumedAt"?, "resumedBy"?}}`: for each key, 5,000 at most, the newest unit result whose run counts for the caller as a lookup of results does (the job and variant, an agent token's repository, CI's results only), with that run's record, which hushrun checks again (C-10); and the check's reuse state for the repository (C-34) |
| `POST {base}/v0/units/reuse` `{"check", "repository", "state": "suspended", "unit", "run"}` or `{"check", "repository", "state": "on", "generation"}`, optional | [ADR 0069] 200 `{"reuse": <state>}`. Suspending takes any caller that records results, and keeps a suspended check as it is; turning it on takes a personal token only (403 otherwise) and the generation the person read: 409 `conflict`, with `reuse`, when it is on already or was suspended again since |
| `POST {base}/v0/units/hints/lookup` `{"repository", "checkId", "address", "os"}`, optional | [ADR 0075] 200 `{"hints": [{"sha256", "body": <base64>}]}`: the newest 3 hints kept at that place, most recently seen first (C-38). 404 or 405: a server from before hints, which a run asks nothing more of them and says so once |
| `PUT {base}/v0/units/hints/{sha256}?repository=&checkId=&address=&os=`, a hint's bytes as the body, optional | [ADR 0075] 201 `{}` when the hint is new at that place, 200 `{}` when the same bytes were there: kept (C-38). Never signed, metered or refused for the org's plan: a hint is never trusted. 404 or 405 as above |
| `GET {base}/v0/usage`, optional | [ADR 0032] 200 `{"org", "plan", "plan_name", "period_start", "period_end", "used", "included", "limit", "overage", "overage_cents", "state", "repositories": [{"repository", "checks"}], "free": [{"repository", "checks"}], "dashboard"}`: the org's plan and this period's recorded checks (below); `hushrun repository usage` shows it (C-19) |

- `{key}` is the run key's 64 lowercase hex.
- **CI reuses only CI's results.** A server may let a repository keep its CI from skipping on a laptop's or an agent's pass: with that setting on, it answers a lookup by the repository's CI (an ID token, or another CI identity the server verifies) only with results CI recorded, and adds `"ciOnly": true`, with `"result": null` too. Lookups by people and agents are unaffected, but for one that asks: [ADR 0037] `?as=ci&repository=<id>`, which `hushrun ready` sends, answers as to that repository's CI, `ciOnly` included, whoever asks, since the setting is told to no one else. A server may ignore the query, and a lookup then answers as to anyone. hushrun then ends its line with `; skipped only on CI's passes` (`unit skipped: this exact code passed in CI 3 min ago (saves 41 s); skipped only on CI's passes`), or says `unit running: no CI pass yet for this exact code; skipped only on CI's passes`. The server enforces it; hushrun only says so. hushrun cloud's setting is the repository page's "CI reuses only CI's results" (api-v1).
- The server decides who may read and record. It keeps skips, and never answers a lookup with one (C-10).
- A server may add fields, which hushrun ignores. Any other answer to a lookup means hushrun can't read earlier results (C-14). [ADR 0044] It answers a result as it was sent: a CI pass that comes back without its `job` doesn't count in CI (C-10). [ADR 0049] A server from before `?job=` ignores it and answers the newest result for the key, without `forJob`: the run then decides on that one by C-10's rules, and skips less. `job` and `variant` are query values, percent-encoded, since GitLab's job names hold spaces and brackets; they combine with an agent token's repository, `ciOnly` and `?as=ci` as those do with each other.
- Errors are `{"error": {"code": "<code>", "message": "<text>"}}`, and hushrun shows the message.
- **Connecting a repository** is for people, so that a repository's CI needs no secret: `POST {base}/v0/repositories` takes a personal token (C-13) only. A server answers 401 without a token; 403 `forbidden` to an ID token, since CI can't connect repositories, and to a person who isn't a member of the org; 400 `invalid` to an id that isn't `host/owner/name`. It compares and keeps `github.com` ids lowercase. A server without the request answers 404 or 405, and hushrun then says to connect CI in the server's own settings. How a server then matches a CI job's ID token to a connected repository is its own; hushrun cloud's is in ADR 0023.
- **Agent tokens** (ADR 0027) are for an org's admins: the three `agent-tokens` requests take a personal token only. An agent token is `{"id", "name", "repository", "prefix", "results_per_minute", "created_at", "created_by", "expires_at", "last_used_at"}`, where `prefix` is the token's first 10 characters (`hra_` and 6): the server keeps the token's hash, never the token. [ADR 0028 amendment 1] A token comes with its agent's key, made where the token is made: `machine` is the key's public half (C-18's text: `ecdsa-p256:…`, or `ed25519:…`) and `proof`, its signature, in standard base64, over `hushrun-agent-machine/v1\n<org>\n<agent name>\n<key id>\n`; the server registers the key, named `agent:<agent name>`, as the token's one machine, in the same transaction. A server answers 401 without a token; 403 to an agent token, an ID token, a person who isn't a member, or a member who isn't an admin; 400 to a body it can't take, a `machine` missing or a key that doesn't parse included, and 400 `invalid_proof` to a proof that doesn't verify; 404 to a `DELETE` of a token that is unknown or already revoked. A server without them answers 404 or 405, and `hushrun token` says so (C-17).
- [ADR 0032] **The org's plan.** A server may limit the checks an org's plan records in a period, as hushrun cloud does: past the limit, it answers the record of a `pass` or a `fail` with 402 and L-10's `quota_exceeded` body (`{"error": {"code": "quota_exceeded", "message"}, "plan", "used", "limit", "period_end", "upgrade_url"}`, only the message sure to be there), and the result isn't recorded (C-14). It never refuses a lookup or a skip so: reuse is free. `GET {base}/v0/usage` says where the org stands: `used`, the checks recorded since `period_start`, until `period_end` (RFC 3339); `included`, what the plan includes, and `limit`, where recording stops, each null for a plan without one; `overage` and `overage_cents`, past what the plan includes; `state`, the server's word for it (such as `ok`, `near`, `over_included`, `at_limit`); `repositories`, each repository's checks, most first, `""` for checks without one; `free`, what open source repositories recorded for free, most first, in none of the other numbers, and left out by a server that doesn't track it; `dashboard`, the org's usage page. Only `used` is required. A server without the request answers 404 or 405.
- **An agent token** records and reads results of its org, as `agent:<name>`, and nothing else: [ADR 0028 amendment 1] its results signed by its one machine, the key it came with (C-18), and it registers no machine of its own. A server answers 401 to one that is unknown, expired or revoked, and 403 at another org, each with a message that names the token by its prefix. A token scoped to a repository reads only that repository's results, and a server answers 403 to a result whose `repository` names another. A server may answer a write with 429 `rate_limited` when the token recorded more results in the last minute than its `results_per_minute`; hushrun doesn't wait a `Retry-After` past its 10 s (L-4), and the result isn't recorded (C-14).
- **The repository's ledger** is for people too, so that the repository's deploys (§10) start from one: `POST {base}/v0/ledgers` takes a personal token only, and answers the org's ledger for the repository, the one the org has or one the server makes, the same ledger however often it is asked. The repository keeps its case; `github_repository_id` is GitHub's id for a repository on `github.com`, which hushrun sends when `gh` names it (C-15), so that the ledger takes the repository's CI ID tokens (L-10) even when it is private. A server answers 401 without a token; 403 to an ID token and to a person who isn't a member, or who may not make the ledger; 409 `conflict` to an id that contradicts the one it knows for the repository; 400 to an id that isn't `host/owner/name`. A server without the request answers 404 or 405, and hushrun then says that promoting the repository needs a ledger from the server's own settings. Who may make a ledger, its name and where else its GitHub id comes from are the server's; hushrun cloud's are in ADR 0025. [ADR 0026] `default_branch` is the repository's default branch, which the server keeps for display (an admin's word, like the id); `dry_run: true` makes, binds and changes nothing, and answers what the request would find or make (`"exists": false` for a ledger it would make, under the name it would take) or the refusal it would get. A server from before `dry_run` answers as for a request; hushrun tells by the answer's `dry_run`.

**C-13 Signing in.** Requests to a server carry `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, from the first of:

1. [ADR 0046] the agent token in `HUSHRUN_AGENT` (C-17), to the origin of the backend in the same string;
2. `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`, when `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` names the backend (C-11); or when it is an agent token, `hra_…`, and the backend, the repository's or the login's, is on hushrun's host: the origin of `HUSHRUN_HOST`, by default `https://api.hushrun.com` (ADR 0027);
3. the personal token `hushrun login` saved for the backend's origin (`scheme://host[:port]`), [ADR 0060] outside CI only (§11.2): CI takes no person's login, as the repository's ledger doesn't (L-10), so that a runner where someone signed in records its jobs as CI, not as that person;
4. in GitHub Actions, when the job has `permissions: id-token: write` (`ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_URL` and `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_REQUEST_TOKEN` are set), an ID token whose audience is the backend's normalized URL, renewed a minute before it expires.

Without any, requests carry no token; a server that refuses them leaves COMMAND to run unrecorded (C-14).

[ADR 0052] In GitHub Actions without any, as in a pull request from a fork, which GitHub gives no ID token and no secret, a lookup asks as the repository's CI: `?as=ci&repository=github.com/<GITHUB_REPOSITORY>`, the base repository in a fork's pull request, before any other query it has, on github.com only (`GITHUB_SERVER_URL` unset or `https://github.com`). A server may answer it, as hushrun cloud does for a public repository of its open-source program from what that repository's CI recorded, and a pass that counts in the job skips, as any does (C-10). Such a run records nothing: it sends no `POST`, for a skip either, and ends with C-11's one line. Outside GitHub Actions, hushrun never asks without a token as CI: a laptop signs in.

Each token stays with the server it is for: a personal token goes to its own origin only, an ID token names the backend it is for, and `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` follows only `HUSHRUN_BACKEND`, which lives in the CI's settings, outside the code. So a backend that the repository's `package.json` names, which a change to that file could replace, gets no token it could use elsewhere (ADR 0023). An agent token is the exception that keeps the rule: it follows the repository's backend only on hushrun's host, which the environment or hushrun names, never the repository, and the server there binds the token to its org (ADR 0027). [ADR 0046] A `HUSHRUN_AGENT` string is never a token, whatever holds it or is around it: it holds a private key. In any other of hushrun's variables it is dropped, with a line (C-17), and the client refuses to send one from anywhere else (L-10).

- **`hushrun login [URL]`** [ADR 0046] exits 2 with an agent set (C-17), and so does any sign-in: it would register the agent's key as a person's machine (C-18). It signs in to URL; else `HUSHRUN_BACKEND`; else the repository's backend (C-11); else the backend it signed in to last; else hushrun's hosted backend, `HUSHRUN_HOST`, by default `https://api.hushrun.com`. Without URL, it says which of these it signs in to. URL is an org's URL, or a server's origin; it must be `https://`, or `http://` on a loopback host, or hushrun exits 2. It follows RFC 8628's device flow, in JSON, at URL's origin:
  - `POST {origin}/cli/login` `{"client": "hushrun <version> on <host name>", "org": "<org>", "machine": {"key": "<public key>", "name": "<host name>"}}` answers `{"device_code", "user_code", "verification_url", "expires_in", "interval"}`. `org` is sent when URL is an org's (`…/o/<org>`): a hint, the org the approval page offers first. [ADR 0028] `machine` is this machine's key (C-18), made the first time, and its name;
  - hushrun prints the user code and the URL, opens the URL unless `--no-browser`, and someone signed in to the server approves the code there, for one of their orgs;
  - hushrun polls `POST {origin}/cli/login/token` `{"device_code", "proof"}` every `interval` seconds until `expires_in` runs out, where [ADR 0028] `proof` is the machine key's signature over `hushrun-machine/v1\n<device_code>\n<keyid>\n`, in standard base64. 200 `{"token", "email", "org", "backend", "machine"}` signs in, where `org` and `backend`, the org the sign-in was approved for and its URL, and `machine`, `{"id", "keyid"}`, the machine the server registered, are optional; 400 with the code `authorization_pending` waits, and `slow_down` waits 5 s longer each time; [ADR 0061] a 429 or a 5xx, or a host out of reach, waits too, since the code may be approved already, and says so once, `hushrun: <reason>: asking again until the code expires`; any other answer ends the sign-in and exits 1, with the server's message. When `expires_in` runs out the sign-in ends, whatever the server asked: with exit 6 and the reason when the host was failing, else with exit 1 and `not signed in: the code <code> expired before it was approved: run the command again for a new one`. [ADR 0028 amendment 1] Two such answers come from registering the machine: 409 `machine_limit`, when the person has as many live machines in the org as the server allows, whose message names the dashboard's page to revoke one on; and 409 `machine_revoked`, when this machine's key was revoked there for good, to which hushrun adds that removing the key file (C-18) makes this machine a new key. A host that can't be reached, or won't start a sign-in, exits 6;
  - hushrun saves the token for the origin, and the key id of the machine registered with it; it says `Registered this machine, <name> (<key id>), for its writes to <origin>.`, or, when the answer names no machine, that the server doesn't register machines. The backend becomes URL when URL is an org's, whatever the answer names; else the answer's `backend`, when it is on the same origin. hushrun checks access with `GET {base}/v0`, and prints `Signed in to <org> as <email>`, with a warning when the org refuses. [ADR 0061] An org that answers 403 or 404, to someone who isn't its member, doesn't become the backend: that stays what it was, and the warning says where results go, `warning: signed in as <email>, but <URL> refused it, so results still go to <backend>: <the server's message>`, or `so results stay on this machine`. When neither names an org, it says so, and forgets the backend: results stay on this machine until `hushrun login <org URL>`; when the answer names no org, it says to create or join one at the dashboard's `/new`, the `verification_url` without its `/device` page.
- [ADR 0041] **`GET {origin}/cli/orgs`**, with the login's token, answers `{"orgs": [{"slug", "name", "role"}], "dashboard"}`: the orgs the person belongs to, and the dashboard, whose `/new` page makes another. `dashboard` is optional, and a server may add fields. 401 means the login no longer works; a server without the request answers 404 or 405.
- **`hushrun logout [URL]`** revokes the token for URL's origin, by default that of the backend `hushrun login` would sign in to: `POST {origin}/cli/logout` with the token. It forgets the token whatever the answer, and forgets the backend when it is on that origin, so `hushrun run` goes back to the cache directory.
- **The credentials file** is `HUSHRUN_CREDENTIALS`, else `<config dir>/hushrun/credentials.json` (`~/.config` on Linux, `~/Library/Application Support` on macOS, `%APPDATA%` on Windows):

  ```json
  {"backend": "https://hushrun.example/o/acme",
   "hosts": {"https://hushrun.example": {"token": "…", "email": "ana@acme.dev", "machine": "sha256:…", "machine_org": "acme"}}}
  ```

  It is written whole, through a temporary file and a rename, readable by its owner only (mode 0600 on Unix); [ADR 0061] each sign-in and sign-out reads, changes and writes it holding a lock on `<file>.lock` beside it, so two at once keep each other's change, and a sign-out leaves a token a sign-in saved there since it began. A token goes to its own origin only, and is never logged. [ADR 0028] `machine`, optional, is the key id of the machine key the sign-in registered (C-18), which lives beside the file, and [ADR 0041] `machine_org`, optional, the org it registered it in.

**C-14 When hushrun runs anyway.** hushrun runs COMMAND whenever it can't tell whether this code passed, so a mistake costs at most a run, never a skip that should have been a run:

- when one of hushrun's variables holds what it can't read (§10.1), such as `HUSHRUN_FORMAT=xml` or an unknown `HUSHRUN_PROVIDER`, it says so in one line, `hushrun: HUSHRUN_FORMAT is "xml", not text, json, env or dotenv, so it's ignored`, and goes on as if it were unset; an empty one is unset, and says nothing; and a variable that isn't UTF-8, whatever its name, is ignored too. [ADR 0046] So is a `HUSHRUN_AGENT` hushrun can't read, in a line that says what's wrong and never what it holds (C-17);
- when it can't tell which code this is, outside a repository for one, it runs and records nothing; [ADR 0037] and so when a scope's `external` input is missing, isn't the top of a git checkout, or includes no file: `hushrun: <NAME> running without skipping: can't tell which code this is (the external input <path>: …)`;
- when results are off (C-11), or the credentials file can't be read, it runs and records nothing; [ADR 0067] in CI with no server, it says so in C-11's one line;
- when the lookup fails (the backend can't be reached, or answers something else), it runs, and still tries to record;
- when the backend refuses the lookup's credential (401 or 403), it says so in one line, `hushrun: <NAME> running: <org> refused <HUSHRUN_AGENT, HUSHRUN_TOKEN, your login or this job's ID token>: <the server's message>`, runs, and doesn't try to record, since the same credential would be refused; its last line is `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>; not recorded` (or `failed in <time> (exit <code>)`);
- when the backend refuses a request that carried no token (401), it runs, doesn't try to record, and prints only C-11's one line, at the end;
- when the backend refuses the record (403 for a result of another repository, 429 for a token over its limit), its last line is `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>; not recorded: <the server's message>`;
- [ADR 0028 amendment 1] when the record needs a machine's signature (C-18) and hushrun has no key to sign it with, it doesn't send the record; and when the backend refuses the record for want of a registered machine's signature (401 `machine_required` or `machine_unknown`), it says the same. Its last line is `` hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>; not recorded: this machine isn't registered: run `hushrun login` `` with a personal token, and `` hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>; not recorded: set `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY` to the key `hushrun token create --agent` printed `` with an agent token, [ADR 0046] `` hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>; not recorded: HUSHRUN_AGENT's key can't sign for its token: make a new string with `hushrun token create --agent` `` with `HUSHRUN_AGENT`, since no variable can replace the key inside it (or `failed in <time> (exit <code>)`); a skip that can't be recorded so says `hushrun: the skip wasn't recorded: <what to do>`;
- [ADR 0032] when the backend refuses the record for the org's plan (402 `quota_exceeded`, C-12), its last line is `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>; not recorded: <the server's message>; upgrade: <upgrade_url>` (or `failed in <time> (exit <code>)`), without `; upgrade: …` when the answer names no upgrade URL or the message names it already; the next run on this code finds no pass, and runs;
- [ADR 0044] in a CI job with several variants that doesn't say which one runs, [ADR 0049] or a pass that counts only in another variant or job, or a job it can't tell about (below), it runs whatever passed, records as usual, and says why in one line, `hushrun: <NAME> running: <why>`; its last line is `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>`, without saying it won't run again;
- [ADR 0047] in a CI job where a later step reads the check's reports (C-26), a pass that counts in the job doesn't skip it: it runs, records as usual, and says why in one line, `hushrun: <NAME> running: a later step reads its reports (<reader>), and a skip writes none`; its last line is `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>`, without saying it won't run again; [ADR 0048] unless this job's own pass wrote nothing those steps read, which narrows it (C-26);
- [ADR 0045] when this machine doesn't meet the check's rule (C-25), it runs and records nothing;
- with `--force`, it runs, and records.

[ADR 0044] **A job with several variants skips only on its own variant's pass.** One key stands for all of its variants (C-9), so [ADR 0049] each run says which variant it is, beside its `job` (C-10), and without that no pass skips a run in it, a laptop's included:

- **On GitHub Actions** hushrun reads the workflow file `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_REF` names, or [ADR 0049] the one `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` names (below), from the checkout, as YAML and never to write, and finds `jobs.<GITHUB_JOB>`. With no `strategy.matrix`, or one of exactly one variant, this rule says nothing: every key has one value, and no `include` item overwrites one or adds a variant of its own. With several variants, or a matrix that can't be counted, since an expression stands for it, for a key's values or anywhere in it, or an `include` item is one, `exclude` not counted, the run takes a variant from `HUSHRUN_VARIANT` when the job says which variant it is: [ADR 0049] the variable is set, the job's own `env` in the file sets it to `${{ toJSON(matrix) }}`, with spaces inside `${{ }}` and the case of `toJSON` and `matrix` ignored, and its value is a JSON object, or `null` read as `{}`, of at most 64 KiB. Then GitHub filled in this variant's values, which make the run's `variant`, and only a pass of this job and this variant skips it (C-10). So a matrix an expression builds works too. With a value that isn't JSON, no pass skips: `hushrun: <NAME> running: HUSHRUN_VARIANT isn't JSON, so hushrun can't tell this variant, and no pass skips it`. Without the variable, or with a job that sets it to anything else, no pass skips, unless its name differs per variant (below): `hushrun: <NAME> running: job <id> of <path> has several variants, and its HUSHRUN_VARIANT isn't ${{ toJSON(matrix) }}, so no pass skips it`. With both a name and the variable, the variable decides. init writes `HUSHRUN_VARIANT` (C-15).
- [ADR 0049] **A pass from outside CI never skips a variant.** A laptop or an agent's sandbox ran none of them, so in a job of several variants, a run with a `variant` takes no `local` pass, nor a generic CI's with no `job` (C-10). Outside CI, such a pass still skips.
- [ADR 0049] **A called workflow names its own file.** GitHub names the caller's file in `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_REF` and the called file's job in `GITHUB_JOB`, so a job of a workflow another calls sets `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` to its file's own path, and the run reads that file instead. It must be under `.github/workflows/` in this checkout, start on `workflow_call`, and its `jobs.<GITHUB_JOB>` must set `env.HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` to that very path; otherwise no pass skips: `hushrun: <NAME> running: HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW names <path>, whose job <id> doesn't name that file, so hushrun can't tell this job, and no pass skips it`, or that it names a file `which isn't under .github/workflows/` or `which can't be read here`.
