The run's variant is then over `{"inputs": …, "matrix": …}`: `HUSHRUN_INPUTS`, which a file that takes inputs (`on.workflow_call.inputs`) must set to `${{ toJSON(inputs) }}` as the matrix's is checked, and `HUSHRUN_VARIANT`, which a job with several variants must set, as above, each `null` when absent; with neither, the job is a plain one, and a pass from outside CI counts there. Either one the job's `env` sets counts whatever the file says of its variants, since it is what GitHub filled in; and either one this run has that the file's job doesn't set means the file isn't the one running, as a caller's checkout can hold a file at the same path when another repository's workflow is called: `hushrun: <NAME> running: <HUSHRUN_VARIANT or HUSHRUN_INPUTS> is set, and job <id> of <path> doesn't set it, so hushrun can't tell this job, and no pass skips it`. Otherwise no pass skips: `hushrun: <NAME> running: job <id> of <path> takes inputs, and its HUSHRUN_INPUTS isn't ${{ toJSON(inputs) }}, so no pass skips it`, or `HUSHRUN_INPUTS isn't JSON, so hushrun can't tell this call, and no pass skips it`. The inputs' values show nowhere: not in the label, the record, or any line. GitHub doesn't pass a caller's workflow-level `env` to a called workflow, so the value comes from the called file, and in nested calls the innermost job names its own.
- **When hushrun can't tell, no pass skips.** That is when the variables don't name a job, or a file of this repository (`GITHUB_REPOSITORY`, ignoring case) under `.github/workflows/`; the file can't be read, isn't UTF-8 or doesn't parse; `jobs.<GITHUB_JOB>` isn't there, or calls a reusable workflow; or a file a job of that one calls with a local `uses:`, or a file such a file calls, can't be read or holds a job with that id. So a reusable workflow's jobs without `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` don't skip in CI: `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_REF` names the caller, and `GITHUB_JOB` a job of the called file.
- **A name that differs per variant.** A `run:` of that job holds `hushrun run <NAME> -- …`, whose NAME holds `${{ strategy.job-index }}`, or `${{ matrix.<key> }}` for every key of the matrix, `include`'s too, and this run's name is one CI can make of it: the index fills in digits, and `${{ matrix.<key> }}` one of the values that key lists, `include`'s too, never nothing. A run counts by its name only when it is such a line: the command line gives NAME; no package.json script started it, so `npm_lifecycle_event` is unset, or `npx`, `bunx` or `hushrun`, what npx, bunx and yarn call a binary they start; no line of the job writes that very name; and no other `hushrun run` started it, which it knows by `HUSHRUN_INSIDE_RUN`, set on the command every `hushrun run` starts. So `test-1`, a script `test:1` or a run inside `test-${{ strategy.job-index }}`, counts by no name; the outer run's key tells variants apart, and its pass skips all of it. Only NAME counts: no other word reaches the key for sure, not `--scope`, COMMAND, a working directory or what follows a script. Any other expression can't be told, so a name that holds one matches no run. When any key of the matrix has an expression among its values, a value that isn't plain, or values only run time knows, no `${{ matrix.<key> }}` can be told either, and only the index is left. The index is a position: after a matrix edit, one index can mean another variant, with the key it had only when the workflow's file is outside the scope (D-7), which `hushrun doctor` warns about. [ADR 0049] So init writes `HUSHRUN_VARIANT` instead, whose values don't move.
- **On GitLab CI**, `CI_JOB_NAME` differs per `parallel` instance and `parallel:matrix` variant, so [ADR 0049] each instance is its own job: a CI pass counts for its own instance (C-10). `CI_NODE_TOTAL` above 1, or a `CI_JOB_NAME` that shows a matrix (`<name>: [a, b]`), gives the run a variant, from that name, its label the matrix's values or `<index>/<total>`, so that no pass from outside CI counts there. Nothing here reads `.gitlab-ci.yml`, and nothing needs to.

**Limits.** Nothing here looks at what a job says about where it runs, so these behave as C-9 says:

- Two workflow files with one job id on one system have one `job`, so they share a pass even when they install different tool versions. It is the trade C-9 accepts for a laptop's pass, and [ADR 0045] `counts_from` (C-25) narrows it: a run on a version the rule doesn't take records nothing. Without a rule, a different job id or check name keeps them apart.
- A local pass skips every job that isn't a job with variants, on whatever system: a laptop stands in for CI (C-9).
- Two runs of one check in one job have one `job`, even around a step that changes what they run on.
- A job of a reusable workflow that hushrun can't read, in another repository or called as `<owner>/<name>/…@<ref>`, without `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW`, reads as a plain job of the caller when it has that job's id, since CI names only the caller and the id: it skips as that job does, whatever variants it has. A call that can't be this job says nothing, so a plain job beside such a call keeps skipping.
- [ADR 0049] Two calls of one workflow with the same inputs are one variant, and share a pass; a call whose inputs change on every run, such as a time, never skips.
- [ADR 0049] `HUSHRUN_VARIANT`, `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` or `HUSHRUN_INPUTS` set for the whole workflow, or in a step, isn't the job's own `env`, and its job runs.
- A file a step starts isn't read, so a `hushrun run` in a shell script or a Makefile that writes out a NAME a variant could take, such as `test-1` beside `test-${{ strategy.job-index }}`, counts as that variant's.
- GitLab jobs of one name in two child pipelines have one `job`.
- hushrun reads the checkout's copy of the workflow, which isn't the one running when a job checks out other code, as `pull_request_target` can.
- A CI but GitHub Actions and GitLab CI names no job, so neither rule applies.
- `hushrun run CHECK…`, with no command, is unchanged: evidence decides there (P-1).
- A repository that gets hushrun from npm has these rules once its lockfile takes a release that has them.

It records nothing when the files changed while COMMAND ran (in the worktree; not with `--rev` or `--digest`), or [ADR 0037] an `external` input did: the result would be for neither version. A command that writes files needs them in `.gitignore`.

- **Exit codes.** COMMAND's (1 when it is outside 0–255), or 128 + N after signal N; 127 when COMMAND isn't found, and 126 when it can't start. A skip exits 0. Usage errors exit 2: no COMMAND, more than one NAME, no NAME when COMMAND's program gives none (C-9), or a flag of `hushrun run CHECK…`, such as `--all`.
- **Output.** hushrun's lines go to stderr and start with `hushrun: `; `-q` silences them. Stdout is COMMAND's.
- **Windows.** A bare command name is looked up as a shell would, in each `PATH` directory with `PATHEXT`'s `.com`, `.exe`, `.bat` and `.cmd`, so `vitest` finds npm's `node_modules\.bin\vitest.cmd`.

**C-15 Setting up.** `hushrun init` sets up `hushrun run` in the git repository it runs in, with one command: `npx hushrun init`. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`. `hushrun init --pipeline`, which `--force`, `--repository`, `--branch` and `--image` imply, writes a deploy pipeline's starter `hushrun.yaml` instead (§10.4): [ADR 0062] one `unit` check, one image at its `location`, and `deploy` from `--branch`, `main` by default; [ADR 0031] and no keys (whether the repository is CI-only is set in the dashboard, T-16); the setup's flags, `--yes`, `--no-browser`, `--dry-run`, `--run`, `--no-run` and [ADR 0041] `--org`, don't go with those (exit 2). Its steps, each printed as it happens:

1. **Look.** The repository root; its id, as run results name it (C-10); [ADR 0037] CI's files and what they run (below); the root's `package.json`; its package manager: `packageManager`'s name, else that of the first lockfile of `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `yarn.lock`, `bun.lock`, `bun.lockb` and `package-lock.json`, else npm; its checks, the `scripts` named `test`, `test:unit`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `type-check`, `types`, `check-types`, `check`, `format:check` or `tsc`; [ADR 0051] when the root heads a JavaScript workspace (**Workspaces**, below), each package's `package.json`, whose scripts are read as the root's; and the jobs of `.github/workflows/*.yml` and `*.yaml` whose steps run one of them through a package manager: `npm test`, `npm t`, `npm run X`, `npm run-script X`, `pnpm X`, `pnpm run X`, `yarn X`, `yarn run X` or `bun run X`, in a `run:` of one line or a block (`bun test` is bun's own test runner, not the script). A command after a `cd` elsewhere, in a step or a job whose `working-directory` (its own, or its job's or workflow's `defaults`) is another directory, or with an option before the script that picks another package (`--filter`, `-w`, `--prefix`, …), runs another package's script.
2. **What CI repeats** (C-16), in the background; and, for a repository on github.com, what GitHub says about it, for its ledger (step 6): `gh api --hostname github.com repos/<owner>/<name> --jq '[.id, .default_branch] | @tsv'`, through `gh`'s own sign-in, within 10 s: its id and its default branch, the branch optional; or none when `gh` is missing, signed out, can't see the repository or fails. The id lets the server name the repository's GitHub Actions as its CI (T-15).

A 401, or no orgs, signs in; a 404 or 405 keeps the backend `hushrun login` saved; any other answer, or none, exits as a sign-in that can't reach the host does (C-13), with nothing changed.   - Gradle: a `buildCache` whose block holds a `remote`, or that `.remote` follows, as `remote<HttpBuildCache>`, `remote(develocity.buildCache)` or `buildCache.remote(…)`, or the Develocity plugin, Gradle Enterprise's before it, applied directly or through a conventions plugin: a quoted string that holds `develocity`, `gradle.enterprise` or `gradle-enterprise`, as `com.gradle.develocity` and `io.spring.develocity.conventions` do; each comments aside, in the settings file (`settings.gradle.kts`, else `settings.gradle`) of its build: the nearest directory with one, from where Gradle starts, the line's directory or the one `-p` or `--project-dir` names inside the repository. Caching must be on: `org.gradle.caching=true` in the build's `gradle.properties`, then `--build-cache`, `--no-build-cache` and `-Dorg.gradle.caching=` on the command line, the last one winning. With caching on, the Develocity plugin counts as a remote cache whether or not it sets one up itself, as ADR 0050 says: a wrong guess only leaves the line to Gradle.
  - Bazel: `--remote_cache`, `--remote_executor` or `--bes_backend` with a value, on the command line, or on any line, for any command or config, of the `.bazelrc` of its workspace (the nearest directory with `MODULE.bazel`, `REPO.bazel`, `WORKSPACE.bazel` or `WORKSPACE`, from the line's, else the root), of what that file imports with `import` or `try-import`, three files deep, or of a file a startup `--bazelrc=` names.
  - Pants: `remote_cache_read = true` in `[GLOBAL]` of `pants.toml` or `pants.ci.toml`, `PANTS_REMOTE_CACHE_READ` set to a yes in CI's files, or `--remote-cache-read` on the command line.

  A package.json script that runs one of these through a cached tool, alone or in its `&&` chain, stays as it is, with the same reason, and its plan's `ciWhy` is `stepped-aside`. A Gradle check left so is still a check for **A rule**, since a person may wrap it.
- [ADR 0045] **A rule.** init writes one rule of C-25, `counts_from: {os: macos}`, for the checks of an Apple project: at the top of `hushrun.yaml` when the repository is one scope, and in a scope when it has several, whether init adds the scope or the file declares it, as a line of its own, every other byte kept. A wrong rule narrows where a pass counts and costs skips for ever, with nothing to say so, so init writes one only when all of this holds; otherwise it writes none and, but for the last point here, says nothing of one. A person can always add one.
  - This run wraps a check at that level that earns a rule: `xcodebuild` or `swift`, past a launcher, in a job whose `runs-on` is one `macos-*` runner, in any case: a string, or a list of one. `swiftlint` earns none, since it reads the same on any machine that has it, and may stand beside one that does. So a repository already set up gets none: a rule would change its checks' keys (C-9), and one someone removed stays removed. Running init again changes nothing.
  - Every check at that level is one of those three in such a job. A check here is any command the table of `cmd/setup/checks.rs` calls one, wrapped now or left with a reason (a command left today can go through hushrun tomorrow, and a rule would hold it to a Mac), JavaScript's included, which a package.json at the root keeps out of what init wraps, any `hushrun run` CI's files have already, as the command it starts, and [ADR 0050] a test script init suggests, in its directory, which never fits, since a person may wrap it. A workflow no push starts doesn't count, nor does a command the table doesn't know, which doesn't go through hushrun, so that a rule doesn't touch it, nor one it leaves for good, such as an advisory check or `fastlane scan`, which earn nothing and stop nothing. A `hushrun run` already there, whose scope is its own to name, or a check after a `cd` init can't follow, could be at any level: unless it fits, no level gets a rule.
  - So a job with any other check says no: Node on `macos-latest`, or `make test`, which starts anything. So does the same check on Linux or Windows, as `swift test` runs on those too. A `runs-on` with an expression says no, such as `${{ matrix.os }}`, and so does a list of labels or a group, which are a self-hosted runner's own words, and a workflow that doesn't parse. A matrix over anything else, with a plain `runs-on`, leaves the system as it says.
  - No job hands off what it runs: one with `uses:`, a workflow's, here or elsewhere, whose jobs aren't its to read, or a job off a Mac that uses a local action (`uses: ./…`), which can run `hushrun run` itself. Either could be at any level, so no level gets a rule.
  - No wrapped script of package.json is at that level: any of them at the top, and one that names `--scope` for a scope. CI's files don't say where a script runs or what it starts.
  - GitLab's jobs are never on a Mac as far as init can tell, so a check in `.gitlab-ci.yml` says no: a job's `tags` are whatever its runners' owner chose, and nothing in the file names a system.
  - `hushrun.yaml` writes no `counts_from` at any level, `anywhere` included, which stays exactly as written; has no `deploy`, which can't hold one (C-8), and about which init says nothing; and has no `checks`, which may name checks CI's files don't show.
  - `hushrun.yaml` can hold it. A rule goes with what step 5 writes, so it doesn't wait for a ledger: when nothing else writes the file, init writes one with `version`, `repository` and the rule, and step 6a adds what the org gives. A rule reads back from what init would write, or the plan doesn't promise it. With no id for `repository`, as with no `origin` remote, there is no file to hold one, since init makes up no id: with no package.json it wraps nothing, and with one it wraps CI's commands and says under `Change by hand` that `counts_from: {os: macos} fits, as these checks run on macOS in CI, with Xcode or Swift, but no origin remote names this repository, so init can't write hushrun.yaml: add it if a pass on another system shouldn't count`. A scope with no line to add it to, in flow style, says `… in scope <name> fits, … but hushrun.yaml has no line for it: …`.

  A project whose manifest is a `Package.swift`, or that has only an `.xcodeproj`, keeps the repository in one scope (Scopes, above), so a repository with one of those and a Go check gets no rule at the top. It gets one by declaring its scopes in `hushrun.yaml` before init runs, where a check left in another scope stays that scope's.
- **Without package.json**, or with no script to wrap, and no check in CI's files, init wraps nothing: it says how to put `hushrun run NAME --` in front of a check, [ADR 0045] or, when it found checks and could wrap none of them, lists each with its reason, and still signs in, connects the repository, and [ADR 0036] sets agents up (C-24). [ADR 0037] Without package.json it writes `backend` in `hushrun.yaml`, and without an `origin` remote to give that file its `repository`, it says to set `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` instead. It says that each clone runs `hushrun hook install`, and how to install hushrun when it isn't on `PATH`.
- [ADR 0041] **`--org <slug>`** names one of the person's orgs (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,38}$`, else exit 2). `--org` for an org `GET {base}/v0` refuses exits 2, saying which orgs are theirs when the backend lists them, with nothing changed. In a repository whose package.json or `hushrun.yaml` names another org, it **moves the repository**: after step 3, it asks for the ledger with `dry_run`, and a 403 or 409 exits 2 with the backend's reason; it shows what changes and asks `Move it? [Y/n]`, unless `--yes` (without a terminal and without `--yes` it exits 2 before signing in; `--dry-run` says that it would). It then gets the ledger and connects the repository as step 6 does, in the new org, where a refusal, no answer, or a ledger with no URL exits 6 with no file changed; and only then writes: in `hushrun.yaml`, `backend:` when it names one and, when `repository_url` is the old org's (it has `/l/<old org>/`), the URL and log key the new org gives (step 6a), every other line kept; then `hushrun.backend` in package.json, when it names one. A backend that isn't an org's URL (`…/o/<org>`), a `hushrun.yaml` that doesn't parse or no `origin` remote exits 2 before anything else, `--dry-run` included. The old org keeps what it recorded. Without `--org`, init never moves a repository.
- **A repository already set up**, whose package.json or [ADR 0037] `hushrun.yaml` names a backend, and whose package.json wraps a script or whose CI's files run `hushrun run`, signs in, with that backend's org as the hint, and gets the repository's ledger as step 6 does, `gh`'s id included, without asking: it connects nothing, and says the repository already uses hushrun. When `hushrun.yaml` would change (step 6a), or [ADR 0036] agents lack anything of C-24, or [ADR 0040] a GitHub workflow runs itself a command a wrapped script runs, which is all it wraps in CI's files there, it shows those lines and asks `Write it? [Y/n]`, or writes them with `--yes`; without a terminal and without `--yes` it says to run `hushrun init --yes`. With `--dry-run`, it says that it would sign in and get the ledger, and what it would write, and does none of it.
- [ADR 0075] **CI's cache step for what test files read.** When a check of `hushrun.yaml` has `tests` `observe` or `reuse`, and init talks to the repository's backend (signed in, as a member: a first setup, or a repository already set up), it asks `POST {base}/v0/units/hints/lookup` (C-12) once, at a place no check has. When the backend answers 404 or 405, it keeps no hints (C-38), and a CI runner starts each run with no list of what test files read: init's `Change by hand` then says the cache step a CI job needs, and edits no CI file for it. For GitHub Actions: `GitHub Actions: in each job that runs a check with `tests` on, add a step `uses: actions/cache@v4` before hushrun's, with `path: ~/.cache/hushrun/units/*.sqlite` (on macOS `~/Library/Caches/hushrun/units/*.sqlite`, on Windows `~\AppData\Local\hushrun\units\*.sqlite`), `key: hushrun-units-${{ runner.os }}-${{ github.sha }}` and `restore-keys: hushrun-units-${{ runner.os }}-`, so that its runs keep what their test files read between them: its backend doesn't keep that for other machines`; for GitLab, whose cache holds paths below the project only, `GitLab CI: in each job that runs a check with `tests` on, set `HUSHRUN_CACHE_DIR: .hushrun-cache` in its `variables` and add `cache: {key: hushrun-units-$CI_RUNNER_EXECUTABLE_ARCH, paths: [.hushrun-cache/units/*.sqlite]}`, with `.hushrun-cache/` in .gitignore, …` with the same end. Each key holds the runner's system, since lists are per system (C-38). Only the store travels, [ADR 0077, amended] `units/*.sqlite`, which names a store's file (C-36), of this schema or an older one a run imports from, without its schema so that the next one needs no edit, and nothing else of `units/`: never the outbox of suspensions or the local reuse state (C-34), since a cache is restored by its key's prefix, and an old one would bring back a suspension a person had since resumed. One file is enough: every write to the store ends by checkpointing its WAL into it (C-36), so a run that ends leaves it whole there. GitHub's for a repository with GitHub workflows, GitLab's for one with a GitLab pipeline, both for one with neither. Nothing where the backend keeps hints (they carry the same lists, to more machines) or can't tell; nothing with `--local` or without a backend, where CI keeps no results (ADR 0067), so the step would spare nothing; and `--dry-run` asks nothing, and says nothing of it.
- [ADR 0067] **`--local`: this machine only.** init's plan with no org in it: the same wraps of package.json's scripts and CI's files, the same files of C-24, the hook, the package and, for scopes or a rule, hushrun.yaml; and no sign-in, no `"hushrun": {"backend"}` and no hushrun.yaml `backend`, no `id-token: write` for CI's jobs, no GitLab token to make, no machine key, and no request to a server or to `gh`. The plan opens with `Results stay on this machine (--local): init signs in to nothing, names no backend and gives CI's jobs no ID token, so CI runs every check as before.`, which is also the first of the JSON plan's `notes`, and it ends with `Commit these changes. From now on a check skips here when this exact code already passed here, and hushrun ready and the pre-push hook go by what passed here. To share results with CI and teammates, run hushrun init again, without --local.` Without a terminal it needs `--yes`, with `run hushrun init --local --yes to apply`. It doesn't go with `--org` or the pipeline's flags (exit 2), and in a repository whose files name a backend it exits 2, saying that the repository shares its results already and that `hushrun login` signs in to it. A plain `hushrun init` later takes the repository as one that names no backend: it signs in, adds the backend and the ID tokens, and wraps nothing twice.
- [ADR 0067] **The one question.** At a terminal on stdin, without `--local`, `--yes`, `--dry-run` or `--org`, with no login saved for the origin, no agent set (C-17) and a repository that names no backend, init asks, after what it found and before anything else, `Share results with CI and teammates (signs in), or keep them on this machine only? [S/l]`. An answer that starts with `l` is `--local`; Enter, or anything else, goes on to step 3.
- **Without a terminal** on stdin, init applies nothing without `--yes`: it shows the plan and exits 2 with `run hushrun init --yes to apply`. `--dry-run` shows the plan and changes nothing: no sign-in, no file, no install, no request to `gh`, and no request to a server but [ADR 0041] the read of a saved login's orgs (C-13), when the repository names no org, within 10 s. Before a sign-in, the plan shows the backend as the one saved for the origin, or as a placeholder.
- [ADR 0049] **Each variant and each call says which it is,** in the same pass as the wraps, in each GitHub Actions job that runs a check through hushrun: a line init wraps, a wrapped script, or a line through hushrun already. A job with several variants (C-14) gets `HUSHRUN_VARIANT: ${{ toJSON(matrix) }}` in its own `env`, and the text says `Each variant of <job> gets its own pass (HUSHRUN_VARIANT in its env).` A workflow of this repository that a running one calls with `uses: ./<file>`, or that such a file calls, is read as running: each such job gets `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW: <the file's own path>`, and `HUSHRUN_INPUTS: ${{ toJSON(inputs) }}` when the file takes inputs, and the text says `<file> is called by <callers>: its jobs say which file they are, so CI can skip there too.` A job that calls a file whose jobs need an ID token gets `id-token: write` too, since a called workflow has no more permissions than its caller gives it. The entries go first in the job's `env` block, or in a new `env:` after its `runs-on`, with a minimal edit (C-15). A value that is right stays as it is, spaces and case included, so that a second init changes nothing; one set otherwise stays too, and the notes say `job <id>: its env sets <NAME> to <value>: make it <what init writes>, or no pass skips it there`. An `env` init can't edit as lines gets the line to add in the notes: `its env is written on one line: add <line> to it`, `its env is shared through <anchor or alias>: add <line> to it`, or `its env comes through <<, and an env of its own would replace it: add <line> to it`. A repository set up before this gets them from `hushrun init` again.
- **The plan as JSON.** `--dry-run` with `--format json`, or `HUSHRUN_FORMAT=json`, prints one JSON document, `hushrun.init-plan/v1` (`docs/schemas/init-plan.v1.schema.json`), in place of all of its text, and changes, asks and sends exactly what the text's dry run does. It holds the repository's id; its scopes; package.json's scripts, wrapped (before and after), through hushrun already, and left with the reason; each CI job that runs a check, with its file, id, provider, `variants` (`one`, `several`, or `unknown` for a file that doesn't read as YAML), `variantIdentity` (`named` when its `hushrun run` names differ per variant, or [ADR 0049] GitLab names each instance; `env` when its own `env`, as init leaves it, says which run it is, `HUSHRUN_VARIANT` in a job of several variants and `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` in a job of a workflow another calls, which decides over a name; else `none`), the jobs of this repository's workflows that call its file (`calledBy`), whether init installs hushrun in it (`setup`: `added`, `present` or `none`), each command init wraps, leaves or finds through hushrun already, with its line, directory, check and status, the wrapped scripts it runs, and [ADR 0047] the steps that read reports (`readers`, C-26), each with its place, name, kinds, paths and whether it runs only on failure; [ADR 0050] the test scripts a person could also wrap (`suggestions`), each with its file, job, line, directory, check, command, the line to use (`after`), whether its job has hushrun once init is done (`setup`, as a job's) and why init leaves it; each file init writes from what it finds, as a unified diff that `git apply` takes, the org's URL a placeholder before a sign-in; the files for agents (C-24), listed; and the notes of its text. What the org and hushrun.yaml get once init signs in comes from the org, so it isn't in the diffs: the notes give it as `<heading>: <line>`. When init can't edit CI's files, as with a hushrun.yaml that doesn't parse or no origin remote to name the repository, each command it would wrap is left, and its `why` says so. For each wrapped command and script, `ci` says what `hushrun run` decides in that job with a pass of that job on record: `skips`, or `runs` with `ciWhy` (`variants`, `reusable`, `cant-tell`, `reports`) and `ciText`, what the run says after `<NAME> running: `. It is computed by the run's own rule (C-14), for an environment like the job's, against the files as init would leave them: [ADR 0049] the job's own `env` there gives the run `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` as written, and `HUSHRUN_VARIANT` and `HUSHRUN_INPUTS` filled in, since their values are run time's; a reusable workflow's job is named by the workflow of this repository that starts the run, its top caller (the first, when several do); a wrapped script that a step's own `hushrun run` starts is a run inside that one; a script gets its `npm_lifecycle_event`. Where the run's words can't be known here, `ciText` is null: a reusable workflow that only another repository's workflow, or none of this one's, starts predicts `runs` with `reusable`; a step outside this repository's checkout, as in a job that checks nothing out with `actions/checkout`, predicts `runs` with `cant-tell`, and `why` says why. A workflow can change after init, so this predicts, and the run decides from the file it runs in. [ADR 0050] A command init leaves to its tool's remote cache has `ciWhy: stepped-aside`, with `ci` and `ciText` null, and so does a script of `scripts.left` it leaves so; any other command it leaves has a `ciWhy` of null, and any other script has none. The text lists CI's commands init leaves only when it wraps one of CI's commands, or no script; the JSON lists them all. Fields are only ever added. Without `--dry-run`, `--format json` changes nothing of init.
- **Exit codes.** 0 when done, when there is nothing to do, and when the person answers no; 2 for usage errors (outside a git repository, a package.json that isn't a JSON object, flags that don't go together, no terminal without `--yes`, [ADR 0041] several orgs and no way to tell which, an org that isn't the person's, a move the new org refuses); 1 when a file can't be written, or when the install fails, once the repository is connected and its ledger asked for; a sign-in that fails exits as `hushrun login` does (C-13).

**C-16 What CI repeats.** While the sign-in waits, init estimates how often CI ran again on code it had already passed. It is a best effort: when the repository is on github.com and `gh`, the GitHub CLI, is installed and signed in, init lists the repository's workflow runs created in the last 30 days (`gh api --paginate`, 100 a page, the newest 1,000 at most) within 10 s of starting, through `gh`'s own sign-in: no token goes through hushrun. It groups the runs by workflow and by `head_commit.tree_id`, the tree of the commit, which is the same for the same files, and counts each completed run, whatever its conclusion, that started after a run of the same workflow on the same tree had passed (its `updated_at`); their time is `updated_at` − `run_started_at`. It prints `Last 30 days, CI ran 96 times on code it had already passed: about 11 h.`, with `once` for one run, the time in hours, in minutes or as `under a minute`, and, after 1,000 runs, the days back to the oldest of them; or nothing at all: when no run repeated, and when `gh` is missing, signed out or failing, or the 10 s run out. It never fails init, and `--dry-run` doesn't run it.

**C-17 Agent tokens, and who hushrun is.** Agents in sandboxes, and CI outside GitHub, record results with an agent token (`hra_…`), which an org admin creates (ADR 0027), and [ADR 0028 amendment 1] the key it came with. [ADR 0046] A sandbox gets both, and the backend, in one variable, `HUSHRUN_AGENT`; CI gets the token in `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` and the key in `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY` or `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE`, as a sandbox set up by hand may. They need no `hushrun.yaml`. [ADR 0028 amendment 1] Every token comes with the agent's machine key (C-18), which signs the agent's results, and with which a pipeline outside GitHub, such as GitLab's, also records a repository's events, signed as the agent (T-12, L-10).

- **`hushrun token create --agent NAME [--repository REPO | --org-wide] [--expires DURATION] [--rate N] [--backend URL]`** asks the org's backend for one (`POST {base}/v0/agent-tokens`, C-12). NAME is a name as L-2 says; its results record as `agent:NAME`. The token is for REPO, by default the repository hushrun runs in (the CI provider's, else `hushrun.yaml`'s `repository`, else the `origin` remote's), or for every repository of the org with `--org-wide`; outside a repository, without either, it exits 2. DURATION is whole hours, 1 h to 365 d, such as `12h`, `30d` or `8w`, by default `30d`. `--rate` is the most results it records a minute, 1 to 10,000, by default the server's. It prints the token alone on stdout, and on stderr what it is and the environment to give the sandbox. [ADR 0046] When stdout and stderr are terminals, outside CI, stderr's first line is `HUSHRUN_AGENT=<the string>`, all a sandbox needs; otherwise the string isn't printed, and a line says that `--format json` gives it. Then come the token and the key as variables of their own, for CI outside GitHub and sandboxes set up by hand: `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` (the token itself when the string shows, else `<the token on stdout>`), `HUSHRUN_PROVIDER=local` for a sandbox that sets `CI=true`, and `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` when the backend isn't on hushrun's host, since the token follows the repository's backend only there (C-13). [ADR 0028 amendment 1] It also makes the agent's key, sends its public half with the request (`machine`, C-12), and shows the key once, after those variables: `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY`, the whole PEM block (C-18). [ADR 0046] It shows the key only when stderr is a terminal, outside CI, where it can't reach a log; otherwise it says that the token records nothing without it, to revoke it, and that `--format json` gives the key. With `--format json`, stdout is `hushrun.token/v1` (`docs/schemas/token.v1.schema.json`), with the token, the key and the string as `hushrun_agent`.
- [ADR 0046] **`HUSHRUN_AGENT`** is a single line: `hushrun_agent_v1_`, then the base64url without padding (RFC 4648 §5) of the UTF-8 JSON object `{"backend": "<the org's URL>", "key": "<the agent's PKCS#8 PEM>", "token": "hra_…"}`. Readers ignore other fields, and spaces, line breaks and quotes around the string. The backend must be `https://`, or `http://` on a loopback host (L-1).
  - **It stands in for three variables and more.** Set, with no `HUSHRUN_PROVIDER`, it overrides bare `CI=true`, and the run is `local` (§11.2); a named CI's own variable (`GITHUB_ACTIONS`, `GITLAB_CI`, `BUILDKITE`, `CIRCLECI`, and [ADR 0061] those of the CI systems §11.2 runs as generic) beats it, as does an explicit `HUSHRUN_PROVIDER`, so a job a secret reaches keeps C-10's and C-14's guards. Its backend counts as named by the environment (C-11), its token goes with it (C-13), and its key signs as the agent's (C-18).
  - **It wins, whole.** `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY`, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE` and a different `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` beside a string hushrun can read are ignored, and it says so in one line: `hushrun: HUSHRUN_AGENT wins over <names>: it holds the backend, the token and the key together`. `-q` silences it, and a `hushrun run` inside another doesn't repeat it. Only an empty `HUSHRUN_BACKEND` stays: it turns results off (C-11).
  - **A string hushrun can't read** is ignored, as §10.1 says, in a line that says what's wrong and holds none of it, such as `hushrun: HUSHRUN_AGENT is damaged: part of it is missing, or something was added, such as a line break, so it's ignored`; `hushrun doctor` fails its `agent` check with the same words, or when a string reads and its key can't sign.
  - **What is a string.** Wherever hushrun looks for one, it is `hushrun_agent_v<digits>_`, or `eyJiYWNrZW5kIjoi`, the base64url of `{"backend":"` that a string starts with without its prefix, anywhere in a value, with at least 48 characters of base64url after the prefix. A value that only names one, such as `hushrun_agent_notes.txt`, isn't. This stops mistakes, not someone who sets out to get past it.
  - **A string in another variable.** The value of any `HUSHRUN_*` variable but `HUSHRUN_AGENT` that holds a string is dropped, and a line names the variable: `hushrun: <name> holds a HUSHRUN_AGENT string, which is never sent: set it as HUSHRUN_AGENT, so it's ignored`.
  - **An agent is set** when `HUSHRUN_AGENT` holds anything, readable or not, or a string was dropped from another variable. It overrides bare `CI=true` as a good string does, and it isn't a person: signing in (C-13) and `hushrun token` exit 2, the pre-push hook takes the pusher for an agent (§7.8), and every login reads as signed out, for every request, `hushrun init`'s included (C-15). With no readable string, a personal token in `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` and the key beside the credentials (C-18) stay out too, so results aren't recorded.
  - **The key stays in the process.** It reaches no header, body, argument, log or message, and only `token create` prints the string or the key. A command line that holds a string, in a flag, an argument or a run's COMMAND, exits 2 before anything runs: it says so, and quotes nothing. `hushrun` never adds to a command's environment what the string holds: the command `hushrun run` starts inherits `HUSHRUN_AGENT` as it inherits any variable, which a `hushrun run` inside it needs, and no `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` or `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY`. An isolated run never passes it in (E-12).
- [ADR 0046] **`hushrun token`** exits 2 with an agent set (below): agent tokens are an org admin's.
- **`hushrun token list [--backend URL]`** lists the org's agent tokens that aren't revoked: prefix, agent, repository or `whole org`, when it expires (`expired` once past), when it was last used and who created it.
- **`hushrun token revoke TOKEN [--backend URL]`** revokes one (`DELETE {base}/v0/agent-tokens/{id}`): TOKEN is its id, its prefix, the token itself, or its agent's name when only one token has it; a name more tokens have exits 2 and lists them.
- **The backend** of `hushrun token` is `--backend`; else `HUSHRUN_BACKEND`; else the repository's (C-11); else the one `hushrun login` saved. It must be an org's URL (`…/o/<org>`), or hushrun exits 2. Requests carry the personal token `hushrun login` saved for its origin, never `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`: without a login there, hushrun exits 2 and says to run `hushrun login`. A refusal shows the server's message: 400 exits 2, 401, 403 and 404 exit 1, a server that can't be reached or fails (5xx) exits 6.
- **`hushrun whoami`** shows where `hushrun run` records results here and what named the backend (C-11); the credential that goes there (C-13): `HUSHRUN_AGENT`, `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`, an agent token or not, the login's token and its email, a GitHub Actions ID token, or none, and why `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` is withheld when it is; and who the backend says it is, from `GET {base}/v0`: `as`, the org, and for an agent token its agent, prefix, repository, expiry and limit. Results on this machine, or off, it says so; [ADR 0067] kept here because nobody is signed in to the repository's backend (C-11), it says which, and to sign in, and the JSON's `signed_out` is that URL, with `named_by` the file that names it. It exits 0 when the backend answered, 1 when it refused the credential, and 6 when it couldn't be reached. With `--format json`, stdout is `hushrun.whoami/v1` (`docs/schemas/whoami.v1.schema.json`).
- **`hushrun doctor`** shows the same as its first check after git and CI, `results`, before it reads `hushrun.yaml`: the backend, `as`, the credential and, for an agent token, its repository and expiry; a warning when the backend refuses or can't be reached. [ADR 0046] Its `ci` check says when `HUSHRUN_AGENT` made the run local, and its `agent` check, there only with an agent set, fails on a string it can't read, or whose key can't sign.

**C-18 Machines.** [ADR 0028] Every sign-in registers the machine it runs on, every agent token comes with its machine, and the server takes a person's or an agent's writes, results included, only from a registered machine. [ADR 0031] A registered machine of a current member, or of the org's agent token, is what makes its events count (T-15, E-8).

- **The key.** One ECDSA P-256 key (E-2) per machine, made the first time it signs in: `<directory of the credentials file>/machine-key.pem` (C-13), PKCS#8 PEM, written through a temporary file and a rename, readable by its owner only (0600 on Unix). `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE` names another path, and `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY` holds the PEM itself, for sandboxes that keep no files; it wins, and [ADR 0046] the key in `HUSHRUN_AGENT` wins over both (C-17). A key path inside the repository hushrun runs in is refused (exit 2). Every sign-in reuses it.
- [ADR 0041] **A machine is registered per org.** A sign-in registers it in the org it was approved for, and `hushrun init` for the org it works with, or the first write of a result to any other org, by a person's token, registers it there with `POST {base}/v0/machines`, once per token and org.
- **Registration at sign-in** is C-13's `machine` and `proof`. A server that answers no `machine` doesn't register machines; hushrun signs in all the same. [ADR 0028 amendment 1] A server may refuse to register one, and with it the sign-in (C-13): hushrun cloud takes at most 20 live machines per person per org (409 `machine_limit`), and keeps revoked for good a machine its owner or an admin revoked in the dashboard, or that went with its owner's membership (409 `machine_revoked`). Signing in again brings back a machine it revoked for going unused, or one `hushrun logout` revoked.
- **Registration later.** A personal token whose sign-in registered no machine registers this machine with its first result: `POST {base}/v0/machines` `{"key", "name", "proof"}`, the proof over `hushrun-machine/v1\n<hex of SHA-256 of the token>\n<keyid>\n`. 201 or 200 registers; 404 or 405 means the server doesn't register machines, and hushrun signs its results all the same, which such a server ignores. hushrun remembers either in `<cache dir>/machines/`, so a machine registers once per token. [ADR 0028 amendment 1] Any other answer, such as 409 `machine_limit`, leaves the result unrecorded, with the server's message (C-14). An agent token never registers a machine: its one machine is the key it came with (below).
- **Results.** [ADR 0028 amendment 1] With a personal or an agent token, every `POST {base}/v0/results/{key}` carries `Hushrun-Signature: <keyid> <base64 signature>`, by the machine key, over `hushrun-result/v1\n<key>\n<hex of SHA-256 of the body>\n`. A person signs with this machine's key, [ADR 0060] and in CI, with a personal `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`, only with the key the job names, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY` or `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE`, never the one a sign-in left on the runner, as ledger events don't (T-12): without one, `` not recorded: in CI, a personal token's results are signed with the key the job names: set HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY or HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE ``; an agent only with the key the environment names, `HUSHRUN_AGENT`'s, `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY` or `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY_FILE`, never the key file beside the credentials, which is a person's. Without a key, hushrun doesn't send the result (C-14). A server answers a person's or an agent's result without the header with 401 `machine_required`, and one signed by a key that isn't a registered machine of the token's owner with 401 `machine_unknown`, and records neither; hushrun says how to fix either (C-14). An ID token never signs.
- **Ledger events.** A machine signs events with its key by default (T-12, `HUSHRUN_SIGNING=machine`), unless a GitHub Actions job's ID token signs them: a person's machine, and in CI an agent's, such as a GitLab pipeline's with the key its agent token came with. A ledger on hushrun's host gets an agent token, in CI too, [ADR 0046] one on the origin of `HUSHRUN_AGENT`'s backend gets the token in it, and outside CI a ledger on the login's origin gets the login's token (L-10). The server writes such an event only when a registered machine of the token's owner signed it.
- **The agent's key** (C-17). [ADR 0028 amendment 1] `hushrun token create` makes a P-256 key for the agent, sends its public half with the request that makes the token, as `machine` with a proof (C-12), and the server registers it as the token's one machine, `agent:<NAME>`. hushrun shows the key once: [ADR 0046] at a terminal, outside CI (C-17), on stderr, to set as `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY`, and inside the string; with `--format json`, in `hushrun_agent` and as `machine_key` (`pem`, `public_key`, `keyid`), beside the server's `machine`. Every sandbox of the agent signs as that machine. hushrun cloud's dashboard makes the same in the browser, with an Ed25519 key, and shows the same string.
- **Unused machines.** [ADR 0028 amendment 1] hushrun cloud revokes a person's machine that went 90 days without a sign-in, a signed result or a signed write (results-v0 §6). Its results then get 401 `machine_unknown`, and hushrun says to run `hushrun login` (C-14), which brings it back. [ADR 0058] It revokes a personal token unused for 90 days too, with its machines, and revoking one's machine in the dashboard revokes every token whose sign-in proved its key; either way the token's requests get 401, and hushrun says to run `hushrun login`.
- **`hushrun whoami`** shows the machine's key id and where its key is (`machine` in `hushrun.whoami/v1`).

**C-19 Usage [ADR 0032].** `hushrun repository usage` shows the org's plan and what it recorded this period, from the backend `hushrun run` records to here (C-11), with the credential it sends there (C-13): `GET {base}/v0/usage` (C-12), and no other request. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`.

- **What it shows.** The backend and what named it, as `whoami` does; the org; the plan (`plan_name`, else `plan`); the period, its end with how far off it is; the checks recorded, of those the plan includes; the limit, and once it is reached that checks still run but their results aren't recorded until the period ends or the org upgrades; the overage and its cost, when there is one; this repository's share, the checks of the `repositories` entries whose repository is, ignoring case, the one `hushrun run`'s results name here (C-10: the CI provider's, else `hushrun.yaml`'s, else the `origin` remote's), with its percent of the org's; when the number is above zero, the checks recorded for free, outside the plan: this repository's, the `free` entries matched the same way, or the org's total outside a repository; and the dashboard.
- **Nothing metered.** Results on this machine are metered nowhere, and it says so (`results stay on this machine, so nothing is metered`); results off, it says why; a server that answers 404 or 405 doesn't report usage, and it says so.
- **Output.** With `--format json`, stdout is `hushrun.repository.usage/v1` (`docs/schemas/repository-usage.v1.schema.json`): `backend`, `named_by`, `directory` and `off` as `whoami`'s, `repository`, and `usage`, the server's answer with `repository_checks`, or null when nothing is metered or the server doesn't report usage. `usage.free` is there only when the server sent it.
- **Exit codes.** 0 when the server answered, when nothing is metered and when the server doesn't report usage; 6, with the server's message, when it can't be reached, refuses the credential (401, 403), or answers anything else, such as a body without `used`.

### 7.7 Updates [ADR 0035]

hushrun's releases are GitHub Releases of the public repository `hushrun/hushrun` (ADR 0034). The newest release is the one `https://github.com/hushrun/hushrun/releases/latest` redirects to, `…/releases/tag/v<version>`: GitHub's latest release, never a pre-release. hushrun reads that redirect rather than follow it, and then takes everything from that tag, so that an archive and its checksum come from one release. `HUSHRUN_INSTALLER_GITHUB_BASE_URL`, the variable hushrun's installers take, replaces `https://github.com` for a mirror or a test's server: an `https://` URL, or `http://` on a loopback address; another is a usage error. Requests carry no credential, and `User-Agent: hushrun/<version>`.

**C-20 Updating.** `hushrun update` replaces the running hushrun with the newest release when hushrun's installer installed it. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`.

- **The newest already.** When this hushrun is the newest release, it prints `hushrun <version> is the newest release.`, and when it is newer, as a pre-release or a build from source can be, `hushrun <version> is newer than the newest release, <newest>.`; it exits 0 and downloads nothing.
- **The installer's installs.** dist's installers leave a receipt, `hushrun-receipt.json`, in `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/hushrun`, else `~/.config/hushrun`; on Windows in `%XDG_CONFIG_HOME%\hushrun`, else `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hushrun`. hushrun updates the binary only when a receipt whose `source` is `hushrun/hushrun`'s and whose `binaries` hold `hushrun` (`hushrun.exe`) names the running binary: `<install_prefix>/bin/` for the `cargo-home` and `hierarchical` layouts, `<install_prefix>/` for `flat`, either for a receipt without a layout, compared as canonical paths.
- **Replacing it.** hushrun downloads the release's `hushrun-<target>.tar.xz` (`.zip` on Windows) for its own target, and `<archive>.sha256`, from `{base}/hushrun/hushrun/releases/download/v<version>/`. It follows redirects there, to `https://` only, and never prints their URLs, which are signed. The checksum file must name the archive, and the archive must have that SHA-256. It unpacks the archive with the system's `tar` (Windows' own `tar.exe` on Windows), in a directory it makes beside the binary, and the binary it finds there must print `hushrun <version>` for `--version`. Then a rename puts it in place of the running one; on Windows, the running `hushrun.exe` is renamed `hushrun.exe.old` first, which the next update removes, and when the new one then can't take its name, `hushrun.exe.old` is renamed back. [ADR 0061] When that fails too, the error doesn't say hushrun is unchanged: it says `hushrun.exe.old` is now the only hushrun there, and to rename it back. Last, the receipt's `version` becomes the new version, its other fields as they were. It prints `Updated hushrun <old> to <new> in <path>.` and the release's page; on stderr, unless `-q`, which release it downloads.
- [ADR 0043] **What init installs.** A release init installs (C-24) leaves such a receipt, with `install_layout` `flat`, so it counts as the installer's.
- **Other installs.** A binary in a `node_modules` directory came with npm, pnpm, Yarn or Bun; any other the installer's receipts don't name came another way: built from source, copied by hand, installed by CI. hushrun changes nothing, and says how to update it: with the package manager, or the way it was installed, or with the installer, or by running the binary a receipt names.
- **Exit codes.** 0 when it updated, or there was nothing to update; 2 for another install, and when `HUSHRUN_INSTALLER_GITHUB_BASE_URL` isn't a URL hushrun reads releases from; 1 when it can't tell which release is the newest, a download fails, the checksum doesn't match, the archive holds no binary that is the release, or the binary can't be replaced. Before the rename, a failure leaves hushrun as it was.

**C-21 The newer-release notice.** At most once a day, hushrun says on stderr, after a command's own output, ``hushrun <newest> is available: run `hushrun update` `` when the newest release is newer than itself. [ADR 0061] A hushrun in a `node_modules` directory, which `hushrun update` leaves alone (C-20), says `hushrun <newest> is available: update the hushrun package with the package manager that installed it, such as npm install hushrun@latest` instead.

- **Only for people.** Only when stderr is a terminal, the output format is `text`, and `-q` isn't given; never in CI, [ADR 0061] as §11.2 detects it everywhere in hushrun, nor where `CI` is set to anything but nothing, `false` or `0`, as an agent's sandbox may be; never from `hushrun update`; and never when `HUSHRUN_NO_UPDATE_CHECK` is set to anything but nothing, `false`, `0`, `no` or `off`. Unlike other booleans (§10.1), a value hushrun can't read turns it off, without a warning.
- [ADR 0065] **And for agents.** When the environment says a coding agent runs hushrun, by C-23's test of who is an agent, stderr needn't be a terminal, and `CI` set with no other sign of CI (C-17) doesn't silence it. The agent is told in words of its own, ``hushrun: hushrun <newest> is available, and this is <this>: when you report back, tell the person, who updates it with `hushrun update` ``, or, from a `node_modules` directory, `… who updates the hushrun package with the package manager that installed it, such as npm install hushrun@latest`. The rest of this rule holds as for people: one record, so an agent and a person on one machine are told once a day between them.
- **Asking.** hushrun asks for the newest release on a thread of its own while the command runs, with a 3 s timeout, when a day has passed since GitHub last answered and an hour since hushrun last asked. It records that it asked before asking, and doesn't ask when it can't record it.
- **Never in the way.** After the command, hushrun waits at most 200 ms for the answer. Whatever happens, the command's output and exit code are what they would have been: an error, a timeout or a damaged record only means no notice.
- **Its record** is `update-check.json` in hushrun's cache directory (§12.6): when hushrun last asked, when GitHub last answered and which release it named, and when hushrun last said so, in seconds since 1970, with a `format` of 1. A file of another format, or one hushrun can't read, is a fresh start. With `HUSHRUN_CACHE_DIR` set to nothing, there is no record, and hushrun doesn't ask.

### 7.8 Before a push [ADR 0036]

**C-22 `hushrun ready`.** `hushrun ready` says what CI would still run on this exact code, and the commands that run it here first. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`.

- **What it looks at.** HEAD; `--rev`, a revision; or `--worktree`, the files as `git add -A && git commit` would record them (D-10). Evidence and results are about content, so a pass from before a commit counts for it.
- **Which checks.** [ADR 0062] With `deploy` in `hushrun.yaml`: the checks it requires (`Policy::required`, C-7), as `run --all` takes them (§10.4), for each scope `--scope` or `--all-scopes` selects, every scope by default; a file without `deploy` adds none of its own. The branch is `--branch`'s, else the current one. And from package.json at that revision, each `hushrun run` of a script, one at a time, when CI's workflows run any of those scripts, of the scripts they run; else of every script. [ADR 0044] A workflow runs what the scripts it starts start, through `npm run` and the like and past a `hushrun run … --`, unless CI fills in that run's NAME: such a run has a key of its own in each variant, and what it starts is its business, so a script only such a run starts doesn't count. [ADR 0037] And each `hushrun run` of CI's own files at that revision, `.github/workflows/*.yml` and `*.yaml`, and `.gitlab-ci.yml`, in a language any: a run CI starts twice counts once.
- [ADR 0037] **Which workflows.** Those a push of the branch starts: `push`, unless `branches` or `branches-ignore` say otherwise, or a push of tags only; or `pull_request`. A workflow that starts for neither says nothing: not one on a schedule, by hand, or for a release. One that `paths` or `paths-ignore` filter stays with CI.
- **Reading a script.** A script counts only when its words are sure: commands joined by `&&`, each `hushrun run`, or that started by `npx`, `npx --no-install`, `npx --no`, `bunx`, `yarn`, `yarn exec`, `pnpm exec` or `npm exec`, with at most a NAME and [ADR 0037] `--scope` before `--`, and a COMMAND with no `$` or backtick and, outside quotes, no `*`, `?`, `[`, `{`, `%` or leading `~`. Other commands of the chain aren't hushrun's, and don't count. A script it can't read for sure is left out: it can't tell the script's key (C-9). Its `cwd` is the root, [ADR 0051] or a workspace package's directory for that package's scripts, which `ready` reads as the root's, with the jobs that start them as init tells them (**Workspaces**).
- [ADR 0037] **Reading a CI file.** A step's `run`, of one line or a block, [ADR 0055] a folded one as YAML gives it to the shell (C-15), or a job's `script:`, which GitLab runs in one shell. A run's `cwd` is its `working-directory`, its job's or its workflow's `defaults`, then each `cd` before it, relative to where the job checks this repository out (`actions/checkout`'s `path`, with no `repository`). Its key is built from its words as a script's; `ci` says why when they aren't sure, or when it is elsewhere: another checkout, or after a `cd` hushrun can't follow. [ADR 0050] A run whose COMMAND holds words CI or the shell fills in, `${{ … }}` or `$X`, has a key only its run can build: `ci`, with `its command takes values only CI knows`. The steps of the composite actions of this repository a job uses are read as init reads them (C-15), and judged by that job.
- **Each check is one of three.** `passed`: it passed on this code, by the evidence rules (P-1 to P-4) or as the newest result for its key, one the jobs that run it take (C-10), which `ready` tells by their names, since a job's platform is measured where it runs. `missing`: it didn't, and a command makes it pass here; it says why (`no pass yet for this exact code`, `its pass expired`, `it failed on this exact code last time`), and that command, its `run`. `ci`: nothing here changes it, CI or another machine has to run it, and it says why: the repository takes CI's evidence only (T-16); the check has no `run`; an unpinned check's `env` names a secret (E-12); a pinned check can't run isolated (E-12), or there is no Docker or Podman; this machine can't sign, or the server lists it for nobody or as revoked, so that its events wouldn't count (T-15); results that are off (C-11); or the evidence or results can't be read, or the backend refuses the credential. [ADR 0037] And for a run: it names no `--scope` among several (C-14), or a scope `hushrun.yaml` doesn't have; its code can't be read, an `external` input included (C-14); this repository's CI reuses only CI's results, so a pass here wouldn't change what it runs (C-12); its job or step needs what this machine lacks: services, a container or an image, a secret, a condition (`if`, `rules`, `only`, `except`, `when: manual`), another operating system, or a GitLab job that `extends` another, [ADR 0055] but for a job's `if:` that reads only other jobs' outputs and results (`needs.<job>.outputs.<name>`, `needs.<job>.result`, `needs.*.result`), with literals, comparisons, `&&`, `||`, `!`, `success()`, `always()`, `!cancelled()` and the functions of values (`contains`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `format`, `join`, `toJSON`, `fromJSON`), and nothing filled in with `${{ … }}` inside it: such a job runs when the jobs it needs say so, as a changes filter's does when its area changed, so its checks are judged as any job's. A workflow a job calls takes that job's condition, `job <id> of <file>, which calls its workflow, has a condition`, unless another job calls it without one; or a CI file's command starts a program this machine doesn't have, or a package.json script one that `node_modules/.bin` lacks too, since a run that can't start records nothing. With no `node_modules/.bin`, nothing says what was installed, and a script's run stays `missing`. A pass from elsewhere still counts for all of these. [ADR 0044] Two more are `ci` whatever passed. A run a job with several variants starts (C-14), since no pass from here counts there: [ADR 0049] `CI runs each variant until that variant passes on this code` when the job says which variant each run is, `CI runs each call until that call passes on this code` for a called workflow that takes inputs, and otherwise that CI runs it every time, what the job lacks, and `To skip there too, run hushrun init`; unless the newest result failed on this code, which stays `missing`, so that an agent doesn't push what it knows fails. [ADR 0049] `ready` reads the files a running workflow calls with `uses: ./<file>`, as init does; a workflow that starts on its own too, besides `workflow_call`, is judged by those runs, as a plain one is. And a run whose newest pass is another CI job's (C-10), which none of its jobs takes, while a run here would skip on it and change nothing. [ADR 0047] A run in a job with a step that reads its reports (C-26), unless its `reports.ci` is `skip`, is `ci` too, with `CI runs it every time: a later step reads its reports (<reader>)`, and a newest failure here still makes it `missing`. `run` decides what CI does; this only says so.
- [ADR 0045] **A rule.** `ready` and the hook start no tool, for any repository, and nothing a repository can name: of programs they start git, and one only the environment brings in, `HUSHRUN_REPOSITORY_TOKEN_COMMAND` when it is set (L-10). [ADR 0060] They register no machine (C-18), so that a push spends none of a person's machines in an org: a machine this login hasn't registered there is `missing` as any other, and the run registers it with its result. A container runtime is looked for on `PATH`, not started. Of a rule (C-25) they read what needs nothing started, `os`, `arch` and `env`, once everything above leaves a check `missing`. No server request is added.

  | Case | State |
  |---|---|
  | A pass under the key | `passed` |
  | A failure under the key | `missing`: `it failed on this exact code last time`, with the usual command |
  | A failure under the key, and a run here wouldn't count: its mark says so, or this machine doesn't meet the rule's `os`, `arch` or `env` | `missing`, with no command, and `why` says what clears it: `it failed on this exact code last time, and a run here wouldn't count (needs go 1.25, this is go1.24.3): change the code, or pass it on a machine that meets the rule` |
  | No result, and a run here under the rule didn't count | `ci`, with what the run said: `a run here didn't count: needs go 1.25, this is go1.24.3` |
  | No result, and this machine doesn't meet the rule's `os`, `arch` or `env` | `ci`, with why: `needs linux, this is macos` |
  | No result, a tool in the rule, and this cache keeps no mark | `ci`: `a run here couldn't say if it counted: <why>` |
  | No result otherwise, a tool in the rule included | `missing`, the usual command: the run decides |

  A failure outranks the rest because `ready` is here to stop a failing check being pushed, and a failure under a rule's key can only come from a machine that met the rule.

- [ADR 0037] **Each key is `hushrun run`'s.** A run is looked up by its name, its COMMAND, its `cwd`, its scope, the scope's digest, its `external` inputs and [ADR 0045] its rule, as C-9 builds them, with `?as=ci&repository=<id>` (C-12): with `ciOnly`, only a pass CI recorded is `passed`. With several scopes, a check is `<scope>/<check>`.
- [ADR 0067] **Results on this machine.** When results stay on this machine (C-11), each run is looked up in `<cache dir>/results` by the same key, with no query: `passed` when its newest result is a pass, whichever job recorded it, and the text says `passed  <check>  here`; `missing` with `it failed here on this exact code last time`, or `no pass here yet for this exact code`, and its command. The text's first line is `<code> isn't ready: <n> check(s) hasn't (haven't) passed here.` when one is `missing`, and a note, last, says `Results stay on this machine, so CI still runs these checks: hushrun init shares results with CI and teammates.`, or, for a repository that names a backend nobody here is signed in to, `…: hushrun login shares results with <org>'s CI and teammates.` Of the rules above, those about CI's runs don't apply: a job's variants, a later step that reads its reports, a pass of another CI job, CI's reuse of CI's results only, and what CI runs outside hushrun, whose notes are left out. The rest hold: a key or code that can't be told, a job or step that needs what this machine lacks, a program that isn't installed, a rule this machine doesn't meet, and the marks below.
- **The commands.** For hushrun.yaml's checks, `hushrun run [--scope <scope>] [--rev <rev> | --worktree] [--isolated] <checks>`, `--isolated` for the pinned ones and a second command for the others; `npx hushrun` when this hushrun is in a `node_modules` directory. For a script, the package manager's own, such as `npm run test`, once for all of a script's runs, [ADR 0051] with `cd <dir> && ` in front for a workspace package's. [ADR 0037] For a CI file's run, `cd <cwd> && hushrun run [--scope <scope>] <name> -- <command>`, without the `cd` at the root. Running them makes the checks `passed`, or leaves one failed.
- **Unrecorded passes.** When a run passes here and the backend refuses its record (C-14, ADR 0032), hushrun leaves an empty file, `unrecorded/<key's hex>`, in its cache directory; for one of hushrun.yaml's checks, whose key is the run key of no command. Such a check is `ci` when it would be `missing`, on this machine: nothing can record it now, and asking again would never end. [ADR 0060] A failure outranks the mark: a check whose newest result, or whose evidence, failed on this code is `missing`, whatever the file says. A run here that fails removes the file, recorded or not, and so does any result or check event this machine records, so that the mark stands for this machine's last run of the key only.
- [ADR 0045] **Runs that don't count.** Every run under a rule that doesn't count (C-25), pass or fail, leaves that file holding its reason, and `ready` reads it, starting nothing, as one line of at most 1,000 bytes, control characters left out. So a `missing` check with a rule and no result becomes `passed` or `ci` after its run, at the cost of one run per key on a machine that doesn't meet the rule, and that run warns before a long command. The mark counts only while its key has no result. A result this machine records removes the file; one recorded elsewhere leaves it, and it isn't read while the key has a result. With no result, it stands for this code on this machine after the machine is fixed too, which costs one run in CI, or one by hand, which counts and clears it. When the file can't be written (a read-only cache, a file for a cache, an empty `HUSHRUN_CACHE_DIR`), the run says so before its verdict, `hushrun: <NAME>: hushrun ready won't learn of this, as the note for it couldn't be kept: <why>`, and `ready`, which tries the cache for a check with a tool in its rule, says `ci`, as the table does.
- **Uncommitted changes.** When a command runs on the files as they are, not an export of the revision, and they aren't the code looked at, a note says to commit or stash what's different. [ADR 0060] A command for hushrun.yaml's checks then exits 2 until they are, since its evidence would be about files that never ran (§10.4); a script's or a CI file's runs, and records for the files as they are.
- [ADR 0040] **What CI runs outside hushrun.** When a workflow a push of the branch starts runs itself, at the root, a command a wrapped script runs, a note names the command, its file and its job, and says what to do: a pass here can't skip it there. It changes no check's state. The hook (C-23) prints it in place of `CI skips`.
- [ADR 0045] **Who recorded it.** A `passed` item has `recordedBy`, from the server's answer (C-12), and the text says `recorded by <who>`. [ADR 0048] And what its pass's reports said, its `reports` (C-27): the JSON has it as `reports`, and the text after who recorded it, `recorded by <who>: <summary>`. Nothing else of another machine's result is shown.
- [ADR 0065] **The skill.** `hushrun ready`, and not the hook, adds a note for the repository's copies of the skill (C-24), as `skill::state_of` tells them, in the working tree: for those that are `older`, ``This repository's hushrun skill is older than this hushrun's: `hushrun skill update` updates <copies>, then commit them.``; for those that are `newer`, ``A newer hushrun than this one, <version>, wrote <copies>: update hushrun with `<command>`.``, where the command is `hushrun update`, or `npm install hushrun@latest` from a `node_modules` directory, which also makes the first `npx hushrun`. It changes no check's state.
- [ADR 0070] **Test files.** For a `missing` check whose `tests` is `reuse` (C-5), a note says how many of its test files a run would run, when the plan (C-34) can be computed from the files on disk, the check's map and [ADR 0075] the hints a run would read (C-38), with one batch lookup: `<check>: CI would run 12 of 340 test files; 328 already passed on the files they read`, or `…: CI would run all 340 test files`; with results on this machine (ADR 0067) `<check>: a run here would run 12 of 340 test files; 328 already passed here on the files they read`; for a suspended check `<check>: every test file runs: reuse of its test files is suspended …`, as a run says it. [ADR 0078] With tests as the unit (C-39), counted as the run's line counts them, by the plan's own second tier and hints: `<check>: CI would run 31 of 2,140 tests, in 4 of 340 test files; 2,109 already passed on the files they read`, or where some test file's tests aren't known here, `<check>: CI would run 4 of 340 test files; 320 already passed on the files they read, and so did 87 tests of the others`. Only for an adapter whose plan reads files alone, pytest's, Vitest's (C-35), Jest's (C-44), Maven's (C-47), Gradle's (C-46) and CTest's (C-49): Cargo's needs a build, Go's `go list` and `swift test`'s `swift package describe`, which `ready` doesn't start; [ADR 0074] and for the pytest adapter, which any COMMAND hushrun doesn't recognize gets, only once pytest reported the check's test files on this machine (its store holds the check, C-36). Only for `--worktree`, or HEAD with nothing uncommitted. The lookup is the server's batch lookup (C-12), with no job, each unit's run taken by CI's jobs as a check's pass is, and nothing sent from the outbox, which reads as suspended; or this machine's. It writes nothing (ADR 0061). Otherwise the note isn't there. It changes no check's state.
- [ADR 0045] **`--strict`.** `hushrun ready --strict` is ready only when at least one check is known and every check is `passed`, because plain `ready` is true when nothing is `missing`, so unrun work reads as ready. `ready` in the output says so too.
- [ADR 0045] **A rule nothing runs.** When a rule in `checks.<name>` names no check `ready` knows, a note says so, to catch a typo: `hushrun.yaml has a rule for go-tset, and nothing runs it`. It changes no check's state.
- [ADR 0044] **`hushrun doctor`** has a check, `variants`, before it reads `hushrun.yaml`, by what CI's files say at HEAD, [ADR 0049] the files a running workflow calls included: for each wrapped check a job with several variants or a called workflow's job starts, `ready`'s line for it (C-22), as `ok`, since that is safe; and, as a warning, a job that sets `HUSHRUN_VARIANT`, `HUSHRUN_WORKFLOW` or `HUSHRUN_INPUTS` to anything but what init writes, `<check>: job <id> of <path> sets <NAME> to <value>, not <what init writes>, so no pass skips it there`. [ADR 0047] Beside it, `reports`: for each wrapped check a job runs with a step that reads its reports (C-26), `<check>: CI runs it every time, since <reader> reads its reports`, as `ok`, or that no step reads any.
- **Output.** Text, or with `--format json`, [ADR 0062] `hushrun.ready/v2` (`docs/schemas/ready.v2.schema.json`; v1 also had `stage`): `revision`, `branch`, `ready`, `checks` (`check`, `state`, `why`, `run`, `recordedBy`), `next` (the commands, in order) and `notes`; [ADR 0067] and `results`, where the results of the runs it looked up are, `shared`, `local` or `off`, left out when it looked up none.
- **Exit codes.** 0 when no check is `missing`, or with `--strict` when a check is known and every check is `passed`; 10 otherwise; 2 for a usage or configuration error, such as an unknown scope or a `hushrun.yaml` that doesn't parse.

**C-23 The pre-push hook.** `hushrun hook install` installs a pre-push hook that says what CI would run again on the code being pushed, and refuses a coding agent's push until that is done. It isn't a gate: it runs no checks, `git push --no-verify` skips it, and CI runs whatever has no pass.

- **What a push is.** The branches on git's stdin, `<local ref> <local sha> <remote ref> <remote sha>` a line, each judged by C-22 at its local sha, going to its remote branch. Tags, a deleted branch (a local sha of zeros), and nothing to push are left alone, and so is a remote whose URL names another repository than `origin` does, ignoring case. A URL that names none, such as a path, is the repository.
- **Who it refuses.** By default a coding agent, and no person; with `--refuse` everyone; with `--warn` no one. The flag is in the lines it installs. Someone is an agent, in order: [ADR 0046] an agent set (C-17) says yes, whatever else; `HUSHRUN_INVOKED_BY` of `human:…` says no, and `agent:…` says yes; an agent token in `HUSHRUN_TOKEN`; `AI_AGENT`, not empty; `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE`, `CODEX_SANDBOX`, `CODEX_CI`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `OPENCODE_CLIENT`, `CURSOR_AGENT`, `GEMINI_CLI`, `AUGMENT_AGENT`, `ANTIGRAVITY_AGENT`, `COPILOT_MODEL`, `COPILOT_ALLOW_ALL` or `COPILOT_GITHUB_TOKEN`; `OPENCODE=1`; or `CURSOR_EXTENSION_HOST_ROLE=agent-exec`. `CURSOR_TRACE_ID` doesn't count: people's terminals have it.
- **What it says, on stderr.** Refused: `hushrun: push refused: CI would run <checks> again on <commit>`, each check with its reason, `Run these here, then push again:`, the commands, and the notes; to someone who isn't an agent, also how to push anyway. Warned: `hushrun: CI will run <checks> on <commit>. To skip that, run these before you push:`, and the commands. Ready with a pass: `hushrun: <commit> is ready: CI skips <checks>`, unless a note says CI runs one outside hushrun (C-22), and any notes. [ADR 0067] With results on this machine (C-22), these say what hasn't passed here instead of what CI would run: refused, `hushrun: push refused: <checks> haven't passed here on <commit>`; warned, `hushrun: <checks> haven't passed here on <commit>. Run these before you push:`; ready, `hushrun: <commit> is ready: <checks> passed here`, then the note that CI still runs them. A `ci` check says nothing, so a machine that doesn't meet a rule (C-25) isn't refused for it, once `ready` can tell. With no command to run, as for a failure a run here can't clear (C-22), it says neither `Run these here` nor `To skip that`. The hook starts what C-22 says `ready` does. `-q` silences all but a refusal.
- **Only a known gap stops a push.** It exits 10 when it refuses, and 0 otherwise: in CI (`CI` and the like, unless `HUSHRUN_PROVIDER=local`, or an agent is set where only `CI` says so, C-17); outside a repository; when it can't read `hushrun.yaml`; and when 15 seconds pass without an answer, saying so. The lines stop a push on exit code 10 only, so a crash, a signal, or a hushrun that doesn't know the command let it through, and they do nothing where hushrun isn't installed. [ADR 0055] A check of a job behind a condition on other jobs' outputs (C-22) with no pass yet for this exact code is such a gap, though its job may not run on this push: the hook can't tell whether its area changed, and asks for one run here.
- **Where it goes.** With a `.husky` directory, `.husky/pre-push`, adding to what is there. With `lefthook.yml`, `lefthook.yaml`, `.lefthook.yml` or `.lefthook.yaml`, it changes nothing, and prints the lines to add, with `use_stdin: true`. Else git's hooks directory (`git rev-parse --git-path hooks`, which follows `core.hooksPath`), `pre-push`, executable; a file there without hushrun's lines stays as it is, and it prints the lines to add. Its lines run `node_modules/.bin/hushrun`, else `hushrun` from `PATH`, between `# hushrun:start` and `# hushrun:end`; `hushrun hook install` again replaces them. [ADR 0045] They run `hushrun` from `PATH` whenever `git ls-files -- ':(icase)node_modules'` prints anything: a `node_modules` git tracks, in any case or as a link, is the repository's own program, which a push doesn't start. A clone keeps the lines it has until `hushrun hook install` replaces them, which a package.json's `prepare` does at each install (C-24), [ADR 0061] keeping their mode; `ready` and `run` install a hook only where there is none. `hushrun hook uninstall` removes them, and the file when nothing else is left.
- [ADR 0061] **The mode is someone's choice.** It is the flag in hushrun's lines, and nothing else holds it. Only `hushrun hook install` outside a package manager's `prepare` or `postinstall` changes it, to what its flags say, the default with none. In `prepare` or `postinstall`, `hushrun hook install` keeps the mode of hushrun's lines that are there, and gives a new hook its flags' mode; `hushrun init` keeps it too, and gives a new hook the default. Lines with no flag, an older hushrun's included, are the default.
- **In a package manager's `prepare` or `postinstall`** (`npm_lifecycle_event`), and in CI, it never fails: without a repository, and whatever else goes wrong, it exits 0. CI gets no hook.
- [ADR 0043] **In a clone that has none.** `hushrun ready` and `hushrun run`, and no other command, install the hook first, with the default refusal, when all of these hold: this isn't CI; the repository's root has `hushrun.yaml`, or a package.json with a `hushrun` key, `hushrun` in `dependencies`, `devDependencies` or `optionalDependencies`, or a script that names it; it has no `.husky` directory and no lefthook file; git's configuration sets neither `core.hooksPath` nor `hushrun.hook`; and nothing has the name of git's pre-push hook, a link that leads nowhere included. The file appears whole and executable and replaces nothing, so runs at the same moment leave one hook, and one line: `hushrun: installed the pre-push hook in <path>; hushrun hook uninstall takes it out`, on stderr, unless `-q`. Whatever goes wrong, such as a read-only `.git`, it says nothing and the command goes on.
- [ADR 0043] **Uninstalling holds.** `hushrun hook uninstall` sets `hushrun.hook` to `off` in the clone's git configuration, whether or not there was a hook, and warns when it can't. [ADR 0061] Only `hushrun hook install` clears it, outside a package manager's `prepare` or `postinstall`. There it leaves it, and installs nothing, and so does `hushrun init`, whose plan then has no hook.
- **Exit codes.** `install` and `uninstall`: 0; 2 outside a repository; 1 when a file can't be written.

**C-24 What init sets up for agents.** `hushrun init` writes what a coding agent needs into the repository, so that every clone and sandbox has it (C-15 steps 4 and 5).

- **The skill.** `.claude/skills/hushrun/SKILL.md`, which Claude Code reads, and `.agents/skills/hushrun/SKILL.md`, which Codex reads, with the same text; OpenCode and Cursor read both. Its `name` is `hushrun`, and its `description` at most 1,024 characters. init replaces a copy that differs, [ADR 0065] unless it is `newer`.
- [ADR 0065] **What it holds.** What every hushrun does the same way, and to run `hushrun guide` (C-28) once per session for the rest. Its frontmatter has only the Agent Skills spec's fields, `name`, `description`, `compatibility` and `metadata`, and `metadata` has `hushrun-skill`, the skill's revision: a whole number, as a string, that goes up only when the text changes. Each revision's text is known by its SHA-256, CRLF read as LF; the texts hushrun wrote before revisions, 0.3.0 to 0.10.1, are revision 0.
- [ADR 0065] **What a copy is**, next to this hushrun's, as one function tells it for every command: `current`, this hushrun's revision with that revision's text; `older`, a lower revision with that revision's text, or a text from before revisions; `newer`, a higher revision, whatever its text; `edited`, any other text, or a file hushrun can't read; `absent`, none.
- **`AGENTS.md`.** A block between `<!-- hushrun:start` and `<!-- hushrun:end -->`: appended, after what the file has; in place of an older block; or a new file. Everything else keeps its bytes.
- **The hook** (C-23), by default. [ADR 0061] A hook of hushrun's keeps its mode, and a clone where `hushrun hook uninstall` ran gets none.
