- [ADR 0043] **Without package.json**, the first `hushrun ready` or `hushrun run` in a clone installs the hook (C-23), and init and the skill say so, and that a clone where nobody has run hushrun has none.
- [ADR 0043] **hushrun itself.** Init's plan adds the newest release of hushrun when it has no package.json; hushrun is in a `node_modules` directory; none is on `PATH`, leaving out directories that hold `_npx` or `node_modules`; this isn't CI or Windows; and no installer's receipt (C-20) names a binary that is still there. It goes in the first of `$CARGO_HOME/bin`, `~/.cargo/bin`, `$XDG_BIN_HOME`, `~/.local/bin`, `~/bin`, `$GOBIN`, `$GOPATH/bin` and `~/go/bin` that is on `PATH` and looks writable, made if it isn't there yet. It is downloaded and checked as C-20's update does, and init leaves a receipt with `install_layout` `flat`, so that `hushrun update` keeps it up to date. Nothing edits a shell's startup file. When no directory fits, or anything fails, init warns, goes on, exits as it would have, and prints the installer's command.
- **The script that installs the hook.** In package.json, with the edits' rules of C-15: `"prepare": "hushrun hook install || exit 0"`, or `"postinstall"` for Yarn from 2 on, which doesn't run `prepare`; after a script that is there, `<script> && (hushrun hook install || exit 0)`; nothing when it names `hushrun hook`, or isn't a string.
- `recipes/agents/` holds the skill and the block.

**C-25 Where a check counts.** [ADR 0045] `counts_from` in `hushrun.yaml` says what a check needs of the machine a run happens on. A run that doesn't meet it records nothing, so nothing skips on it. It is about where a run happened, never whose evidence counts: a pass under the key skips on any machine. Its values are the machine's own word: it stops mistakes, not people. Anything that needs more is C-12's "CI reuses only CI's results", which also makes the repository's evidence CI-only (T-16).

```yaml
# iOS app
counts_from:
  os: macos
```

```yaml
# Go backend
counts_from:
  os: linux
  go: "1.25"
```

```yaml
# Monorepo: each `hushrun run` names its scope, `hushrun run --scope ios swiftlint -- swiftlint --strict`
scopes:
  ios:
    include: ["ios/**"]
    counts_from: {os: macos}
  api:
    include: ["api/**"]
    counts_from: {os: linux, go: "1.25"}
checks:
  swiftlint:
    counts_from: anywhere
```

- **Keys**, a closed list, each taking one value or a list, where a list means any of them.

  | Key | Meaning |
  |---|---|
  | `os` | `macos`, `linux` or `windows`. |
  | `arch` | `arm64` or `amd64`. |
  | `go`, `rust`, `node`, `xcode` | A quoted version prefix, compared by dotted segment: `"1.25"` matches 1.25 and 1.25.14, not 1.250 or 1.2. A pre-release, with anything but numbers after them, such as `1.25rc2` or `1.94.0-nightly`, matches only a prefix that writes it out whole. |
  | `env` | A map of variables that must hold exactly these values: `env: {SANDBOX: go-7}`. [ADR 0046] Read as hushrun reads its own variables (C-17), and it can't name `HUSHRUN_AGENT`, `HUSHRUN_TOKEN` or `HUSHRUN_MACHINE_KEY`, in any case, which hold a secret it would compare to. |

- **Levels.** `checks.<name>.counts_from`, else `scopes.<name>.counts_from`, else the top level. The nearest wins whole, with no merging. `counts_from: anywhere` means no rule at that level, and the levels further out don't apply. A check's rule applies by name in every scope. A check that exists only for its rule needs nothing else.
- **A run** of a check with a rule:

  1. Looks up as C-10 says. A pass under the key skips on any machine, under C-10 and C-14's guards, and skipping measures nothing.
  2. Otherwise measures, for information. When this machine doesn't meet the rule, it says so before a long run: `hushrun: go-test running: this run won't count: needs go 1.25, this is go1.24.3`. A tool that can't be measured is no error, and says nothing yet.
  3. Runs COMMAND in place, with its own exit code.
  4. Measures again, because a toolchain may arrive during the run. This decides.
  5. Met: it records as C-10 says, and its last line is `hushrun: go-test passed in 6s on linux, go1.25.14; it counts`, naming the OS, `arch` when the rule does, and the tools the rule names (`rust 1.94.0`, `node 24.1.0`, `xcode 16.2`). A failure is recorded, with its usual line. Not met: it records nothing, costs no quota, keeps COMMAND's exit code, leaves C-22's mark with the reason, pass or fail, and its last line is `hushrun: go-test passed in 6s; it won't count: needs go 1.25, this is go1.24.3`, or `failed in 6s (exit 1); …`.

  A rule changes none of C-10's and C-14's guards: it never makes a run skip where they refuse, and they never make a run count where the rule refuses. Without a rule, nothing is measured, and a run's lines stay as they are.
- **What doesn't count:**
  - a tool the rule names that isn't there (`needs go 1.25, and go isn't here`) or can't be measured, because it fails, says no version or takes too long (`needs xcode 16, and hushrun can't tell which xcode this is: …`);
  - an `env` variable that is unset or differs (`needs SANDBOX go-7, and it isn't set`, `… and it holds something else`, whose value is never shown);
  - a COMMAND that picks its own toolchain while the rule names that tool (`needs rust 1.94, and cargo +nightly picks its own toolchain`). Measuring asks what PATH finds, so this is read from COMMAND's words:
    - the tool's program given by a path, such as `/opt/go1.24/bin/go` or `node_modules/.bin/node`;
    - `PATH`, or a variable that picks the tool, set in COMMAND: `GOTOOLCHAIN`, `GOROOT`, `GOENV_VERSION`, `ASDF_GOLANG_VERSION`, `MISE_GO_VERSION`; `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN`, `RUSTC`, `ASDF_RUST_VERSION`, `MISE_RUST_VERSION`; `NODENV_VERSION`, `ASDF_NODEJS_VERSION`, `MISE_NODE_VERSION`; `DEVELOPER_DIR`;
    - a `+toolchain` first argument, and a program whose name is the tool's and a version, such as `go1.24.3`;
    - `rustup run`; `mise exec`, `mise x`, `mise use` and `asdf exec`, for any tool; `fnm exec`, `fnm use`, `volta run`, `nvm exec`, `nvm run`, `nvm use`, `nodenv exec`, `n exec`, `n run`, `n use` and `bunx --bun` for node; `goenv exec` and `gvm use` for go;
    - a launcher (`uv run`, `poetry run`, `pipenv run`, `pdm run`, `rye run`, `hatch run`, `python -m`, `python3 -m`, `bundle exec`, `npx`, `bunx`, `pnpm exec`, `yarn exec`, `npm exec`, `pnpm dlx`, `yarn dlx`) whose first word, or `-p` or `--package`, names the tool, with or without a version, such as `npx node@22 t.js` or `npm exec --package=node@22 -- vitest`. `npx vitest run` starts something else, on the machine's node, and counts, and so does `npx playwright test --project=node@22`, whose word is playwright's.

    `env`, `nice`, `time`, `timeout` and `export` are read past, with their options. Each command of a `sh -c '…'`, `bash -c '…'` or `cmd /c …` string is read the same way, and `.exe`, `.cmd` and `.bat` are left off a program's name.
- **`hushrun init`** writes one rule, `counts_from: {os: macos}`, only for a repository or a project whose checks run in `macos-*` jobs with Xcode or Swift, and never over what a file says (C-15, **A rule**).
- **Measuring** happens only in `hushrun run`, where the repository's code runs anyway, before and after COMMAND, and only for a check with a rule. A tool is asked in COMMAND's working directory, with the PATH COMMAND gets, so that a version manager answers as it would for the check, stdin closed, for 10 s at most. `node` is asked again, when a `node_modules/.bin` from that directory up to the repository's root holds one, with those first on the PATH, nearest first, as npm runs what a script starts: nothing says whether COMMAND goes through npm, so the rule has to hold for both. Each row of the table is one tool with its own parser. On Windows a program is found as C-14 says.

  | Name | How |
  |---|---|
  | `os`, `arch` | The running binary's, as `platform` is in `job` (C-10), `darwin` being `macos` |
  | `go` | `go env GOVERSION` |
  | `rust` | `rustc --version` |
  | `node` | `node --version` |
  | `xcode` | `xcodebuild -version` |

- **Limits.** This stops honest mistakes, not determined ones. COMMAND is read as words, as far as this says: a wrapper it doesn't name, such as `docker run golang:1.24 go test` or `make test`, is the command it starts with, a script's own `export PATH=…` isn't seen, and neither is `.npmrc`'s `use-node-version`. `arch` is the running binary's, so an x86-64 hushrun under Rosetta says `amd64`. A rule's `env` values are in plain text in `hushrun.yaml`, so they name a sandbox rather than hold a secret. A mark stands for this code on this machine, even when the machine changes (C-22).

**C-26 Reports a later step reads.** [ADR 0047] A skip writes no report. So in a CI job where a later step reads a check's reports, a pass that counts in the job (C-10) doesn't skip the check: hushrun runs it, records it as usual, and says why in one line, naming the first such step, `hushrun: <NAME> running: a later step reads its reports (<reader>), and a skip writes none`. Its last line is `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>`, without saying it won't run again (C-14), unless what it wrote narrows this (below). Outside CI nothing changes: a laptop or an agent skips on a pass as before. `<reader>` is the step's `uses:` without its `@ref`, else its `name:`, else `run: ` and at most 40 characters of its command, cut after a word, with `…`.

- **On GitHub Actions** hushrun reads the workflow file and the job that C-14's variants rule reads, once, for both. When it can't tell which job it is, C-14 runs the check already, and this rule adds nothing. A step of that job reads reports when it:
  - uses an action of the catalog (`cmd/setup/readers.rs`), matched by its name without its `@ref`, ignoring case: Codecov's (`codecov/codecov-action`, `codecov/test-results-action`) and Coveralls' uploads; coverage comments and reports (`py-cov-action/python-coverage-comment-action`, `MishaKav/pytest-coverage-comment`, `MishaKav/jest-coverage-comment`, `ArtiomTr/jest-coverage-report-action`, `romeovs/lcov-reporter-action`, `zgosalvez/github-actions-report-lcov`, `irongut/CodeCoverageSummary`, `5monkeys/cobertura-action`, `danielpalme/ReportGenerator-GitHub-Action`, `madrapps/jacoco-report`, `insightsengineering/coverage-action`, `orgoro/coverage`, `paambaati/codeclimate-action`, `qltysh/qlty-action/coverage`); test results (`dorny/test-reporter`, `EnricoMi/publish-unit-test-result-action` and its `/linux`, `/macos`, `/windows` and `/composite`, `mikepenz/action-junit-report`, `test-summary/action`, `scacap/action-surefire-report`, `ctrf-io/github-test-reporter`, `trunk-io/analytics-uploader`, `datadog/junit-upload-github-action`, `pmeier/pytest-results-action`, `phoenix-actions/test-reporting`); and other reports (`SonarSource/sonarqube-scan-action`, `SonarSource/sonarcloud-github-action`, `github/codeql-action/upload-sarif`);
  - uploads an artifact (`actions/upload-artifact`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact`) whose `path` has a line that names a report: it holds a report's token, ignoring case, and ends in `.xml`, `.info`, `.lcov`, `.json`, `.out`, `.trx`, `.sarif` or `.xcresult`, or in `/`, or has no extension, as a directory. The tokens are, for test results, `junit`, `test-results`, `test_results`, `testresults`, `test-reports`, `surefire-reports`, `failsafe-reports`, `nextest`, `.trx`, `results.xml`, `report.xml`, `allure-results`, and `TEST-` in capitals, as Surefire and Gradle name their files; for coverage, `coverage`, `lcov`, `cobertura`, `jacoco`, `clover`, `kover`, `htmlcov`, `.coverage`, `coverprofile` and `cover.out`; and for other reports, `.sarif` and `reports/`;
  - uses an action the catalog doesn't know, with an input whose value names a report file: a token, and one of those endings or `/`, so `requirements-coverage.txt` and `coverage` don't count;
  - runs a command that reads reports: `codecov`; `codecovcli` with `do-upload`, `upload-process`, `upload-coverage`, `-f` or `--file`; Codecov's `codecov.io/bash` script; `coveralls`; `coverage` with `combine`, `report`, `xml`, `html`, `json` or `lcov`; `genhtml`; `lcov`; `llvm-cov` with `report`, `show` or `export`; `cargo llvm-cov report`; `go tool cover`; `gcovr`; `nyc report`; `c8 report`; `reportgenerator`; `datadog-ci junit upload`; `trunk flakytests upload`; `junit2html`; `allure generate`; `sonar-scanner`; Maven's `sonar:sonar`; or Gradle's `sonar` or `sonarqube`, after a launcher C-15's table knows, or `xcrun`. A path alone never makes a `run:` step a reader, so `hushrun run unit -- jest --coverage` isn't its own;
  - or uses a local composite action (`uses: ./<dir>`, with `runs.using: composite` in its `action.yml`) one of whose own steps reads reports, one level deep.

  What CI fills in (`${{ }}`) names nothing, and what is around it still can: `target/test_results_${{ matrix.crate }}.json` names a report, and `${{ env.OUT }}` none. Exclusions (`!…`) name none. A step whose `if:` is exactly `failure()`, spaces and `${{ }}` aside, never runs after a skip, which succeeds, so it doesn't count.
- **A linter's skip** leaves coverage and test results as they were. A check whose command C-15's table knows as a linter, a formatter, a type checker or vet still skips when every such step reads only coverage or test results; one that reads other reports, such as SARIF, runs it. A package manager's script goes by its name and a task runner's target by its own, and a command the table doesn't know counts as a test, since it may write anything.
- **`checks.<name>.reports.ci: skip`** (C-5) says the repository's readers handle a skip: the check skips on a pass as before.
- **`HUSHRUN_REPORTS_READ=true`** (§10.1) says the job reads reports, on any CI: every check of the job runs, a linter's too, since nothing says what the readers read, unless its `reports.ci` is `skip`. Its line is `hushrun: <NAME> running: HUSHRUN_REPORTS_READ says this job reads reports, and a skip writes none`. Nothing reads `.gitlab-ci.yml` at run time, so on GitLab, and on CI that init doesn't edit, this is how the rule applies. `false`, or unset, adds nothing: on GitHub Actions the workflow's steps still decide.
- [ADR 0048] **What this job's own pass wrote narrows it.** A step stops concerning the check when all of this holds: the pass that counts here is this very job's on GitHub Actions, recorded by this very version of hushrun, since another may read the steps otherwise; its `reports` says `filesComplete` (C-27); this checkout is `GITHUB_WORKSPACE`, where the steps' paths start; the workflow file, and the `action.yml` of each local action its steps use, is in the check's scope (D-7), so that a change to the steps changes the key; and the step reads only the paths it names, none of which names a file of the pass's `files`, as itself or as a directory above it. A step reads more than it names when it names no path, uses an action the catalog doesn't know, runs a command, or reads other reports, such as SARIF; Codecov's actions look for reports of their own besides `files`, unless the step sets `disable_search: true` or a `directory`. A path CI fills in, but for `${{ github.workspace }}` at its start, an absolute one, one outside the repository or the whole repository can't be told, so its step concerns the check. When no step does, the run skips on the pass as on any other, and the pass's last line said `; it won't run again on this code`. So in a job with two test checks and an upload of the coverage one of them writes, the other skips after its first pass there.
- **The run decides** from the file it runs in. A workflow can change after init, so init's plan predicts the decision with the same code (C-15); [ADR 0048] what a pass wrote it can't know, so it predicts as for a pass that wrote what the job's steps read.
- [ADR 0069] **No test file is reused there either** (C-34): a reused test file writes no report. Wherever a pass wouldn't skip the check for its reports, whatever the pass wrote, the run says, before COMMAND, `hushrun: <NAME>: every test file runs: a later step reads its reports (<reader>), and a test file it reuses writes none` (or `HUSHRUN_REPORTS_READ says this job reads reports, …`), and its `units` says `ranAnyway: reports` (C-10). `reports.ci: skip` lets the check reuse, as it lets it skip.

**C-27 Report summaries.** [ADR 0048] After COMMAND exits, pass or fail, `hushrun run NAME -- COMMAND` finds the test and coverage reports this run wrote and records their summary in the result's `reports` (C-10): counts, and each file's path and format, at most 4 KiB. Never a test's name, a message, or a file's contents: no report file leaves the machine. A skip carries the summary of the pass it stood in for, and shows it. Reading a report never changes the run's verdict or exit code. [ADR 0062] `hushrun run CHECK…`, isolated or not, records none.

- **Which files.** The union of: the files the command line names (pytest's `--junitxml` and `--junit-xml`; pytest-cov's `--cov-report=xml:P`, `lcov:P` or `json:P`, else `coverage.xml`, `coverage.lcov` or `coverage.json` where it ran; go's `-coverprofile`; jest's `--coverageDirectory`; vitest's `--coverage.reportsDirectory`, `--outputFile.junit`, and `--outputFile` with `--reporter=junit`; phpunit's `--log-junit`, `--coverage-clover` and `--coverage-cobertura`; cargo llvm-cov's `--output-path` with `--lcov` or `--cobertura`; nextest's `--profile` when `.config/nextest.toml` gives it a JUnit `path`; dotnet's `--results-directory` and `--logger "trx;LogFileName=P"`; rspec's `--out` after a JUnit formatter), each a file or, for a directory, what is below it; `checks.<name>.reports.files` (C-5), from where the check ran; on GitHub Actions, the paths the job's readers read (C-26), from the root; and the places tools write to by default, from where the check ran and from the root: `junit.xml`, `junit-*.xml`, `*junit*.xml`, `test-results.xml`, `rspec.xml`, `report.xml`, `test-results/**/*.xml`, `test-reports/**/*.xml`, `build/test-results/**/*.xml`, `target/surefire-reports/*.xml`, `target/failsafe-reports/*.xml`, `target/nextest/*/junit.xml`, `TestResults/**/*.trx`, `**/TEST-*.xml`, `coverage.xml`, `cobertura.xml`, `**/cobertura-coverage.xml`, `lcov.info`, `coverage/lcov.info`, `coverage.lcov`, `coverage/coverage-summary.json`, `coverage.json`, `coverage/clover.xml`, `clover.xml`, `coverage.out`, `cover.out`, `coverage.txt`, `target/site/jacoco/jacoco.xml`, `build/reports/jacoco/**/*.xml`, `build/reports/kover/**/*.xml`, `coverage/.resultset.json`, `TestResults/**/coverage.cobertura.xml` and `TestResults/**/coverage.opencover.xml`. Of those, a regular file inside the repository, never a link or through one, modified at or after the run's start less 2 s; a link a path of the job's readers matches, or one to a directory that a path goes into, or a directory where a path goes that is too deep for `**` or can't be read, leaves the list incomplete (below). The search enters no `.git`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, `venv` or `__pycache__`, and no hidden directory through `**`, which goes at most 6 levels down; [ADR 0060] no wildcard of the readers' paths goes into those either, but one of the five that a path names, word for word, leaves the list incomplete; past 200,000 directory entries or a second, the run records no summary and says nothing.
- **Formats, by content, never by name.** JUnit XML (root `<testsuites>` or `<testsuite>`) and TRX (`<TestRun>`) hold test results; LCOV (`TN:` or `SF:` first), Cobertura (`<coverage>` with `line-rate`), JaCoCo (`<report>`), Clover (another `<coverage>`), OpenCover (`<CoverageSession>`), Go's coverprofile (`mode: set`, `count` or `atomic` first), istanbul's summary (`total.lines.total`), coverage.py's JSON (`totals.num_statements`) and SimpleCov's result set (entries holding `coverage`) hold coverage. A file in none of them, or broken, says nothing. XML is read as a stream, with no DTD and no entity expansion.
  - JUnit counts each `<testcase>` once, at any depth, by its children: `<failure>` is failed, else `<error>` an error, else `<skipped>` skipped; Surefire's reruns are no outcome, and the summary attributes are never read. Its time is the root's `time`, else the sum of the suites just below it, else unknown. TRX takes `ResultSummary/Counters`: `total`; `failed`, `timeout` and `aborted` as failed; `error`; `notExecuted` as skipped; its time is `Times`' start to finish.
  - Cobertura takes its root's `lines-covered`/`lines-valid` and `branches-covered`/`branches-valid`, else each `<line>` once per file and number, covered when its `hits` is above 0, with its `condition-coverage`'s branches. LCOV takes each record's `LF`/`LH` and `BRF`/`BRH`, else its `DA` and `BRDA` lines. JaCoCo takes only the report's own `<counter>`s, Clover the project's own `<metrics>` (statements, and conditionals for branches), OpenCover the session's own `<Summary>` (sequence points, and branch points). Go counts statements: each block once, with the highest count it is given, covered when above 0. istanbul takes `total.lines` and `total.branches`; coverage.py `totals.covered_lines` of `num_statements`, and `covered_branches` of `num_branches`; SimpleCov merges its commands' lines and branches, covered when any covered them.
- **The summary.** Test results are summed over every file of them. Coverage comes from one format only, the first a run wrote of LCOV, Cobertura, JaCoCo, Clover, OpenCover, Go, coverage.py, istanbul and SimpleCov, summed over that format's files, so coverage written in two formats isn't counted twice. A file over 64 MiB, more than 256 MiB of one kind's files, or a summary past 3 s drops that kind's summary; more than 5,000 files gives none. A count over 10^9 drops its kind's summary, and a duration over 10^11 ms is unknown.
- **The record.** `reports` holds `files` (each `path` from the root, `/`-separated, its `kind`, `tests` or `coverage`, and its `format`, a word such as `junit` or `cobertura`), every file read, whatever it counts, at most 16 of them, each path at most 160 bytes, the others counted in `filesLeftOut`; `filesComplete`, `true` when `files` names every file the search found, each read and none left out, and the search went everywhere the paths of the job's readers go, for C-26, else left out; `tests` (`total`, `failed`, `errors`, `skipped`, and `durationMs` when the files say), when a test-results file was read; and `coverage` (`format`, then `lines`, or `statements` for Go, and `branches`, each `{covered, total}`), when a coverage file was. A part that counts nothing is left out; serialized, `reports` is at most 4,096 bytes. A search that finds nothing records `{"filesComplete": true}`; one that finds only files it can't read records no `reports`. A skip's record has the `reports` of the pass it stood in for, as `savedMs` has that pass's duration. Readers ignore what they don't know in it (C-10).
- **What people see.** A run's last line says its summary after its time, `: <summary>`, and every ending keeps its form after it: `hushrun: <NAME> passed in <time>: <summary>; it won't run again on this code`, `hushrun: <NAME> failed in <time> (exit <code>): <summary>`. A skip's line says its pass's: `hushrun: <NAME> skipped: this exact code passed in CI 3 hours ago (saves 1m 21s): <summary>`. `<summary>` is the tests, `1,204 tests` (`1 test`), then `, 3 failed`, failures and errors together, and `, 14 skipped` when they aren't 0; then the coverage, `87.3% of lines covered`, or `of statements covered` for Go's; joined by `, `. Counts group thousands with commas, and a percentage has one decimal, rounded half up, but never `100.0%` for less than all, nor `0.0%` for more than none.
- **On GitHub Actions**, unless `--no-ci-output` says otherwise, every run writes the step outputs `hushrun-result` (`pass`, `fail` or `skip`), `hushrun-skipped` (`true` or `false`), and `hushrun-tests`, `hushrun-tests-failed` and `hushrun-coverage-lines` (its lines, or Go's statements, as a percentage with one decimal), those three empty without a summary, so that in a step that runs several checks the last run's values win. A skip also writes a step summary: `### <NAME> skipped`; when and where this exact code passed, with a link to its run only when the pass is this repository's, since it can be another of the org's, which is never named; what the skip saved; and its pass's summary as a table, in the columns that have values: `Tests`, `Failed`, `Skipped`, `Lines covered` (`Statements covered`) and `Branches covered`, each covered as `87.3% (8,123 of 9,310)`.
- **A summary the server refuses**, as one that finds the result invalid would (400 `invalid`), leaves the run unrecorded, and its line ends `; not recorded: <what the server said>`. The exit code is the command's.
- [ADR 0069] **A run with reused test files** (C-34): its summary's tests are those that ran, and its coverage is left out, since a figure of what ran would read as a drop. Its last line says, after its summary, `; reusing <N> test files saved about <time>`, the reused test files' recorded durations added up, or `; <N> test files reused` under a second.

### 7.9 What agents learn [ADR 0065]

**C-28 `hushrun guide`.** `hushrun guide` prints what a coding agent needs to know about this hushrun beyond its skill (C-24), from the binary, so that it says what this hushrun does: `recipes/agents/GUIDE.md`. Then, under `## Here`, what is true here: `This is hushrun <version>, with skill revision <n>.`; C-21's line for agents, when the notice's record (C-21) names a newer release; and each copy of the skill, `- <copy>: <state>`, the repository's two when it runs in a git repository, relative to its root, then the person's own, `~/.claude/skills/hushrun/SKILL.md` and `~/.agents/skills/hushrun/SKILL.md`. An `older` copy says the command that updates it, and a `newer` one how to update hushrun. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`, nor a repository.

- **It asks nobody.** It reads the notice's record, and asks no server, GitHub included. With `HUSHRUN_NO_UPDATE_CHECK` set (C-21), it says nothing of releases. It changes nothing.
- **Output.** Text, or with `--format json`, `hushrun.guide/v1` (`docs/schemas/guide.v1.schema.json`): `version`, `skillRevision`, `guide`, `update` (`latest` and `command`, or null) and `skills` (`path`, `state`, `revision`, null for `edited` and `absent`). Fields are only ever added.
- **Exit codes.** 0.

**C-29 `hushrun skill update`.** `hushrun skill update` writes this hushrun's skill (C-24) where agents read it: the two copies of the git repository it runs in, at its root, as init does; or with `--global`, the person's own, `~/.claude/skills/hushrun/SKILL.md` and `~/.agents/skills/hushrun/SKILL.md`. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`.

- **Which copies.** An `absent` or `older` copy is written. A `current` one is left: `<copy> is current.`. An `edited` or `newer` one is left, with a warning on stderr, `warning: left <copy>: it was edited by hand; --force replaces it`, or ``warning: left <copy>: a newer hushrun wrote it (skill revision <n>, this hushrun's is <m>): update hushrun with `<command>` ``; `--force` writes them too.
- **How.** Each copy whole, through a temporary file beside it and a rename. A copy that is a link, as skills managers make them, keeps leading where it did: the file it leads to is written. It prints `Wrote <copy>.` for each.
- **Exit codes.** 0, whatever it left; 2 outside a git repository without `--global`, `this isn't a git repository: run it in one, or use --global for your own copies`; 1 when a copy can't be written, or the home directory can't be told.

### 7.10 Test by test [ADR 0068]

`checks.<name>.tests` (C-5) turns on, for one check run as `hushrun run NAME -- COMMAND`, a finer level under C-10: its test files. With `observe`, every test file runs: hushrun records what each one read and how it ended, and says what `tests: reuse` would have done, and it never changes COMMAND's outcome or exit code. [ADR 0069] With `reuse`, inside a run whose check missed, test files that already passed on the files they read aren't run again (C-34). In what people read, a unit is a **test file** and what it read is **the files it read**.

**C-30 Units and the four sets.** A unit is what a runner collects and can select: for pytest, a test file; for Cargo, a test binary, named by its target's root file (`src/lib.rs`, `src/main.rs`, a file of `tests/`). Its name is its path from the repository root, `/`-separated. Every file of the check's scope (C-4) is in exactly one of four sets, by one function, in this order:

- **ignored**: `tests.ignore` names it. A change to it runs nothing.
- **units**: the adapter's unit rule names it, from the files alone, before any test runs. For pytest, a file ending in `.py` whose name matches pytest's `python_files` (`fnmatch`, on the name), read from the first of `pytest.ini`, `pyproject.toml` with `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` (or `[tool.pytest]`), `tox.ini` with `[pytest]` and `setup.cfg` with `[tool:pytest]` in each directory from where COMMAND runs up to the root; `test_*.py` and `*_test.py` without one. When a pattern names a directory, or pytest reports another value than this (C-32), no unit is observed. For Cargo, the root file of each target of a workspace member that `cargo metadata` says is tested (`test: true`). [ADR 0070] For Go, the `_test.go` files of each package `go test` tests, as `go list -deps -test` names them (`TestGoFiles`, `XTestGoFiles`); the unit is the package, named by its directory from the root, `.` for the root. [ADR 0071] For Vitest, the test files its `include` and `exclude` name for every project, as its adapter listed them in the run that wrote the check's map (C-33): a file no run listed is a source.
- **sources**: for an observed adapter, a file of a kind it observes being read, `.py` for pytest, [ADR 0071] `.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.jsx`, `.ts`, `.mts`, `.cts` and `.tsx` for Vitest; for a compiler-given adapter, a `.rs` file that some rustc dep-info file of COMMAND's build names, [ADR 0070] or a `.go` file that `go list` names as part of a listed package of the repository (`GoFiles`, `CgoFiles`, a test variant's `GoFiles`). Another file a build names, such as an `include_str!` target or an embedded file, is not a source: a test may open it.
- **rest**: every other file, whatever its kind.

A file the runner collects that the rule doesn't name is still recorded as a unit, under its path. A unit's own file outside the scope makes it `not observed` (C-32).

**C-31 The unit key.** A unit's key is the SHA-256 of the JCS form (RFC 8785) of:

```json
{"schema": "hushrun.unit-key/v0", "check": "<NAME>", "command": ["<COMMAND>", …], "cwd": "<dir>", "scope": "<scope>", "unit": "<path>", "reads": "sha256:<hex>", "rest": "sha256:<hex>", "shape": "sha256:<hex>"}
```

- `check`, `command`, `cwd` and `scope` are the run key's (C-9), and so are `external` and `counts_from` when the run key has them; `digest` isn't in it.
- `reads` is the source digest (D-4, D-5) of the units and sources the unit read in the run that recorded it, itself included: each path with its kind and content hash.
- `rest` is the source digest of the rest set. An empty set digests the manifest's header line alone, `hushrun-source-v1\n`, so `sha256:0bc5283b0122b17c7afdfc307dd1ff1f27206b60b07097e1652bfa3e5b008a2d` (D-6's "an empty scope is an error" is about scopes).
- `shape` is the SHA-256 of the sources set's paths, their names and not their contents: each path's UTF-8 bytes followed by one NUL byte, sorted by bytes as D-4 sorts them, each once.

It holds no machine, OS, branch, commit or time. For example, the JCS form `{"check":"unit","command":["pytest"],"cwd":"","reads":"sha256:f9ff6a0b6c0010a2e5de57c58f0b4486a0860ceaaf568ad9b7d5e484b9bb7f55","rest":"sha256:0e5dd440aedb46c67c94ada9bb16a1ab071611b9d309798387b00287cafcecbc","schema":"hushrun.unit-key/v0","scope":"default","shape":"sha256:bc9bfee412d783a547d7a117a5c8f0c6ca2bf2a10a75ed71ee934f07149dc8e5","unit":"tests/test_cart.py"}` has the key `sha256:e1356f6ee33ab0c42b7c0b0df486822f5d96fc46a341f8f7f9ee9fd21c959f1c`. `testdata/digest/unit-keys.json` holds that vector with its files, and three more (an empty rest set, `external` and `counts_from`, non-ASCII paths), and `tools/refdigest.py unit-keys` recomputes them.

A run proves what the runner executed: Python's own bytecode cache, which compares a source's whole-second time and its size, can make a run execute the previous bytes of a file edited twice within one second to the same size, and hushrun doesn't defend against it. What **read** means: a unit read a file when it imported it, directly or through any chain of imports, or opened it, at any point, while the runner collected it, in its fixtures or in its tests; never "executed a line of it". For an observed adapter: a module another unit loaded first is still on this unit's list; a file opened from a frame of a repository module is an edge of that module, and reaches every unit that imports it; a repository module that code outside the repository loaded first is read by the unit running then and by every unit after it; and the `conftest.py` and `__init__.py` of every directory from the root to the unit are read. For a compiler-given adapter: what the test binary's own dep-info names; what the dep-info of each workspace library it may link names, which is its package's path dependencies of any kind, then theirs other than dev-dependencies, closed; and, for an integration test, a bench or an example, what every target of its package was built from, since it may run them.

**C-32 Adapters and `HUSHRUN_UNITS`.** Before COMMAND starts, hushrun makes a directory of its own for the run, `<cache dir>/units/runs/<ULID>`, names it in `HUSHRUN_UNITS` in COMMAND's environment, and writes there `hushrun.json`, `{"schema": "hushrun.units/v0", "root": "<the repository's root>", "reuse": [<units an adapter may deselect>]}`, `reuse` empty but in a plan (C-34), [ADR 0069] which adds `pythonFiles`, pytest's unit rule as hushrun read it (C-30), and `binaries`, `[{"unit", "path", "sha256"}]`, each listed test binary's path and the SHA-256 of its bytes; [ADR 0071] and `testFiles`, the test files the plan classified as units, for Vitest; [ADR 0077] and `unit` and `reads`, the check's `tests.unit` and `tests.reads` as this run takes them (C-39), and `reuseTests`, `{"<unit>": ["<id>", …]}`, the tests an adapter may deselect one by one, empty but in a plan; an adapter that reads none of these three deselects only what `reuse` lists. It removes the directory however the run ends; one a stopped hushrun left is removed a day after it was made. With `tests` absent or `off`, results off (C-11), `--rev` or `--digest`, hushrun makes nothing and sets nothing; with the last two it says `hushrun: <NAME>: test files aren't observed: --rev and --digest name other files than these`.

- **Which adapter.** A COMMAND `cargo [+toolchain] nextest run …` or `cargo [+toolchain] test …`, not `--doc` nor `--no-run`, gets the Cargo adapter; [ADR 0070] `go test …`, the Go adapter; [ADR 0071] one run of Vitest, the Vitest adapter (C-35); [ADR 0082] one run of Jest, the Jest adapter (C-44); [ADR 0085] `mvn …` or `mvnw …`, the Maven adapter (C-47); [ADR 0084] `gradle …` or `gradlew …`, the Gradle adapter (C-46); [ADR 0087] the COMMANDs C-49 lists, the CTest adapter; [ADR 0086] the COMMANDs C-48 lists, the `swift test` adapter; any other the pytest adapter. They are tried in that order, the first that takes COMMAND wins, and the CTest and `swift test` adapters come last since they also take wrapper COMMANDs (below); [ADR 0077, amended] a COMMAND they would take stays the pytest adapter's when this machine's store (C-36) says pytest last reported the check's test files, as it did before they took it (a `ctest` whose tests are pytest's), and the run, `hushrun ready` and `hushrun why` all go by it; a hint isn't asked before the adapter is chosen, so a machine whose store doesn't hold the check gives such a COMMAND to CTest or `swift test`; the word keeps them away only while pytest reports: a run so kept in which pytest wrote no facts at all forgets the check, word and map, in the store's write after the run's record, and says once `hushrun: <NAME>: test files weren't observed: pytest reported none, so the next run chooses how to observe them afresh`: a COMMAND that starts Cargo, Go, Vitest, Jest, Maven or Gradle by name is theirs in a Swift package too, and `ctest` is the CTest adapter's. A **wrapper** COMMAND is a shell's `-c` line (`sh`, `bash`, `zsh`, `dash`, `ksh`), a script with a `#!` line that COMMAND names by its path (its first 256 KiB), or `make` or `gmake` with its Makefile (`GNUmakefile`, `makefile` or `Makefile`, in `-C`'s directory or COMMAND's); it goes to an adapter that loads through `PATH` (CTest's, `swift test`'s) only when its own text starts that adapter's program by name (`ctest`; `swift test` or `swift-test`) and names no other runner with an adapter: `pytest`, `py.test`, `tox` or `nox` (the pytest adapter's, which any other wrapper keeps), or the other program on `PATH`. A program is known by its name, its file name without its directory and without `.exe`, `.cmd` or `.bat` in any case, [ADR 0077, amended] and on Windows, whose file system ignores case, compared in lower case (`MVN.CMD` is `mvn`). A COMMAND that starts `env NAME=value …` is recognized by what follows the assignments (the key still holds COMMAND as written), [ADR 0077, amended] and every tool an adapter starts for it (Cargo's plan build, `cargo metadata` and `rustc -vV`, `go env` and `go list`, `swift package describe`, CTest's listing) gets those assignments over hushrun's environment, as COMMAND's program does, so that `env GOARCH=386 go test` is known to test for another target; with an option of `env`'s own (`-i`, `-u`, …) it is recognized as `env`. Where an adapter can't load safely hushrun loads none and says, once, `hushrun: <NAME>: test files aren't observed: <why>`: for pytest, a COMMAND that runs tox or nox, or a Python with `-I` or `-E`; for Cargo, a runner that a Cargo configuration file (`.cargo/config.toml` or `.cargo/config` from COMMAND's directory up, or in Cargo's home) or COMMAND sets, or a hushrun whose path holds a space; [ADR 0070, 0071] for Go and Vitest, what C-35 lists.

Below Python 3.8 the plugin does nothing. [ADR 0069] **Deselecting.** After collection and every other deselection, the plugin removes the tests of the units `reuse` lists and tells pytest they were deselected (`pytest_deselected`); a unit all of whose tests it removed, one or more, is `deselected` in its facts, and `removedItems` counts them. Only one process may: the pytest that first starts with the directory claims it (a file `claim`, made exclusively, holding its pid), and a pytest-xdist worker whose parent is that pid; and only while pytest's `python_files` is `pythonFiles`. Any other pytest, nested or later, deselects nothing. An error of its own while it deselects deselects nothing. When pytest would exit 5 (no tests collected) and that process, or its workers, removed any test, the session's status is 0: a failure or an error gives another status first. A test finished only once the report of its teardown was logged and no `pytest.exit` or interrupt came up through its run: a test cut short counts as not finished, for its unit's `finished` too, so its file didn't run whole. A fixture of module, class, package or session scope whose end fails failed everything it served: pytest reports the error on whichever test ran last, so the plugin notes which fixtures each test was served (by definition where pytest says, else by name), and no test the failing fixture served counts as finished, in its file or another; none records a pass, and their files didn't run whole.

The runner starts the test binary with what its environment would have been (that variable as the environment had it, no `HUSHRUN_UNITS`), passes stdio, the exit code, and SIGINT, SIGTERM, SIGHUP and SIGQUIT through, and ends by the test's signal when the test did; a `--list`'s output goes on as it comes. Every artifact must be fresh, within 120 s: else Cargo is stopped and no unit is observed, and the run says why.

Files that say no `start` are one start. pytest sends it. The target runner writes `"invocation": {"binary", "args", "listed", "exit", "signal", "durationMs"}` per start, `listed` the tests a `--list` printed. [ADR 0077] An adapter's file may be `hushrun.unit-facts/v1`, which adds to each unit `tests`, `[{"id", "finished", "failed", "deselected", "durationMs", "ran", "opened", "dirs", "notFollowed"}]`, the tests this process ran or deselected, `notFollowed` being what wasn't followed inside the test's own window and the unit's `notFollowed` what was outside every window; `loaded`, `{"ran", "opened"}`; and `shared`, `[{"tests", "ran", "opened"}]`, `tests` indexes into its `tests`; and to the file `paths`, the paths from the root that `ran`, `opened`, `dirs` and `imports` name by index, and `imports`, `{"<index>": [<index>, …]}`, each file's direct imports. An adapter that knows tests and not what each read sends `id`, `finished`, `failed`, `deselected` and `durationMs`. A file whose `tests` don't read is not read; a `v0` file reads as it always did. [ADR 0077, amended] And each test's `skipped`, `true` for one that finished without its body running, and each unit's `skipped`, how many of its finished tests were, for a unit told without its tests; pytest sends both (skip, skipif, a skip raised in setup or in the test, an xfail that isn't run, and a test file skipped whole as it loads, `pytest.skip(allow_module_level=True)` or `pytest.importorskip`, as one skip of its unit; an xfail that ran isn't skipped).

So a skip in a run that failed and a failure no test explains are `not run` even where something wasn't followed, and a failure is a failure whatever else holds. A unit with no test, or whose tests were all skipped or deselected, ran and passed. For a test that didn't finish, the adapter's `notKept` is said in place of the rule's.
