A start that exited otherwise failed it. A binary of the build no start ran is `not run`, and one whose dep-info, or that of a target it read, isn't there `not observed`. Doctests are no unit.
- **Convergence.** No adapter, or one that wrote nothing: everything ran, and the check is recorded with no `units`. A signal that stops COMMAND, or files that changed while it ran: nothing is recorded, for the check or any unit, as C-10 says. [ADR 0077, amended] An adapter that learns after COMMAND that its runner left out something the plan didn't list says so, with why in words people read (its `read`'s `left_out`, or a facts file's `leftOutUnlisted`): hushrun says `hushrun: <NAME>: running it once more with nothing left out: its adapter left out what the plan didn't list (<why>)`, starts COMMAND once more in a directory of its own whose `hushrun.json` lists nothing, and decides everything, its exit code, its units and the check's result, from that start alone; [ADR 0077, amended] only when the start's plan listed something to leave out: in `observe`, or with a plan that listed nothing, another start would be the same, so no unit is recorded, the run says once `hushrun: <NAME>: test files weren't observed: its adapter left out what nothing listed (<why>)`, and COMMAND's exit code stands. [ADR 0077, amended] An adapter that knows the runner ran what was built before the files now (a stale program) says so, with why in words people read (its `read`'s `stale_build`, or a facts file's `staleBuild`): when COMMAND passed, nothing is recorded, for the check or any unit, the run says once `hushrun: <NAME> passed, but it isn't recorded: <why>`, and its last line promises nothing of the next run; when COMMAND failed, its fail is recorded as usual. When that start can't be set up, or says the same again, nothing is recorded for the check or any unit, hushrun says `hushrun: <NAME> wasn't recorded: …` with why, and exits with that start's exit code, or 1 for 0.

**C-33 Unit results.** `ran and passed` records `pass` and `ran and failed` `fail` under the unit's key, computed from what it read in this run, with its duration; no other state records anything. Unit results go with the run that executed them, and only once that run's own result was recorded (C-10): [ADR 0069] with a server, in the request that records the run, `POST {base}/v0/runs/{key}` (C-12), [ADR 0078] at most 50,000 a run, failures first, as C-12 sends them (an older server keeps the first 5,000), and a server from before it records the run alone and keeps none, said once, `hushrun: <NAME>: test files' results weren't kept: <org> doesn't keep them yet, so no test file is reused`; on this machine, `<cache dir>/results/unit-runs/v0/<run key>/<ULID>.json` holding `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-run/v0", "runKey", "run": <the run's result>, "units": [{"key", "result", "durationMs"}]}`, and one `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-result/v0", "result", "durationMs", "run"}` per unit in `<cache dir>/results/units/v0/<unit key>/<ULID>.json`; both are swept as this machine's results are (C-11). Never a test's name or a path is sent to a server: keys are hashes.

- **When a unit's pass counts.** Exactly when the result of the run it went with would count for this run: its `job`, `variant` and `provider` (C-10, C-14) and its key's `counts_from` (C-25), by the same function. The newest that counts decides. [ADR 0069] Lookups are one batch for every key a run asks about; a server answers each key's newest result among those whose run counts for the caller, with that run's result, which hushrun checks again.
- **What `observe` says.** For each unit, the keys the plan would compute: from the lists C-38 gives, this machine's map and [ADR 0075] the hints a plan would read (for Cargo, from this build), unless `tests.always` names it. When C-38's rule would reuse it, `tests: reuse` would have reused it. Before the run's last line: ``hushrun: <NAME>: with `tests: reuse`, <N> of <M> test files would have run; <K> already passed on the files they read`` (`it read` for one), then `, saving about <time>` when their durations add up to a second or more; or `…, all <M> test files would have run`, then `: <file> changed, and any test may read it` (`is new`, `is gone, and any test may have read it`) for the first file of the rest set that differs from the map's, else `: <file> is new, which can change what tests load` (`is gone`) for the first source added or removed, else `: none had passed here yet on the files it read` without a map. With units `not observed`, it adds `; hushrun couldn't follow all that <n> of them read (<what>), so they would always run`, [ADR 0078] `<n>` counting tests where the line counts tests (C-39). Each unit that would have been reused and ran and failed is a disagreement: ``hushrun: <NAME>: <unit> failed, though it passed before on the same files it read: with `tests: reuse` it would have been reused``, kept in the check's audit.
- **The map and the audit.** Per check, by its id, the SHA-256 of the JCS form of `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-map-id/v0", "check", "command", "cwd", "scope", "repository"}`, [ADR 0070] `repository` as the reuse state's id has it (C-34), so that two repositories with one check and one command on one machine keep their maps apart. [ADR 0072] Both are in this machine's store (C-36). The map holds, for each unit that ran, the units and sources it read, each with its kind and content hash then, and what wasn't followed, [ADR 0075] and for each unit reused on a list another machine saw, that list (C-38); it keeps other units while their file is in the scope ([ADR 0074] for a Go package, while a file is below its directory), and holds the run's rest set (each path's kind and content hash) and sources; it is a hint that chooses which files a key hashes, never trusted. The audit keeps the newest 200 disagreements. Neither is written when COMMAND's files changed, the run didn't count (C-25), or nothing was recorded.

**C-34 Reuse, audit runs and the reuse state.** [ADR 0069] For a check with `tests: reuse` whose own key missed (C-10), `hushrun run NAME -- COMMAND` doesn't run again the test files that already passed on the files they read.

- **The plan**, before COMMAND, on the scope's files as they are (the ones the run's key is for; otherwise nothing is planned). For pytest, the units are the unit rule's (C-30), and each one's keys are computed from the lists C-38 gives, what the check's map says it read (C-33) and [ADR 0075] what other machines saw: hints, never trusted, since a wrong list makes a key no run recorded. [ADR 0074] Until pytest has reported the check's test files on this machine, or [ADR 0075] on another whose hint this one read, the pytest adapter's plan says nothing and lists nothing: a COMMAND hushrun doesn't recognize may never start pytest. A store that can't be read (C-36) is no map: every test file runs, said once. For Cargo, hushrun first makes COMMAND's build, `cargo test --no-run --message-format=json --locked` with COMMAND's options that choose what Cargo builds, Cargo's progress on stderr, so that COMMAND's own build finds it done; the units are its test binaries, and the keys come from its dep-info (C-31). One batch lookup (C-33), [ADR 0075] in pages of 5,000 keys, asks for the newest result that counts here under every key, with the check's reuse state. A unit runs when no list is known for it, `tests.always` names it, it was `not observed` (or, for Cargo, can't be), C-38's rule doesn't reuse it (no list's key has a pass, or one has a fail), or it is audited; every other is listed in `hushrun.json`'s `reuse`, and a binary with its path and SHA-256 (C-32).
- **Audit runs.** Of the units that could be reused, 2%, rounded up and at least one, run anyway: those whose SHA-256 of `<key hex>\n<the run's start, RFC 3339>` is lowest. So a run in `reuse` always runs at least one unit that could have been reused.
- **What people read**, before COMMAND: `hushrun: <NAME>: <K> of <M> test files run; <R> already passed on the files they read, and <A> more that did run as a check` (`it read`, `runs`, for one); `hushrun: <NAME>: all <M> test files run: <why>`, with ADR 0068's reasons (C-33's `observe` line); `all <M> test files run; <A> that already passed on the files it read runs as a check`; or `hushrun: <NAME>: every test file runs: <why>` when nothing can be reused: `--force runs it whole`; a job whose variants or reports stop a skip (C-14, C-26); `nothing here can read which passed`; `can't read which passed (<why>)`, for a lookup that failed, `its server doesn't keep test files' results yet` for one that answered 404 or 405, and a suspension that waits to reach the server; `the build failed`; more than 5,000 test files; or the suspension below. With no test file, it says nothing. Its last line says what reuse saved (C-27).
- **Deciding.** A unit is `reused` only when the plan listed it and every process of the adapter that saw it deselected it (C-32); reuse never chains, so it records nothing. A run that passed with reused units is a pass (C-10): the same files skip the whole check next time.
- **A second run.** A unit that had a pass that counted, audited or listed and run all the same, and ran and failed, runs once more after COMMAND: COMMAND as written, again, in a directory of its own whose `hushrun.json` lists every other unit the first run saw, so that only those run, its output as COMMAND's. Before it: `hushrun: <NAME>: <unit> failed, though it passed before on the same files it read: running it once more`. `ran and passed` then: `hushrun: <NAME>: <unit> failed, then passed when it ran again: it may be flaky`, and nothing else; the first run's `fail` is what goes under its key, so the next run runs it. Any other state, a second run that can't be set up or that a signal stops included, is a disagreement. The run keeps COMMAND's exit code and verdict.
- **A disagreement**, once the files are known not to have changed while either ran (C-14), is kept in the check's audit (C-33) and suspends the check's reuse for the repository: ``hushrun: <NAME>: <unit> failed again, though it passed before on the same files it read: reuse of <NAME>'s test files is suspended until a person turns it on again with `hushrun tests resume <NAME>` ``.
- **The reuse state** of a check, by its NAME and the repository the run names (C-10, lowercase, `""` for none): `on`, the default, or `suspended`, with who and when, and `generation`, the transitions it went through. It has one home: with a server, the server's (`POST {base}/v0/units/lookup` reads it, `POST {base}/v0/units/reuse` writes it, C-12); in local mode, `<cache dir>/units/reuse/<id>.json`, the id the SHA-256 of the JCS form of `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-reuse-id/v0", "repository", "check"}`, written whole under a lock beside it. Suspending a suspended check changes nothing; a file that doesn't read is suspended. A suspension that can't reach the server is written first to `<cache dir>/units/outbox/`, whole, and the next plan for that check sends it before anything else; while one waits, nothing is reused. [ADR 0070] A server that answers a 5xx or 429 after L-4's retries wasn't reached: what it was sent waits. While the check is suspended, every test file runs: ``hushrun: <NAME>: every test file runs: reuse of its test files is suspended by <who> on <when>, since <unit> failed twice though it had passed on the same files it read; a person turns it on again with `hushrun tests resume <NAME>` ``, and `units` says `ranAnyway: suspended` (C-10).
- **`hushrun tests resume NAME`** turns it on again, for people: with a coding agent (C-23) it exits 2, `error: a person turns reuse of <NAME>'s test files on again, …`. It needs no `hushrun.yaml`, and reads the state where results go (C-11), from the repository it runs in: on already, `Reuse of <NAME>'s test files is on for <repository>.`; else it says who suspended it, when, and for which test file where it knows, and turns it on naming the generation it read: `Turned it on again: the next run of <NAME> reuses test files that passed.` On already by someone else: it says so, exit 0. Suspended again since: exit 1, and it stays. A server takes a personal token only for it.
- **Limits.** A wrong reuse needs a pass recorded under the same key, which needs a run that read the same files with the same contents: what an adapter doesn't see (a file outside the scope, an environment, the network, the clock) is what audit runs are for, and they catch it only when they choose it. A unit that passes only after another ran may fail when that one is reused; its second run fails too, and reuse is suspended.

**C-35 Go and Vitest.** [ADR 0070, 0071] The Go adapter is compiler-given, the Vitest adapter observed; each keeps COMMAND byte for byte as written.

- **Go.** For `go test [flags] [packages] [-args …]`, hushrun adds `'-exec=<hushrun> __hushrun-test-runner go <N> <runner word>…'` last to `GOFLAGS`, after what `go env GOFLAGS` says, chained to an `-exec` it holds; the runner starts each test binary in its package's directory with `-test.testlogfile`, `GOFLAGS` as COMMAND's environment had it and no `HUSHRUN_UNITS`, and doesn't start one whose directory `hushrun.json` lists. With `-exec`, Go's test cache neither stores nor finds a result; a package with no start is `not run`. A unit read every file `go list -e -deps -test -json` says its test binary is built from, of the packages of the repository in its `Deps` (asked with COMMAND's `-tags`, `-race`, `-msan`, `-asan`, `-mod`, `-modfile` and `-C`, in its environment, before COMMAND for the plan and after it for the record), and what Go's test log says its tests opened or looked at in the repository, with every file below a directory they listed. A plan keys it by what it is built from now and what the map says it read last time, [ADR 0075] or with no map, by what it is built from alone and by what hints say it read (C-38). It is `not observed` with a `TestMain` (the test main `go list` writes calls one), the repository's cgo, SWIG or `.syso` code in its packages, a package `go list` can't load, a program it started (an `open` of an executable file in the log; [ADR 0074] a relative `open` followed at once by a `stat` of a directory from which it names an executable file, or of a directory that is gone, as `os.StartProcess` logs a program `exec.Cmd.Dir` starts; and an `open` of a file gone by the time the log is read right after an `open` of `/dev/null`, as `exec.Cmd` opens it for a child's standard streams), or no log from a start that exited 0. Not offered, said once: `-c`, `-exec`, `-fuzz`, `-overlay`, `-toolexec`, `-compiler`, `-buildmode`, `-linkshared`, `.go` files as arguments, a flag hushrun doesn't know, a `GOOS` or `GOARCH` other than the host's, or a `GOFLAGS` or hushrun path with a quote. [ADR 0083] With `tests.unit: test`, each test one by one, by C-45; with COMMAND's own `-run` or `-skip`, by package.
- **Vitest.** For one run, `vitest run` or `vitest --run`, however started (`npx`, `pnpm [exec]`, `yarn exec`, `bunx`, `bun x`, `npm exec`, a path), hushrun writes its plugin to `<cache dir>/adapters/vitest-<hash>/` and names it, a `file:` URL, in `HUSHRUN_VITEST`, which the repository's Vitest config loads with one line `hushrun init` proposes (C-15): `plugins: [process.env.HUSHRUN_VITEST && import(process.env.HUSHRUN_VITEST).then((m) => m.default())]`. A config Vitest would load without `HUSHRUN_VITEST` in it, or none, loads nothing: `hushrun: <NAME>: test files aren't observed: Vitest loads hushrun's adapter from one line in <config>, which `hushrun init` proposes: `<line>``. The first Vitest process to start with the run's directory claims it. The plugin puts its setup file first in each project's `setupFiles`, appends to each module Vite makes outside `node_modules` one statement that records its id when it runs, removes in the sequencer, after Vitest's filters and `--shard`, the test files `hushrun.json` lists when Vitest's own list is its `testFiles` and coverage is off, and sets `passWithNoTests` only when that left none of a list that had some; its reporter writes how each test file ended. A unit read the modules its worker ran, the files it opened, read or looked at through `fs`, every file below a directory it listed, and the config files (`configFileDependencies`). It is `not observed` with a child process, a worker thread, a native addon, a module a plugin made from no file, no word from the setup file, or, for its project, a pool other than `forks` or `threads`, `isolate: false`, browser mode, typecheck, a `globalSetup`, an environment or runner of the repository's, CSS processing, a Vite plugin hushrun doesn't know, or a Vitest other than 4. Not offered, said once: `vitest` without `run`, `watch`, `bench`, `list`, `--watch`, `--typecheck`, `--browser`, `--ui`. With `--coverage` every test file runs. A plan's units are the map's list (C-30), [ADR 0075] else the newest hint's (C-38); with none yet, every test file runs. [ADR 0081] With `tests.unit: test` the plugin also writes `vitest-tests` in the run's directory, whole, before any worker starts: `unit`, `watch` (`reads: test` and coverage off), the repository's root, the config files, and `reuseTests` only when it would remove test files, else `{}`; its setup file tells each test and leaves out the listed ones (C-43).

**C-36 This machine's store of what test files read.** [ADR 0072] One SQLite file per repository, `<cache dir>/units/<id>.v2.sqlite`, the id the SHA-256 of the JCS form of `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-store-id/v1", "repository"}`, the repository as the run names it (C-10), lowercase, `""` for none; in WAL mode, with `PRAGMA application_id` `0x68757368` and `PRAGMA user_version` `2`, its schema's version, as in its name. [ADR 0077] Schema 2 adds, for each unit, its tests (C-39): each test's id, its last state (`passed`, `failed`, `not-observed` or `not-run`; a test reused keeps the row it stands on), its duration and what wasn't followed inside it; [C-37, amended] for a `not-run` test, or a `passed` one that keeps no pass of its own (`neverLeftOut`), why it kept no result (`not_kept`, a column a `CHECK` ties to those two states): the adapter's `notKept` for a test that didn't finish, else the sentence of the rule of C-32's one function that decided it; and each unit's row, `seen here` only, why it kept no result of its own when it last ran here, by that same function (`not_kept`, a column a `CHECK` ties to `seen here`), which `hushrun why` says (C-37); and lists a test read with `reads: test`, one row per distinct list, which tests share; [ADR 0078] a test's row names its list (`list_ref`), and a list no test names is pruned. An adapter is named by its word, 1 to 32 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`. A `v1` file is left for an older hushrun; [ADR 0077, amended] when there is no `v2` file and a `v1` file is hushrun's store of schema 1, a run with `tests` on makes the `v2` file from a copy of it before anything reads the store, every row of schema 1 copied into schema 2's tables in one transaction, whole or not at all (schema 2 is schema 1's tables, with the adapter's column taking any word, and its tests' tables), the new file taking its name only when no other hushrun made one meanwhile; in CI it says once ``hushrun: <NAME>: what its test files read was taken from <file>, which an older hushrun keeps: a CI cache step that names `units/*.v1.sqlite` restores a file no run updates, so name `units/*.sqlite` in it instead``. `hushrun ready` and `hushrun why`, which write nothing, never import; [ADR 0077, amended] they read the adapter word C-32 chooses by from the `v1` file, read only, when there is no `v2` file, so they choose as the run does. It holds, per check id (C-33), the map, the audit's disagreements, the check's name, command, cwd and scope, and the direct edges its adapter saw (C-37); [ADR 0075] each unit's row says where it came from, `seen here` or `shared` (a list another machine saw, which reused it here, C-38), in a column with a `CHECK`, and a `shared` row the system whose machine saw it, `linux`, `macos` or `windows`, in a column a `CHECK` ties to the first (a `seen here` row has none); and the content id of each hint this machine sent or received, by its address and system, the newest 200 of a check. Never a unit result, the reuse state or the outbox (C-33, C-34), which stay files. Its tables are `STRICT`, with foreign keys and `CHECK`s.

- **Writing.** A run writes once, after it recorded (C-33), in one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction after a busy wait of at most 5 s: the units it saw replace their rows, a `shared` one included, [ADR 0075] the units it reused on a hint's list are kept `shared`, the hints it read are noted, the check's sets are this run's, the edges from every file a unit that ran read are this run's, its disagreements join the audit, and a check no run wrote for 30 days goes, with what only it needed. [ADR 0075] Each write, the note of a hint sent included, ends by checkpointing the WAL into the file when no reader holds it, so a run that ends leaves the store whole in `<id>.v2.sqlite`. The first to make the file holds `<file>.lock` while it does. A write that can't happen is dropped: the next run may reuse less, nothing else.
- **Reading.** A plan, `observe`'s line, `hushrun ready` and `hushrun why` read it; the last two open it without creating it and query only, and write nothing (ADR 0061). A store missing reads as no map. One busy past the wait, not a SQLite database, damaged, or with other ids reads as no map too, and the run says once `hushrun: <NAME>: every test file runs: this machine's record of what its test files read can't be read (<why>), so the next run here starts it again` (in `observe`, ``with `tests: reuse`, every test file would have run: …``); [ADR 0077, amended] one whose tables aren't the ones this hushrun makes (an older build of the same schema) can't be read either; the next run that writes sets it aside as `<file>.unread` (the newest one kept), with its `-wal` and `-shm` gone, and makes it again, as it does a store any write to fails for anything but busy, once, in the one place that writes. Nothing about it fails a run or causes a reuse.
- **The sweep** (C-11) leaves this schema's files alone and removes any other file directly in `<cache dir>/units/` that no run touched for 30 days. A cache CI keeps between jobs (RFC 0003 D3) keeps `<cache dir>/units/*.sqlite`, saved once hushrun has exited.

**C-37 `hushrun why NAME UNIT [-- COMMAND]`.** [ADR 0073] What a run of the check would do with one unit, for the files on disk now, and why, from what this machine knows. It needs no `hushrun.yaml` beyond what a run needs, works in any directory of the repository (UNIT is from there or from the root; for Go, a package's directory or one of its `_test.go` files), and writes nothing (ADR 0061). COMMAND, cwd and scope are the check's newest run's in the store (C-36), with a note when the check ran with more than one command; or COMMAND after `--`, with `--scope` when there are several.

- **One answer**, a line `<unit> runs: <why>` or `<unit> would be reused: <why>` (in `observe`, `would run with `tests: reuse`` and `would be reused with `tests: reuse``), then lines indented by two, from the first that holds: `tests` off; the adapter not offered for COMMAND (C-32's why); not a unit of the check (outside the scope, in `tests.ignore`, or not named by the unit rule, C-30); `tests.always` names it; never seen run here, [ADR 0075] nor by another machine whose hint it read; `not observed` the last time it ran here, with what couldn't be followed; the check's reuse suspended (C-34's sentence); C-38's rule reuses it: reused, and an audit run may still run it, and when the list is another machine's, `it passed on the files another machine saw it read, and none of them has changed since`, with `another machine saw it read <files>`; a key's newest result failed (`machines saw it read different files, and its newest result on one of those lists failed` with more than one list); with no entry here and no result under another machine's list, `this machine has never seen it run, and no pass that counts here matches what another machine saw it read`; else, with no result under its key, the first of: a file it read changed or is gone, named, with a path of direct edges from the unit to it when the store holds one (for Cargo, that its test binary is built from it), or a file new below a directory it listed; a file of the rest set changed, new or gone, or a source new or gone, so that every unit runs; or no pass that counts here for what it read, unchanged since it last ran here.
- **What it reads.** The keys are the plan's (C-31, C-34), from the lists C-38 gives: the map, and [ADR 0075] with results on a server, the hints a run would read (for Go, by the sources of its last run here). Whether a key passed, failed, or the check is suspended comes from one batch lookup of results (C-33), as `hushrun ready`'s (C-22), counted as a run here counts a pass, sending nothing that waits in the outbox. When the lookup can't answer, and for Cargo, Go and Vitest, whose units and what each is built from are those of the last build or run here, a line says the answer is as far as this machine knows.
- **Edges.** The pytest adapter writes in its file (C-32) `edges`, `[from, to, kind]` between the repository's files: `import`, `open`, and `read` for a unit that read a file through no edge of its own; the Vitest adapter the imports of Vite's module graph; the Go adapter each package's imports, by their directories, and the files each package's tests opened. A path is the shortest through files the unit read, imports and opens first. No key reads an edge.
- [ADR 0078] **A test.** With `tests.unit: test` (C-39), UNIT may be `<test file>::<test id>`: the answer is for that test, named so, from its test file's reasons first (not a unit, `tests.always`, not observed, never seen run, suspended; its test file passed whole on the files it read: reused with it), then the second tier's, by the lists C-40 gives, hints' included: `not observed` the last time it ran here; [C-37, amended] `not run` the last time it ran here, or a pass it keeps none of, with the reason its run recorded (C-32's one function's, or the adapter's `notKept`, C-36), which it says as it was recorded, reason `not-kept`; never seen run here nor by another machine; a pass on its list (`another machine saw it read <files>` when the list is another's), with the audit's line for tests; a fail; else what changed among the files it read. With `tests.unit: file`, the answer is its test file's, with `its tests are reused with their test file: <why>`. [C-37, amended] A test file whose row says why it kept no result of its own the last time it ran here (C-32, C-36), with no pass that counts on what it read and nothing changed among it since, gets that reason, reason `not-kept`, in place of `no-pass`; a test file that kept nothing and has no row of its own here (one told without its tests, which a `not run` doesn't map) is still `never-ran-here`. A test file one of whose tests was `not observed` the last time it ran here isn't reused whole even when its key passed: ``<unit> runs: hushrun couldn't follow all that its test <id> read the last time it ran here (<what>), so it isn't reused whole``, reason `test-not-observed`; and each other test's answer adds `its test file isn't reused whole: …`.
- **Output.** Text on stdout; with `--format json`, `hushrun.why/v1`: `{"schema", "check", "command", "unit", [ADR 0078] "test": <id> | null, "decision": "runs" | "reused" | "not-a-test-file" | "off", "reason", "says", "detail": [...], "asFarAsThisMachineKnows", "file", "path": [{"file", "then": "import" | "open" | "read" | null}]}`. It exits 0 when it explains, 2 outside a git repository.

**C-38 Hints.** [RFC 0003 §3.11; ADR 0075] A hint is what this machine's store says a check's test files read (C-33, C-36), in a form another machine can fetch from a server (results-v0, Hints) to compute the same unit keys (C-31). It is never trusted: a wrong hint makes a key no run recorded, and that unit runs. It holds, for each unit whose reads were fully observed, the units and sources it read and the directories it listed ([ADR 0070]); for Vitest, the test files the runner listed ([ADR 0071]); and the adapter's word, 1 to 32 of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-`. No content hash, edge, rest set or result, and no path: every path is a token. [ADR 0079] A hint of version 1 also holds tests, their ids sealed (C-41).

- **The key.** HMAC-SHA-256 (RFC 2104) with the ASCII bytes `hushrun.unit-hints-key/v0` as its key, of the bytes `shape` hashes (C-31): each source path's UTF-8 bytes followed by one NUL byte, sorted by bytes, each once. It is computed from those bytes, not from the shape digest, and never leaves the machine.
- **A token.** The first 16 bytes of HMAC-SHA-256 of a path's UTF-8 bytes under the key.
- **The address** a hint is kept under: the SHA-256 of the JCS form of `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-hints-address/v0", "check": "sha256:<check id>", "shape": "sha256:<shape>"}`, the check id C-33's. It goes to the server; the key can't be derived from it.
- **The system.** `linux`, `macos` or `windows`, by the build's target. Any other system has none (`none` in a debug log) and shares no hints.
- **The bytes.** One zlib stream (RFC 1950), with no preset dictionary and nothing after its Adler-32, of the **content**, whose numbers are 32-bit unsigned big-endian: `hushrun.unit-hints/v0` and a line feed; the adapter word's length in one byte and the word; the count of tokens and the tokens, 16 bytes each, strictly ascending; the count of unit files and their indices into the tokens, strictly ascending; the count of units, and for each, ascending by its own index, its own index, then the count and indices of the files it read and of the directories it listed, each strictly ascending. Every index names a token and every token is named. So a hint has one content, and its **content id** is the content's SHA-256, whatever compressed it.
- **Bounds.** At most 2 MiB of bytes (2,097,152), 16 MiB of content once inflated (inflating stops there), 5,000 units, 5,000 unit files, 1,000,000 tokens and 2,000,000 entries (unit files, reads and directories together). A writer past one writes nothing and says the hint is too large; a reader checks each count against its bound and the bytes left before it allocates.
- **Reading.** A hint over a bound, not one zlib stream, of another schema, or whose content is cut, has bytes after its end, or isn't canonical, is not read, for a reason a debug log gives, and never a panic. Tokens are matched only by computing the token of each file of the scope the reader is given, and of each directory holding one (`""` and `"."` for the root): a read or a unit file matches a file, a listed directory a directory, a unit's own token either (a Go package is its directory). A unit with a token that matches nothing, or that two paths of the scope share, is dropped whole and runs; a unit file that does is left out of the list. What a reader yields names only the scope's files and their directories.
- **When a run asks for them.** [ADR 0075] Only where they can change something: a plan (C-34), `observe`'s line (C-33), `hushrun ready` (C-22) and `hushrun why` (C-37) of a check whose own key missed, or would, with `tests` `observe` or `reuse`, whose results go to a server (never this machine's directory, ADR 0067), whose adapter isn't Cargo's (its keys come from its build), on one of the three systems. One `POST {base}/v0/units/hints/lookup` (C-12) per system whose hints it reads. `hushrun ready` and `hushrun why` note nothing of what they read.
- **Whose.** This machine's system's (the plan's S3), never one pool of several: with no rule, and with a `counts_from` (C-25) that names this machine's system, among others or alone. Only where the rule names `os` and this machine's system isn't among them, the systems it names, one lookup each, in its order: only those leave results for the check, and a pass under a key skips on any machine. A machine always writes under its own system.
- **Where a run asks.** The address and the key, for pytest and Go, from the sources set, as `shape` is. For Vitest, whose list of test files a hint holds, from every file of a source kind, with no test file named, so that a new test file runs everything once (ADR 0071); the plan's units are then the map's list, else the newest hint's.
- **The lists.** One function gives the lists a unit's key may be made from, and the plan, `hushrun ready`, `observe` and `hushrun why` all call it: this machine's own entry when it has one (one `not observed` runs, whatever the hints say; a `shared` one only when its system is one this run reads, else as if there were none, so a list a rule borrowed keys nothing once the rule is gone), or for Go with none, what its test binary is built from now; then each hint's list for the unit, newest first; the same files once, and 3 lists at most. A list from a hint is its reads and every file now below a directory it listed, and for Go what its test binary is built from now. A key for each list from the files on disk now (C-31), and the batch lookup, in pages of 5,000 keys. **A unit may be reused when some list's key has a newest result that counts here and is a pass, and no list's key has one that is a fail**: two runs that saw it read different files and disagree is a reason to run. The audit share, `tests.always`, `not observed`, suspension and the 5,000 test files stay as C-34 has them.
- **Kept here.** A unit reused on a hint's list is kept in this machine's store as that list, `shared`, with the system it was asked for (C-36); the next run of the unit here replaces it with what it saw.
- **Sending.** After a run whose map was kept (only when its files didn't change while COMMAND ran) and whose result and units' results a server recorded (C-33), in `observe` and `reuse`: the hint, from the store, of every unit the run executed and fully observed (`ran and passed`, `ran and failed`) and every one it reused, with the list whose key hit when that list was seen here or came from this machine's own system (another system's list stays that system's), and for Vitest the store's list of test files, under this machine's system. When this machine already sent or received a hint with its content id for the address and system, nothing is sent; else one `PUT {base}/v0/units/hints/{sha256}` (C-12). The store notes a content id once the server kept it, and every one a run read; a `PUT` that fails notes nothing, so the next run of the check sends the hint again, from that note and nothing else. Never in local mode, never without a credential, never for a run that recorded nothing, such as one that doesn't meet `counts_from` (C-25). A hint the writer calls too large isn't sent, said once: `hushrun: <NAME>: what its test files read wasn't shared with other machines: it is too large (<n> <what>, more than <most>)`. Sending never fails a run: what goes wrong is logged for debug, as the store's errors are.
- **What converges.** A server that doesn't answer for hints: this machine's map alone, as without them. One that answers 404 or 405, from before hints: the same, nothing more is asked of it in that run, and the run says once, after it recorded, `hushrun: <NAME>: <org> doesn't keep what other machines saw its test files read yet, so this machine goes by what it saw itself`; not when it keeps no units' results either, which C-33 says once. A hint that can't be read, of another schema or too large: left out, the others used. One that names a file that is gone: that unit's list is dropped. One wrong, stale, hostile, or written under another key: its keys match no recorded result, or its tokens no file, and those units run. No hint and no map: pytest and Vitest run everything, and that run sends the first hint.

`testdata/digest/unit-hints.json` holds vectors of the key, tokens, address, content, content id and what a read gives, and bytes no reader takes; `tools/refdigest.py unit-hints` recomputes them.

**C-39 A test as the unit.** [ADR 0077] With `tests.unit: test` (C-5), a check reuses each test of a test file on its own, beside its test files. A **test** is one named test of a unit: the smallest thing its runner can leave out alone and report alone. Its id is the adapter's: for pytest, the node id after `<path>::`, parameters included; [ADR 0078] an item whose node id has no `::` has no id, and stays with its test file, which runs it whenever it isn't reused whole. Its **name**, everywhere a person reads it, is its unit, `::`, and its id: `tests/test_cart.py::TestCart::test_total[eur]`.

- **Its key** is the SHA-256 of the JCS form (RFC 8785) of C-31's fields, computed the same way, under `"schema": "hushrun.unit-key/v1"`, with `"test": "<id>"`: `{"schema": "hushrun.unit-key/v1", "check", "command", "cwd", "scope", "unit", "test", "reads", "rest", "shape"}`, and `external` and `counts_from` when the run key has them. With `reads: file`, a test read what its test file read, and `reads` digests that list; [ADR 0078] with `reads: test`, its own list (C-40). A test file's own key stays C-31's `v0`, unchanged. `testdata/digest/unit-keys.json` holds two such vectors, and `tools/refdigest.py unit-keys` recomputes them.
- **Which adapters.** One that knows tests (pytest; [ADR 0081] Vitest, C-43; [ADR 0082] Jest, C-44; [ADR 0083] Go, C-45; [ADR 0084] Gradle, C-46; [ADR 0085] Maven, C-47; [ADR 0086] `swift test`, C-48; [ADR 0087] CTest, C-49) reports each, and deselects the tests `reuseTests` lists (C-32). With one that knows none, the check reuses by test file and says once ``hushrun: <NAME>: its tests are reused by <test file>: <runner>'s tests can't be reused one by one yet``, `<test file>` being `test file`, `test binary` (Cargo) or `package` (Go). With `reads: test` and an adapter that can't watch a test, it says once ``hushrun: <NAME>: each test is taken to have read what its <test file> read: <runner> can't be watched test by test yet`` and runs with `reads: file`, `<test file>` there also being `test class` (Gradle, Maven), `test target` (`swift test`) or `test program` (CTest); [ADR 0078] one that can (pytest on Python 3.12 or later, C-42; Vitest, C-43; Jest, C-44) but didn't in a run says ``hushrun: <NAME>: each test is taken to have read what its test file read: <why>``, its facts' `settings.watch`.
- **States**, by C-32's one function, from a test's own facts: `ran and passed` when it finished and nothing unfollowed was in it, a skipped test included in a run whose COMMAND passed; [ADR 0077, amended] in one that failed, a skipped test is `not run`, and its test file records no pass of its own; `ran and failed` when it failed or errored in setup, body or teardown; `reused` when the plan listed it and every process that reported it deselected it; `not observed` when something wasn't followed inside its own window (its setup, body and teardown), or outside every window of its file (collecting or importing it, a fixture of module, class or session scope, a teardown that may end one, [ADR 0077, amended] and the end of such a fixture wherever its runner ends it, as pytest ends a parametrized one switching its value inside the next test's setup), which is every test's of the file; `not run` when it didn't finish, [ADR 0077, amended] or when its test file failed while none of its tests failed (a failing `afterAll`, a crash between tests: a failure no test explains, so no test's pass would bring it back). A test file's own state is decided as before, with what wasn't followed inside its tests, and is `not run` when some of its tests were reused one by one: it didn't run whole.
- **Two tiers.** A plan asks each test file's own key first (C-34): a pass reuses all of it. A test file one of whose tests was `not observed` the last time it ran here isn't reused whole, [ADR 0077, amended] whatever `tests.unit` is now: a check switched back to `unit: file` keeps the guard that test's row holds, until the file runs whole again. For each other test file, not named by `tests.always`, whose lists are known (C-38), each test this machine's store has for it, but one that was `not observed`, [ADR 0079] and each a hint holds of it (C-41), is keyed by each of its lists (C-40) with its id, in one batch lookup, in pages of 5,000 keys; C-38's rule reuses it. A test reused on a list another machine saw keeps that pass as its row here. A run records a test file that passed whole under its own key, as before, and nothing more with `reads: file`; [ADR 0078] with `reads: test`, each of its tests too; one that didn't run or pass whole records each of its tests that `ran and passed` or `ran and failed` under the test's key, and its own `fail` when one of them failed. The keys of a test file some of whose tests were reused hold what it read in this run and what the store said it read when it last ran here.
- **Audits.** Of the tests the plan could reuse one by one, 2%, rounded up and at least one, run anyway, those whose SHA-256 of `<key hex>\n<the run's start>` is lowest. A test that had a pass that counted, audited or listed and run all the same, and ran and failed, runs once more after COMMAND, as C-34's second run, with its file not in `reuse` and every other test of it the first run saw in `reuseTests`. Its outcome, and a disagreement, are C-34's, by its name.
- **What people read.** Before COMMAND, when the store knows every test file's tests: ``hushrun: <NAME>: <N> of <M> tests run, in <F> of <T> test files; <R> already passed on the files they read`` (`it read` for one), then `, and <A> more that did run as a check` for audited tests, an audited test file's counting as its tests; `hushrun: <NAME>: all <M> tests run; <A> that already passed on the files it read runs as a check` when only audits are; where it doesn't know them all, ``hushrun: <NAME>: <F> of <T> test files run; <R> already passed on the files they read, and so did <K> tests of the others``. Numbers past 999 have commas. In `observe`: ``hushrun: <NAME>: with `tests: reuse`, <N> of <M> tests would have run, in <F> of <T> test files; <R> already passed on the files they read``, with C-33's saving. The run's last line says `reusing <R> tests saved about <time>`, or `; <R> tests reused` under a second.
- **Limits.** 50,000 tests a check, counted from the store [ADR 0079] and the hints read: past them the plan, and `observe`, reuse by test file for that run and say ``hushrun: <NAME>: its tests are reused by test file in this run: it has <N> tests, more than the 50,000 this version reuses one by one``, and `hushrun.json` says `unit: file`. [ADR 0078] Decided once: a run whose plan listed tests counts by test whatever its adapter reported; one that reports more than 50,000 with none listed counts by test file. On every path what wasn't followed inside a test is its test file's too: the file records no pass of its own, and with its tests not kept one by one, its row keeps what wasn't followed. A test whose facts say `neverLeftOut` records no pass of its own, so no plan lists it.
- **Counts.** `units` (C-10) counts test files: one `ran` when it or any of its tests ran, `reused` when none of its tests ran and some were reused. `units.tests` counts tests the adapter told: `total`, `ran` (`ran and passed`, `ran and failed`, `not observed`) and `reused`. A test file reused whole whose adapter told none of its tests counts the tests this machine's store, else a hint, knows of it, each `reused`; when neither knows them, the run sends no `units.tests`, rather than one that leaves them out. `savedMs` adds the reused tests' recorded durations. Coverage of a run that reused a test isn't recorded (C-27).

**C-40 What a test read.** [ADR 0078] One function gives a test's list, for both values of `tests.reads`, and every reader calls it: the record, and through what the record kept (this machine's store, C-36, or a hint, C-41), the plan, `observe`, `hushrun ready` and `hushrun why`.

- **With `reads: file`**, or when its adapter reported less than what follows, a test read its test file's list (C-31, C-38).
- **With `reads: test`**, its **code** is its test file; the files whose functions were called while it ran (`ran`); the files every test of its file reads as code (`everyTest`: what ran at collection, module top level and class bodies); what its file loaded at collection (`loaded.ran`); and what each fixture it requested, directly or through another, ran while its runner kept that fixture's value (`shared`). Its **list** is that code; the files the code imports directly (`imports`) and opens (`opens`); the files it opened while it ran (`opened`), its file at collection (`loaded.opened`) and its fixtures; and the directories it listed (`dirs`), with every file now below them; [ADR 0078, amended] and for every file of those, the files it re-exports from (`reexports`), followed to the end through re-exports only, since a file that only re-exports (`export * from`) has no function of its own to call. pytest sends it from each repository module's source (`ast`, once a module): a package's `__init__.py` that binds names at its top level with `from X import …`, a module with `from X import *`, and a module whose `__all__` names what it bound with `from X import`; a plain module that takes a constant with `from X import` and is imported for it by a third re-exports nothing, and stays the first known miss below. A test's key digests its list (C-39).
- **Facts** (C-32). `hushrun.unit-facts/v1` carries it, fields older adapters needn't send: each test's `ran`, `opened` and `dirs`, indices into the file's `paths`; each unit's `loaded` (`ran`, `opened`), `everyTest`, and `shared` (`tests`, the tests it served, by their place in the unit's `tests`, and what the setup `ran` and `opened`); the file's `paths`, `imports` and `opens`, each `{"<index>": [<index>, …]}`, [ADR 0078, amended] and `reexports`, shaped like `imports`, the files each file re-exports from; and `settings.watch`, why it watched no test. A unit any of whose `loaded`, `everyTest` or `shared` doesn't read, or a test of which any of `ran`, `opened` and `dirs` is missing, reads its test file's list.
- **The store** keeps each test's own list as a set tests share (C-36): `list_ref` names it, one row per distinct list, pruned when no test names it.
- **Known misses** (RFC 0003 §3.3, at a test's grain): a value two imports away whose code never ran in the test (for Python, through a plain module that passes a constant on, which no re-export rule names), or a registry another module filled at import; [ADR 0078, amended] and a value an earlier test computed and code that doesn't run again kept (a `functools.cache` wrapper, which is C, a cached property), which a later test takes from the cache with none of the code that computed it running, so which test misses it depends on the order they run in. The audit's second run catches them, and suspends reuse.

**C-41 Hints with tests.** [ADR 0079] A hint (C-38) can hold a check's tests, by id, sealed, and with `tests.reads: test` each test's own list (C-40).

- **What it holds.** With `reads: test`, every test with a key (one that `ran and passed` or `ran and failed`) and its own list, or none when it read its test file's; with `reads: file`, ids alone, and only of test files that didn't pass whole. A test file reused in part is part of the hint, as one that ran is.
- **The content** is `hushrun.unit-hints/v1` and a line feed, then version 0's after its first line (C-38); then the count of tests' lists, and each list as the count and indices of the files it read and of the directories it listed, strictly ascending, the lists strictly ascending and each named by a test; then the count of sealed bytes and the bytes: a 24-byte nonce, then the ciphertext and its 16-byte tag. A hint with no tests is version 0.
- **The seal.** XChaCha20-Poly1305 under the **seal key**, HMAC-SHA-256 with the ASCII bytes `hushrun.unit-hints-seal/v1` as its key of the bytes `shape` hashes, never sent; its associated data, the content before the count of sealed bytes; its nonce, the first 24 bytes of HMAC-SHA-256 under the seal key of `hushrun.unit-hints-nonce/v1`, a line feed, the associated data and the plaintext. The plaintext: the count of units with tests, and for each, ascending by position, its position among the content's units, the count of its tests (1 or more), and each test, strictly ascending by its UTF-8 bytes, as the count and bytes of its id (1 to 4,096) and the index of its list, or `0xFFFFFFFF` for its test file's. 50,000 tests at most.
- **Reading.** A seal that doesn't open, a plaintext that isn't the one canonical plaintext, or a tests' part that doesn't read (lists out of order or past a bound, a seal shorter than a nonce and a tag, bytes after it, none at all), reads as no tests; the units stand, and a token only the tests' lists named is then named by no index. A test whose list names a token that matches nothing here is left out. Version 0 reads as before.
- **Too large.** A writer leaves out tests' lists, then tests, and says once ``hushrun: <NAME>: what each of its tests read wasn't shared with other machines: it is too large, so they take each test to have read what its test file read``, or ``hushrun: <NAME>: its tests weren't shared with other machines: there are too many, so they reuse them by test file``.

`testdata/digest/unit-hints.json` holds vectors of version 1, the seal key, and bytes whose tests don't read (`noTests`: a seal flipped, another key's, a plaintext not canonical, a seal too short, lists out of order, no tests' part); `tools/refdigest.py unit-hints` recomputes them, with XChaCha20-Poly1305 checked on its draft's example.

**C-42 pytest watches a test.** [ADR 0080] With `tests.unit: test` and `tests.reads: test`, on Python 3.12 or later, the pytest adapter reports what each test touched (C-40): through `sys.monitoring`'s `PY_START`, the files of the functions called between its setup and teardown (module top level and class bodies, which run at import, are `everyTest`); through the audit hook, the files it opened and the directories it listed; from the module graph, each module's direct imports and the files its own code opened; and what each fixture's setup ran and opened, for every test that requested it while pytest kept its value, [ADR 0080, amended] or was served it, by name or asked for at run time (`request.getfixturevalue`), and for the test inside whose window its setup started; and what ran, or was opened, while no test's window, shared setup or test file's collection was open (a `pytest_configure`, `pytest_sessionstart`, `pytest_collection_modifyitems` or `pytest_collection_finish` of a `conftest.py` or a plugin of the repository) is read by every test of the process. Below 3.12, its facts' `settings.watch` says `Python <X.Y> has no sys.monitoring, which watching a test needs (3.12 or later)`, and each test reads its test file's list. [ADR 0080, amended] It watches only while no other tool holds a `sys.monitoring` tool id (a debugger, coverage.py's sysmon core, a profiler, the repository's own), checked as it starts and at each window's start and end: with one there as it starts, it watches nothing and `settings.watch` says `<tool> uses sys.monitoring, which watching a test would disturb`; when one appears, or asks for a tool id, it stops watching for the rest of the process, releases its tool id, reports no test of the process as watched, and says `<tool> started using sys.monitoring during the run, which watching a test would disturb`. Each xdist worker watches and reports the tests it runs.

**C-43 Vitest's tests.** [ADR 0081] With `tests.unit: test` and the Vitest adapter (C-35), the setup file, in each worker, tells each test of its test file, from what `vitest-tests` says; without that file it tells none, and the file is ADR 0071's.

- **A test's id** is its suites' names and its own, as Vitest formats them, joined with ` > `; the k-th test of the file whose joined name the file already had, in the order the file declares them, is `<name> [k]`. Tests whose ids collide (the same id from different suites, or a suffixed id still another's) are told as one entry per id, failed if any of them failed, finished only if all did, with no `ran`, `opened` or `dirs`, and `neverLeftOut` (C-39). Each case of `test.each` and `describe.each` is a test.
- **Leaving out.** When the runner starts the file's suite, after collection and Vitest's filters and before any hook of the file, each test `reuseTests` lists for the file whose mode is `run` gets mode `skip` and a skipped result, and each suite all of whose tests are then skipped gets mode `skip`, so that its `beforeAll` and `afterAll` don't run. A test the file skips, a todo, a test a filter left out, and one whose id another shares are never left out. Vitest exits as it would: a pass stays a pass when tests, a file's tests, or every test are left out.
- **How each ended**, when the run's files end, after every hook: `finished` when its window closed (the runner's end of the test, after its hooks, fixtures and retries; for a test that skipped itself, the next boundary) with a result; `failed` when Vitest failed it, or when, after it finished, a suite above it failed in a hook of its own (an `afterAll`, a cleanup, a snapshot check); `deselected` when the setup file left it out and it didn't run. A test the file skips, or a todo, finished. A test a filter (`.only`, `-t`, a line, a tag) left out isn't told; nor are the tests of a file that can't be collected. [ADR 0081, amended] A test the file skips (`test.skip`, `describe.skip`, `test.todo`, `test.skipIf`) or that skipped itself (`ctx.skip()`) is `skipped`, and the main process's file says each test file's count of skipped tests (`skipped`). The plugin's file and each worker's say `start`, one id for the start of Vitest that claimed the run (`HUSHRUN_VITEST_START`); when Vitest says its run ended more than once, the main process's file holds every end.
- **Not followed.** A child process, a worker thread or a native addon inside a test's window is the test's; inside a suite's hooks, each test's of the suite; while the file loaded, after its suite, or in a file not watched alone, the unit's.
- **Watching** (`watch`): V8's precise coverage through `node:inspector` in the worker, taken at the file's start, each suite's start and end, and each test's end. A test's `ran` is each file a function of which was called in its window, but the script's own function and Vitest's module wrapper; `opened` and `dirs` what `fs` opened, looked at and listed in it, with the listings of the suites that serve it and of loading. `shared` holds what each suite's hooks ran and opened, with the tests under it; `loaded` what ran and was opened while the file was imported and collected; `everyTest` the config files and the project's setup files; `imports` each module's direct imports as the worker's module runner evaluated them; `opens` each file opened, by every module of the repository on the stack of its open (C-40); [ADR 0081, amended] `reexports` the files each module re-exports from (`export * from`, `export { x } from`), as the plugin read each module Vite made outside `node_modules` and resolved it through Vite. The unit is named by its path from the root. With `reads: test` and coverage on, the main process's `settings.watch` says ``Vitest's coverage is on, and it shares V8's counters with watching a test``. Paths outside the root and below `node_modules` are left out. A file with a concurrent test, or where two windows, or a suite and a window, were open at once, sends no test's `ran`, `opened` or `dirs`.
- **Coverage on** leaves out no test and watches none. A file that two projects run is merged so that a test finished only where every worker that told it finished it, and what it read is known only where every worker said it.

**C-44 Jest.** [ADR 0082] The Jest adapter is observed, knows tests (C-39), and keeps COMMAND byte for byte as written. Its word is `jest`.

- **Which COMMANDs.** One run of Jest 29 or 30, started as `jest` or a path to it, `npx [--no-install] jest`, `pnpm [exec] jest`, `yarn [exec] jest`, `bunx jest`, `bun x jest`, `npm exec [--] jest`, or `node [options] <…>/jest/bin/jest.js`. Not offered, said once: `--watch`, `--watchAll`, `--onlyFailures`, `--onlyChanged`, `--changedSince`, `--lastCommit`, `--listTests`, `--showConfig`, `--clearCache`, `--init`, `--version`, `--help`, a `--testRunner` other than jest-circus, Jest under Bun's own runtime (`--bun`), and a Jest installed where COMMAND runs (`node_modules/jest/package.json`, from its directory up) whose major isn't 29 or 30. `npm test` hides which program runs: the default adapter is chosen.
- **Loading.** hushrun writes its file and a reporter to `<cache dir>/adapters/jest-<hash>/` and sets `NODE_OPTIONS` to what COMMAND's environment had, then ` --require <file>` (in double quotes, backslashes doubled, when the path has whitespace; a path with a quote isn't offered), and `HUSHRUN_JEST` to the file. In a Node process with no `hushrun.json` in `HUSHRUN_UNITS`, it does nothing; in one where no Jest module loads, it writes nothing. A script that sets `NODE_OPTIONS` without what it had drops it: Jest then writes nothing, and the run says `hushrun: <NAME>: test files weren't observed: Jest wrote nothing for hushrun: …` with both fixes, keeping what `NODE_OPTIONS` had or the line `process.env.HUSHRUN_JEST && require(process.env.HUSHRUN_JEST);` first in Jest's config (`import { createRequire } from 'node:module'; if (process.env.HUSHRUN_JEST) createRequire(import.meta.url)(process.env.HUSHRUN_JEST);` for an ES module config). `hushrun init` proposes that line (C-15) where a `package.json` script that runs Jest does so, and writes none.
- **The run's directory.** The first Jest process whose `readConfigs` returns claims it (`jest-claim`); it alone adds the reporter, chooses test files and writes `jest-main-<pid>.json`, and tells the workers it starts, through its environment, whether they may leave tests out. A Jest started inside a test file writes nothing. Everything a start does comes from the `HUSHRUN_UNITS` it starts with.
- **The unit rule** is Vitest's (C-30): the test files `SearchSource#isTestFilePath` names in every project, as the run that wrote the map listed them; a file no run listed is a source. **Sources** are `.js`, `.mjs`, `.cjs`, `.jsx`, `.ts`, `.mts`, `.cts`, `.tsx` and `.snap`.
- **Reads.** A test file read every module Jest compiled while it ran (through Node's `vm`), every file it opened, read or looked at through `fs` and `fs/promises`, every file below a directory it listed, what its process read since the test file before it ended, every repository file Node itself compiled in its process (a config, an environment, a resolver, a transformer of the repository's), what the main process compiled and what its globalSetup read, and every file named `jest.config.*`, `babel.config.*`, `.babelrc.*`, `jest-preset.*` or `jest.preset.*` of a code kind. Where ts-jest checked types (its language service), every file of a code kind.
- **Selection** (C-32). Whole test files in the test sequencer's `sort`, after Jest's filters and `--shard`, before any worker starts, only when Jest's own list of test files is `hushrun.json`'s `testFiles` and coverage is off; `passWithNoTests` is true for the run only when that left none of a list that had some, so a filter that matched nothing stays Jest's failure. Tests at jest-circus's `run_start`, before any of their hooks, under the same two conditions: each that `reuseTests` lists gets `mode = 'skip'`, but an ambiguous one.
- **A test's id** is its suites' names and its own, from the outermost, the root excluded, joined with ` > `, as jest-circus collected them before `--randomize`; the k-th test (k from 2) of a file with an already-seen joined name gets ` [k]`, in declaration order. Tests whose ids collide (a joined name that two nestings make, or a suffixed id another test's equals) are reported as one entry per id, failed if any of them failed, finished only if all finished, with no list of their own and `neverLeftOut: true` (C-40): no pass of their own is recorded, so no plan lists them.
- **A test's facts** come from jest-circus's events: finished when it ended (`test_done`), or was skipped or marked todo by its own code; not finished when a focus or `--testNamePattern` skipped it, or it never ended; failed when it ended with errors; deselected when the adapter left it out. [ADR 0082, amended] Read when its file ends: failed when it has errors then (Jest 30 attaches a late error to the test that raised it), or when an `afterAll` of a block above it failed (a root-level one's being every test's); not finished, every test of the file, after an uncaught exception, an unhandled rejection not handled later, or an unhandled error of the file's that isn't an `afterAll`'s; `skipped` when its own code skipped it or marked it todo, and the file's facts count those (`skipped`). Each file says `start`, one id for the start of Jest whose main process claimed the run (`HUSHRUN_JEST_START`); a test file two projects of one start ran ran whole only when each process that ran it finished it. A failing `beforeAll` or `afterAll`, an error outside any test, a test file that fails to load, or one whose snapshots Jest calls obsolete while not updating them, fails the test file. What wasn't followed inside a test's window (from `test_start` to its end, its `beforeEach` and `afterEach` included) is that test's; outside, every test's of the file; in a file with a `test.concurrent`, every test's.
- **Watching a test** (`reads: test`, C-40), with V8's precise coverage in each process, taken at jest-circus's boundaries: `ran`, the files a function of which was called in its window, and the modules first compiled in it; `opened`, the files it opened or looked at, but a module Jest read to compile it; `dirs`, the directories it listed, and those listed outside every window (`opens` and `loaded` name files, not directories). `loaded`: what collecting the file called and opened. `everyTest`: the modules compiled before the test file (setup files), the repository files Node compiled for its process, and Jest's config files as the main process found them. `shared`: what each `beforeAll` and `afterAll` ran and opened, for the tests under its block that ended. `imports`: each file's direct imports, as Jest's runtime resolves them, manual mocks included. `opens`: [ADR 0082, amended] what was opened, by every repository file on the stack of the open, the test file included. `reexports`: each import whose specifier the importer's own text re-exports (`export * from`, `export { x } from`, `module.exports = require(…)`, `__exportStar(require(…))`, `...require(…)`, `Object.assign(exports, require(…))`), or whose module's own objects or functions reappear in the importer's exports, getters uncalled. None of these is sent for a concurrent test, in a process where ts-jest checked types, where Jest's own V8 coverage is on, or where the runtime can't say what a name resolves to (Jest 30.0 to 30.4); `settings.watch` then says why, in those words: ``ts-jest checks types with a language service, which reads every file of code for every test file``, ``Jest's own V8 coverage is on, and it takes the counters watching a test needs``, ``this Jest's runtime doesn't say which file a module name resolves to, which watching a test needs (Jest 29, or 30.5 and later)``, ``test.concurrent tests run at once, so each of them takes its test file's list``.
- **`not observed`**: a child process, a worker thread, a native addon a module of the repository loaded; no jest-circus event (an environment hushrun didn't wrap); a test file Jest ran and no worker described (`unseen`); a runner other than jest-circus (every test file); a process or worker the globalSetup started (every test file); an error of the adapter's.
- **Coverage** (`--coverage`, or the config): everything runs and is observed, and nothing is left out: ``hushrun: <NAME>: every test file runs: Jest's coverage is on, and a partial run's coverage would read as a drop``, or from the config, ``no test file was reused: …``.

**C-45 Go's tests.** [ADR 0083] With `tests.unit: test` (C-39), the Go adapter (C-35) knows tests. A **test** is a top-level `Test…`, `Example…` or `Fuzz…` function of a package, by its name, its unit the package (`cart::TestTotal`); a subtest belongs to its top-level test, a fuzz target's seeds to it; a benchmark isn't a test. What a test read is its package's (`reads: test` runs as `reads: file`, said once).

- **Listing.** hushrun's runner starts each test binary first with `-test.list=. -test.skip=^$`, in the run's environment, standard input closed, standard error discarded, `GOCOVERDIR` the run's directory, for at most 60 seconds, and takes from what it printed the names that begin `Test`, `Example` or `Fuzz`. It tells none of the binary's tests, and the package runs as a package, when the binary has a `TestMain` (its function names hold `<package>.TestMain`), the listing fails, takes longer or prints a line that isn't a Go name, or its arguments hold `-test.run`, `-test.skip`, `-test.list`, `-test.fuzz`, `-test.memprofile` or a `-test.count` that isn't a positive number. A package the plan reuses whole isn't started: its tests are those this machine's store has for it, each `deselected`.
- **Selection.** The tests `reuseTests` lists for the package that the binary listed are left out with `-test.skip=^(<name>|…)$`, each name quoted as Go's `regexp.QuoteMeta` does, before COMMAND's arguments; none past 64 KiB of pattern. A binary that refuses `-test.skip=^$` but lists without it (a Go before 1.20) leaves none out, and the runner says once a run, on the binary's standard error, `hushrun: go test's tests are reused by package: this Go can't leave out one test (Go 1.20 and later can)`. A package one of whose tests was left out didn't run whole.
- **Outcomes.** The runner adds `-test.memprofile=<a file of the run's directory>`, passes the binary's output on byte for byte through a pipe of its own (standard output and error as one, when its own are one file), and reads it. A test is `failed` when any `--- FAIL: ` names it, anywhere in a line, by its top-level name (up to the first character no Go name has). It `finished` only when the run ended normally and hushrun didn't leave it out: the memory profile was written (Go's test main returned from running the tests); the output was read to its end once the binary exited, every line under 1 MiB ([ADR 0083, amended] output still open after it, quiet for 250 ms or at most 1 s, is handed to a forwarder hushrun starts and doesn't wait for, which copies it to `go test` to its end, and the runner exits with the binary's code: `go test` then sees what it sees without hushrun, the binary gone and its output open, and its own wait decides, printing `Test I/O incomplete` and failing the package past it); its last line but `coverage: …` ends with Go's summary, which with `-test.v=test2json` follows a `\x16`; and the summary is `PASS` with exit 0 and `-test.paniconexit0`, or `FAIL` with exit 1, a `--- FAIL: ` seen, every name one gave listed by the binary, and no `-test.failfast`. Otherwise no test of the start finished. Each test the binary listed is told, `deselected` when hushrun left it out. Its `durationMs` is Go's own `--- PASS`, `--- FAIL` or `--- SKIP` line's time, summed, else 0. [ADR 0083, amended] It is `skipped` when a `--- SKIP: ` line names it or one of its subtests (with `-v` or `-json`; without them a skip isn't told). [ADR 0083, amended] A binary that ended normally on `FAIL` without `-test.v` (true or `test2json`) finishes none of its tests but those a `--- FAIL: ` named: a pass can't be told from a skip there. Its facts' `settings.note` then says ``a package that failed keeps its other tests' results only when go test names each one: add -v to keep them``, which the run says once as `hushrun: <NAME>: <note>` (any adapter's `settings.note`, each distinct one once), and each such test's `notKept` says too. [C-37, amended] A test of a start that didn't end normally, which no `--- FAIL: ` named, is told `notKept` with why: the binary ended before Go's test main returned (a panic, a timeout, `os.Exit` or a signal), it failed under `-test.failfast`, or its output couldn't be read to Go's summary. `hushrun why` gives a test's reason from its row, never from COMMAND's flags, which `GOFLAGS` may add to. Each start's file says `start`, `go-test-<pid>` of the `go` process that started the runner.
- **COMMAND's own selection.** A COMMAND with `-run` or `-skip`, or `-test.run` or `-test.skip` after `-args`, reuses by package and says once ``hushrun: <NAME>: its tests are reused by package: go test -run's tests can't be reused one by one yet`` (`-skip's` for `-skip`).

**C-46 Gradle.** [ADR 0084] The Gradle adapter is observed, knows tests (C-39), and keeps COMMAND byte for byte as written. For `gradle`, `gradlew`, `gradle.bat` or `gradlew.bat`, by name or path, with any tasks, hushrun writes its Groovy script to `<cache dir>/adapters/gradle-<hash>/hushrun.gradle` and names it in `HUSHRUN_GRADLE`, and the test-JVM hook's jar in `HUSHRUN_GRADLE_HOOK`; the build's settings file applies the script with one line `hushrun init` proposes and never writes (C-15): `if (System.getenv("HUSHRUN_GRADLE")) apply from: System.getenv("HUSHRUN_GRADLE")` in `settings.gradle`, after any `pluginManagement {}` and `plugins {}` block, or `System.getenv("HUSHRUN_GRADLE")?.let { apply(from = it) }` in `settings.gradle.kts`. A settings file Gradle would read without `HUSHRUN_GRADLE` in it, or none, loads nothing: ``hushrun: <NAME>: test files aren't observed: Gradle loads hushrun's adapter from one line in <file>, which `hushrun init` proposes: `<line>` ``. Not offered, said once: `--continuous`. A COMMAND that hides Gradle (`make test`, a script) gets the default adapter, which does nothing for it.

- **Units and tests.** A unit is a test class, a nested class its outer class's, named by its source file: the file its outer class's `SourceFile` attribute names, when exactly one file of the project's test source directories is at its package's path, else when exactly one such file declares its package; else `<project directory>::<class>`, a unit of no file. Two classes of one file are one unit. A test's id is `<class as Gradle names it>.<method>`, with `(args)`, `[n]`, and `[n]` followed by `(x)`, removed, so every invocation of a method (parametrized, repeated, a factory's, a Spock feature's rows) is one test, and a nested class's method is `pkg.Outer$Inner.method`.

A unit `reuse` lists is left out whole by an exclusion Gradle evaluates while it looks at the task's class files; a test `reuseTests` lists, through the hook for the JUnit Platform and TestNG (entry `pkg.Outer$Inner#method`), and through Gradle's filter patterns `pkg.Outer$Inner.method` and `pkg.Outer$Inner.method[*]` for JUnit 4, up to 250 tests a task, past which they run; [ADR 0084, amended] a class of the default package, or whose name starts upper-case, gets no pattern (Gradle reads one that starts upper-case as a simple class name, of every package): its tests run. [ADR 0084, amended] The hook matches a method's whole name, and tells each test it leaves out with the entry that listed it; a test it left out that no entry names exactly, or an entry it neither left out nor saw run in a class that ran, makes the run start COMMAND once more with nothing left out (C-32). Kotest's tests aren't left out one by one: they run, [ADR 0084, amended] and are told never left out (the hook saw them with no method), so they record no pass of their own and no plan counts one as reused. What a task leaves out is one of its inputs; the hook's list reaches its test JVM through a system property computed when the task starts, from `HUSHRUN_UNITS`, as every file of the run's is. A task left with no test by hushrun doesn't fail for that. The task's own `--tests` and includes apply with the plan's.
- **Outcomes**, from Gradle's own test events: a test failed when an invocation failed (a retry that passed included), finished when its task's root suite ended; [ADR 0084, amended] a skipped test, and its class, is told skipped, and a skipped test finishes only when nothing of the run failed (a task, a test, a suite), else its class didn't run whole either. [ADR 0084, amended] A task that failed with no failed test or suite, whose failure's type says its test process ended badly (`TestSuiteExecutionException`, `ProcessExecutionException`, `ExecException`; its message's words only where no type is named), or whose hook events can't be read ran nothing whole; [ADR 0084, amended] the hook also goes on each test JVM as a Java agent that writes that the JVM began and how it ended: a JVM that began and never ended ran nothing of its task whole, one a test ended with `System.exit` leaves the classes below that call (else the task) not run whole, whatever Gradle says of the task; a class the hook's events of the task started and never ended didn't run whole, and none of its tests finished but those that failed. Each start of Gradle keeps its tasks' facts apart, with `start`: a test failed when any start failed it, and finished, and its class ran whole, only when every start and task that ran it did. `initializationError`, `classMethod`, `executionError` and a suite that failed with no failed test fail their class. With Isolated Projects on, the script says so and observes nothing.

[ADR 0087, amended] One known to be older than what it is built from (`ninja -n` has work, or on Makefiles a file it read is newer), whose test ran in a start of `ctest`, makes that start's facts say `staleBuild`, ``<program> is older than what it is built from (<what changed>): build before ctest``, so a pass of the run records nothing, the check's included (C-32); one whose being up to date can't be told (a record that isn't `VALID` with no work for `ninja -n`, `ninja` not answering, a directory of a build named by a test) is only `not observed`.
- **Outcomes.** Read from the JUnit file `ctest --output-junit` writes: COMMAND's own or its test preset's (`output.outputJUnitFile`, from the test directory) when it names one, which must have changed in this run and be no older than it by more than two seconds; else hushrun's own, in the run's directory. Nothing is read of a file that is missing, doesn't parse, or holds another number of `<testcase>` than its `<testsuite>`'s `tests`, nor when `ctest` ended by a signal: no test then finished. `status="run"` passed; `fail` failed; `notrun` whose message starts `SKIP_RETURN_CODE` or `SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION` was skipped, which passed; any other `notrun` and `disabled` didn't finish. [ADR 0087, amended] A skipped or disabled test is told as skipped (C-32): in a run that failed it records no pass. Cases match the tests that ran by name, in order: a name with no case didn't finish; a name with as many cases as tests matches one to one; any other count didn't finish, and failed if any of its cases did. Under `--repeat until-pass` or `after-timeout`, [ADR 0087, amended] or with no `--repeat` a test preset's `execution.repeat` of those modes, a test that passed is `not observed` (`repeat until-pass`): its pass may follow a failure. A unit's selected tests are those not left out and not `DISABLED`.
- **Selection.** For a test run hushrun may select in (`--rerun-failed` and `-I`, [ADR 0087, amended] and a test preset's `filter.include.index`, pick tests by position: their runs are only observed), the tests left out are those whose unit `reuse` lists or whose id `reuseTests` lists, when hushrun's `ctest` checks each such unit's programs again: every one built in a CMake build directory, up to date, not modified after the plan's file was written, and the files of a source kind of the scope it reads now exactly those of every list the plan keyed its unit by, which the plan writes to `ctest-plan.v1` in the run's directory (for an audit's second run, the first run's); kept although listed, a test whose name holds a line break, a fixture's setup or cleanup (`FIXTURES_SETUP`, `FIXTURES_CLEANUP`) that a kept test requires, every test with the name of a kept test, and every test of a unit `reuse` lists when one of its tests is kept. They are left out by the first that fits of: `--exclude-from-file <file>` with COMMAND's last such file's lines and the names (CTest 3.29 and later, and not with a preset that sets `filter.exclude.fromFile`); one `-E`, COMMAND's last `-E` or else the test preset's `filter.exclude.name`, `|`, and each name as `^<name>$` with `^ $ . [ ] | ( ) * + ? \` escaped by a backslash, joined by `|` with no group, while about each name's escaped length and 7 bytes, and the user's expression twice, stay within 60,000 bytes and the argument within 100,000; `-R` with the names kept, likewise. Each replaces COMMAND's options of its kind. With every test left out, `--no-tests=ignore` is added. Before it runs, the real `ctest` lists again with the final arguments, and the tests listed, by name and command, must be exactly those expected; otherwise the next way is tried, and with none COMMAND's `ctest` runs unchanged.
- **The plan.** For `ctest …` itself, the plan lists and walks before COMMAND, [ADR 0087, amended] and keeps its listing in the run's directory, which hushrun's `ctest` takes for the same `ctest`, words and directory, and keys each unit by what its build says it is built from now, with the map's list and the hints' (C-38); for any other COMMAND, by the map's and the hints'. It writes each unit's lists to `ctest-plan.v1`. `hushrun ready` (C-22) plans from the map and hints alone. Units aren't bound to a built file's bytes: the check above stands in for it.
- **Facts.** One file per start of hushrun's `ctest`, [ADR 0087, amended] each its own `start` (C-32), `hushrun.unit-facts/v1` with each unit's tests when the run's `unit` is `test`, else `v0`; failures by unit. Its `settings` hold `own` (each unit's own files), `everySource` (the units whose build couldn't be read) and `selection` (`--exclude-from-file`, `-E`, `-R`, or why nothing was left out), or `notObserved` with why nothing was.
